## Page 9

The Invocation to the Illuminator
[1]
First Edition
O Mother! On the day you emerged from the mouth of the great sage Shri Chandika in the form of an explanation of the Devimahatmya, on the day you, hearing the unique and mysterious essence of Sadhana, became Mantra-mugdha with joy and wonder, on that day you, in the form of Vasana, rose from the Prana. "From drinking that Amrita, our world, scorched by desire, becomes like a dry, arid land, but the Prana also rises daily, becoming sweet and juicy. Let every man and woman drink that Amrita and be freed from the world's suffering. Then, from the deep, dark veil of the cunning mystery, let the pure, smooth, sweet truth be illuminated by the clear, smooth, sweet light of the truth." Today, these two Vasanas are advancing towards success due to your majestic grace. Seeing this, our long-ignorant heart also declares a crore of prostrations to your lotus feet and is grateful. About 12 years ago, the first edition of this book was published in Bengali. This book had such an effect on the hearts of Bengali sadhus that their life became a new stream. Gradually, its light fell on people in other regions as well, and their strong desire was to taste its essence in Hindi. The result of this united desire of those people is that the respected scholar Shri Shivnarayan Sen Gupta, residing in Post Firozabad, Agara district, a former teacher at the Rishikul Brahmacarya Ashram, Haridwar, has published this book. CCO. Vasishtha Tripathi Collection. Digitized By Siddhanta eGangotri Gyaan Kosha

## Page 10

(6)
Those who have made immense efforts have translated the first part of this chapter into Hindi. Although this translation was completed in the year 1627 CE, for some reason, it could not be published in front of the readers.
Satyasarup Guru, who revealed these immortal mantras in this period of dharma's decline, did not reveal his special name even at the request of the sadhakas. However, it is ordered to be said that his incarnation took place in the village of Thakur Vadi in Navagram, Varisal district - that is where we, the people, are known as Guru Grah.
This book is an exegesis of Sri Sri Chandi (Devi Mahatmya). The Lord has said in the Gita, "Mamekam sharanam braj, arh tvaam mokshayishyami" - He himself, taking the form of a handless one, rescues each jiva. The details of this game of war are found here.
Mahatma Ji says that reading and teaching the scriptures, and giving other instructions is not difficult, but putting those instructions into practice in one's own life is difficult. But the Guru's assurance is that the Lord, moved by love, rescues his children.

## Page 12

Brahmananda, the supreme source of bliss, is the embodiment of knowledge. Beyond duality, it is like the sky, the ultimate reality to be realized. It is eternal, pure, immutable, and the witness of all wisdom. Beyond emotion, it is free from the three gunas and is my Guru. I bow to You, Guru! You, who embody Narayana in many forms, are the one who has made this arrangement for Your service. Be satisfied with Your own service. By renouncing the play of ignorance and the lack of bliss, stand once as Your wisdom-filled, bliss-filled embodiment, from which service will be successful. May the servant be blessed!

## Page 15

On the 15th of the bright half of the month of Chaturdashi, on a Saturday, at dawn, the first girl is recognized. According to this, as per the commentary 8-6-1068, the invocation is:
* Mother's Love *
"Let the sons of immortality hear."
O sons of immortality, dear to my heart! Why are you still suffering from the deep pit of sorrow, pain, and false ignorance? Again and again, you are being destroyed by the blows of birth and death, by the suffering of disease, grief, and fever, by the fierce whirlwind of instability, and by the burning thirst of hatred, leaving you lifeless, broken, and breathless! Come, run, my son! My child! Look, I have opened my vast bosom for you. Stretching my infinite arms, I run behind you, following you. Call me, my dear child, from your beloved throat, and hearing my sweet maternal call, I will forget myself, and I will forget you too. I will make you immortal by drinking the nectar of your burning milk in my heart. You will become immortal! That is why I am calling out with my free throat:
Come, my child! Come, my son! Open your eyes once. Now, close your eyes and remain as a statue for how long! Look, even for a moment, I have not taken you out of my lap. You were born from my womb, nurtured in my lap, and filled with my milk, you are advancing. There is no sorrow or burning, no birth or death.

---

## Page 16

Awakening
There is nothing, no despair or pain. What you see that makes you afraid and agitated is all my love milk. Listen! The victory of truth has risen in a roar, the light of the truth world has illuminated the horizon, the sky is being filled with the sweet embrace of the mother, the earth has abandoned its rigidity with the embrace of the truth, the ocean is being stirred by the sound of truth, the air is being agitated by the clash of the truth's sound, the space is being filled with the sacred sound of the truth's syllable; are you still sleeping? Are you still being agitated by the pain of the sorrow of the false word stuck in your mouth? Come, and stand up once, turn your face, I have been sitting and watching your faces for many, many ages. Come and sit in the mother's lap, become a child of devotion, and be initiated into the immortal stream of life. Be enlightened in the pure water of joy and peace. Become immortal by experiencing the eternal state and the state of Brahma in the mother's lap. May the auspicious blessings of the revered Guru shower on your heads. You are blessed.

## Page 17

Devi Sukta
Who am I?
Ambhrun, the sage, became a brahman-versed girl, a maiden, due to which she was also a sage. Along with the Paramatma, who is of the nature of Satchidananda, she realized the identity, that is, complete unbrokenness, and what she manifested as her own self is known as the Devi Sukta. This contains eight mantras. This Devi Sukta is the primary source of Chandi. Chandi or Devi Mahatmya is merely its analysis. Devi Sukta - this is the insight of a sage who is free from desire and error; therefore, it is impersonal. Whatever knowledge and emotion is expressed in Chandi, even if it is uttered from the mouth of a sage, it remains eternal and impersonal. In all ages, in the hearts of all advanced sadhakas and great souls, it is the same knowledge and the same emotion that is expressed. Only due to the difference in time and place and language, the expression of impersonal knowledge and emotion is highlighted in different languages. The subject of Devi Sukta is Satchidananda, the form of Paramatma. In Devi Mahatmya, this Paramatma is described as the embodiment of Mahamaya. Paramatma and Mahamaya are one. From a scholarly perspective or through oral discussion, Maya is described as the embodiment of Mahamaya.

---

## Page 18

2
* Devi Sukta
is said to be separate from the Atma; but those who are Sadhakas, who are Brahmavid, who are Atmajn Purushas, they know that Atma and Maya are entirely identical. As long as Sadhana exists, as long as the body exists, Atma appears in the form of Maya. When Paramatma, then there is no Sadhya, no Sadhana, no Sadhaka, no Shastras, no Chinta, and no Bhasha. From the moment Bhasha, Chinta, or Sadhana enters, Atma appears in the form of Maya. Therefore, Devi Sukta is also revealed as Mahamaya in Chandi. This subject will be discussed in detail in its proper place.
The main goal of all Dharmic Shastras is Paramatma Jnana. The Atma, despite its infinite diversity in caste, class, and lineage, exists identically in all beings. The name for knowing this truth is "Atmajnana." Only the Jeeva is inclined to know its own form. Until it understands this, it remains an ordinary Jeeva. When the Jeeva can directly experience this Atma-nusandhana, people start calling it a Sadhaka, a Bhakta, etc.
When a human experiences this Atma-bhimukhi Gati, the characteristics that manifest are called Nivritti Marg or Sadhana. These characteristics are described in Dharmic Shastras as Vidhi and Nishedha. In reality, Karma alone is Sadhana, Jeeva alone is the Sadhaka, and the experience of Atma-swarupa is the Sadhya. Sadhana of any kind without Atma-bhava is not fully fruitful. As long as worship of any deity other than "" is done, even if the object of worship is only "", it remains a ritualistic act (because there is nothing other than "I").

---

## Page 19

The Devi Sukta
3
is performed; therefore, it is incapable of providing the fruit of liberation. Therefore, the essence is that all forms of Sadhana are pervaded by ignorance. Then, even the most minor forms of Sadhana like food, etc., which are based on self-inquiry, are ineffective. This is because the self is "I am" - "I am She." Recognizing oneself - attaining Her - and realizing the self directly are all one and the same. In the Devi Sukta, the element that is revealed as "I" is described as Mahamaya in the Chandi. The self in the Devi Sukta is the same as "She" in the Chandi. Therefore, those who consider Shri Shri Chandi to be only a scripture of the Shakta tradition are mistaken. Such a view is highly erroneous. The true essence of the living being, whether through knowledge or ignorance, is something that requires substantial knowledge; otherwise, the path to achieving the desired goal becomes very long. Therefore, entering the Chandi element without knowing the Devi Sukta is considered unorthodox in the scriptures. We often see that most sadhakas in the world have mostly futile aspirations. The main reason for this is aimlessness. Obstacles in the path of Sadhana, without knowing the divine form, without attaining immortality, are nothing to marvel at. Let's go, sadhak! Let us seek refuge in the Devi Sukta to gain a slight understanding of the form of the Mother.

## Page 21

* Devi Sukta
Gains light. No one understands it as "I am home" in the same way, similarly, the realization "I am the body," "I am the mind" has never come to us. Just as I am sad when the house is broken, and happy when the house is clean, in the same way, the happiness and sorrow associated with "I" in conjunction with the body, prana, mind, etc., is special. The happiness and sorrow of the body, etc., only experiences happiness and sorrow. Why this matter will be said further. In reality, the body, etc., devoid of happiness and sorrow is an empty person.
In this way, we have found the true substance we were looking for. Now we are trying to understand its form. So far, we have advanced with the help of thought and intellect. Now the help of strength and wisdom will be needed. Because there is no way to know the true self without the grace of that knowledge.
Then what harm is there in trying to understand as much as can be held by our petty intellect?
Well, if we start trying to understand and say the "I" separate from the body, we will certainly start saying and understanding that it is inconceivable, invisible, and all-sensed, but "it is true." By thinking, it cannot grasp who "I" am, it cannot be said by words, it cannot be experienced by eyes, ears, etc., senses, but it can understand that it is a true substance. In some way, it cannot be perceived that "it is not." This "I" which is truth, we always experience it, but we cannot understand it. Well, let's give the name "truth" or "atma" to this "I."
It is said in the scriptures that the form of this atma is "bliss." By analyzing the substance of bliss, we see that truth is knowledge.

---

## Page 22

Devī Sukta
Ananda, meaning Sat, Chit, and Ananda. Sat is a sattva, meaning something exists. Chit is this sattva, which is chaitanya, meaning it is not just existence, but it is chinmaya, meaning it is jnanamaya, and this jnanamaya sattva is niravchinn anandamaya. And further, it is discussed in a simple way. I am, I understand that I am, and this is the most dear thing to me; therefore, it is anandamaya. This is my true self, Sachchidananda Atma. This is my truth. This truth is the ultimate goal of the jeeva. Because there, in that 'I', there is no birth, death, happiness, sorrow, laughter, crying, nothing at all, only full ananda. But worldly happiness and this ananda is not just one thing. In this world, happiness comes to us from obtaining the desired thing. Conversely, sorrow comes. But "I am a place where, whether one obtains or does not obtain the desired, the undesired, or anything at all, there is always constant ananda. What else can be done, there is no body, mind, prana, senses, dharma, adharma, happiness, sorrow, jeeva jagat, etc., any feeling at all. It is all feelings, vinirmukta Sachchidananda Atma, that is 'I', that is truth. Experiencing eternal union is the Brahmi state. The big thing is that always holding this 'I' thing is what is called human nature. The person who does not know what 'I' is, is a beast. This is the statement of the shastra-kara. This 'I' is the sadhak's ishtadev. Kali, Krishna, Shiva, Durga, Allah, Gaud, etc., are only different forms of this. The more a sadhak is near their ishtadev, the more they are elevated and the more they are happy, because happiness or ananda is their very form. This principle will be discussed in detail at various places. Through repeated discussion, the self-principle will be understood well, and then the Chandi principle will be discussed.

## Page 23

Devi Sukta
Entry will be made. The daughter of Ambhrun Rishi, Vak, when I positioned myself in this 'I', what she said is the Devi Sukta. She said, "I move with Rudras and Vasus. I manifest in the form of ten Rudras and eight Vasus. The eleven Rudras are 'Rudayahi samantakale iti Rudra' from the Ved. Sage Sayanacharya said that the one who brings destruction at the end of time is Rudra. The five Jnana Indriya (eyes, ears, etc.), the five Karmendriya (speech, hands, feet, etc.), and the mind are these eleven Rudras. These are the causes of birth and death of the living being; therefore, they are the destroyers. We always experience fragmented consciousness through these sensory paths. I am the Atma in the form of Sachchidananda; no one else is. Through these sensory paths, I manifest as Shudra Shudra knowledge. 'I am who I am; we always know this through the senses and the mind. When the senses and the mind sleep, we cannot experience the consciousness of the Atma; therefore, whatever we perceive through the senses and think in the mind is all the Satya form of the Atma. O Sadhak! Keep this word of the Ved deeply in your heart. Do not forget it when entering the Chandi Tattva. Eight Vasu wealth - the eight treasures of wealth. These manifest in eight forms of wealth like Anima, Mahima, etc. Or in Bhagavat, the word Vasu is attributed to pure Satva guna. From the rise of pure Satya guna, eight external signs like the trembling of the body, sweating, etc., appear on the Sadhak. These are the wealth of the devotees. Even from this eight Vasu form, 'I' - the Satya form of the Atma - is manifested. I am the ruler of the world in the form of twelve Adityas and the group of world gods. Aditya is born from Aditi."

## Page 24

8
* Devi Sukta
Aditi - Prakriti is trigunatmika, sattvamayi, rajamayi, and tamomayi. Buddhi, ahankara, citta, and man, these four antahkarana are produced from Prakriti. Among these four antahkarana, there are twelve differences according to the deficiency of the combination of the three gunas. For example - sattvagunatmika buddhi, rajogunatmika buddhi, and tamogunatmika buddhi. Similarly, man, citta, and ahankara become twelve differences from being guna. These are known as the twelve Adityas. Man, buddhi, citta, ahankara, and sattva, raja, and tamoguna are all forms of the same true self that is revealed.
There is no fault in calling man Rudra and then Aditya. The part of man that is associated with knowledge and is the center of kamendriya and the center of the senses is Rudra, the cause of sorrow. And the part that is associated with buddhi and the highest value is Aditya, that is, in that part of man, the light of chaitanya is more prominent.
Vishwadeva - who shines as chaitanya in this cosmic form, he is Vishwadeva. He who, despite having many names, forms, and behaviors, reveals the light of chaitanya, is called Vishwadeva. He who, despite having many specific names, forms, and behaviors, gains the light of chaitanya, is called Vishwadeva. Differences in names, forms, and behaviors show countless differences of that chaitanya part; therefore, there are many Vishwadevas in this Vishwadeva - 
In the form of a murti too, the self is eternally revealed; that is why what appears to us as the world is true. As stated in the Taittiriya Upanishad - "Whatever is, that is the truth. Whatever we see directly, that is all true. O Sadhaka! Remember that in this world that shows us, only the light of 'I' is true. This world is the manifestation of 'I' in the form of the universe. All these elements will be discussed in detail in the Devi Mahatmya.

## Page 25

Devī Sukta
6
Ahṁ Mitrāvaruṇau - Mitra is one of the names of the Sun. Among the twelve Adityas, he is the principal one. The light of the sattvic quality of the inner organ is called Mitra. The real point is that Dharma is Mitra. Dharma is the true friend; because, even after death, he accompanies and gives joy. Varuṇa is the lord of water. He submerges the living being in the ocean of the world for an infinite time. The word Varuṇa means the lowest place. Therefore, "Mitro Varuṇau Dharmadharm" is also said in the scriptures.
Indrāgni Sukha Dukha. Indra is the embodiment of wealth, that is, the form of happiness, and Agni, the form of burning, is the form of sorrow. Therefore, the word Indrāni means happiness and sorrow. Similarly, Ashvinau - Prāṇāpānau, that is, the Kalpavriksha. Prāṇa and Apana winds are called Ashvinikumāra. Mitrāvaruṇau, Indrāgni, and Ashvinau; these are dual deities; therefore, they are Dvandva. In the gross world, this deity is Dharmādharmma and the happiness and sorrow that arise from it, and their enjoyment are revealed in the form of Apana along with Prāṇa as a single "I" - the pure consciousness of the self. Prāṇa is not just a solid wind, it is a place of experience. Due to the companionship of Apana, the enjoyment of Prāṇa is realized. The word "Vibhāmi" means to hold both. Apart from the self, there is no other substance. Therefore, he is revealed in these forms and even when revealed through these specific emotions, his pure, unbroken consciousness remains unchanged, only the self is the cause and material of the world; therefore, he is one - "I" - but shines with many emotions, therefore, he is the holder of many emotions. Therefore, the word "Vibhāmi" is used in the Mantra.
Here it is also said that the words Rudra, Vasu, Aditya, etc.

## Page 26

10
* Devi Sukta
Seeing this explanation, one should not think that these names refer to any divine forms. The words like Rudra, which are the illuminators of a specific consciousness, are the names of that specific emotional aspect of consciousness. It is ever-present everywhere. Bound by the fervent prayers of devotees, it manifests in the form of a specific deity with grace. It shines according to the impressions of each devotee. This is how the descriptions of divine forms are found in the Puranas and other scriptures. See the concept of the deity in the second section.
I am the one who drinks Soma, I am the son of Tvashta, the nourisher, I am Bhaga. I give wealth to the sacrificer, the one who offers oblations, the one who is empty. || 2 ||
In the translation, I hold the deities Soma, Tvashta, Pusha, and Bhaga. Those who perform sacrifices like Soma Yagna, rich in oblations, according to the purpose of those deities, I hold their sacrificial result as "I" || 2 ||
Explanation - The word Ahana means destroyer of enemies. The word Soma means Soma Yagna. Soma Yagna is performed to defeat enemies like lust, anger, etc. Later, the word Soma means the moon. The moon is the deity who is the master of the mind. When the mind rises to subdue enemies like lust, anger, etc., it is called Ahana Soma.
Tvashta - the architect of the world, who constructs this world. That is, he who through consciousness manifests the world in various names and forms.
Pusha - the sun. Later, the form of consciousness that manifests as nourishment in a physical and mental sense. His name is Pusha.
Bhaga - the lord of wealth, that is, the lord. All kinds of prosperity.

---

## Page 27

* Devi Sukta
11
And that which is manifest as Chaitanya (consciousness) due to the unobstructed will is known as Bhag (the divine).
This means that I, Soma, Tvashta, Pusha, and Bhaga, the deities who destroy enemies, are all contained within me. It is my Atman, which is the embodiment of Sanchidananda (blissful consciousness), that manifests all these forms. I hold the dravina (sacrificial offering). It is not just Soma Yaga and other ritual actions that I hold, but even the dravina of all ritual actions is held by me. When the prescribed ritual actions are performed as per the scriptures, an extraordinary, auspicious result is obtained. After time, this extraordinary result produces the promised fruit. This extraordinary is what is called dravina. The sacrificer, who performs the ritual actions with a lot of havis (sacrificial ghee) with the intention of pleasing the deities, holds the dravina of such rituals for the benefit of the sacrificer. All ritual actions, in the form of karma, sanskara, and kamaphala, shine in the form of my single Chaitanya Atman. This is the essence of this mantra.
I am the first among the yajamana (sacrificers), the physician of the world, and the first among those who perform yajnas. Therefore, I, the Devi, have created the Purusha, the earth, and the universe. In this universe, I am the only Adishakti (primordial power). I am the one who gives both earthly and celestial wealth. I am the embodiment of Brahma in direct experience and the embodiment of knowledge. This knowledge is the origin of all worship. I am present in various states in the form of the universe. With a vast heart, I am present in countless beings, and the deities worship me in various states of mind.

---

## Page 28

12
* Devi Sukta
Explanation - In this Mantra, the word "aham" is used in the form of feminine gender. "Aham" is ungendered and operates in all genders. A saying is also heard - "You are a man, a woman in thought, who will meet with wisdom in scriptures." By seeing the use of the word "feminine gender" in this Mantra and also seeing the development of consciousness in the form of power in other Mantras, it is known that the ancient sages named these Ved Mantras as the Devi Sukta. It was previously said - "Aham" is unmanifest and indescribable. From the moment it appears in the sentence, it is revealed in the form of power. Use the word "puruṣa" for male, "durga, kālī, rādhā" for feminine, or "brahma" for neuter - nothing comes out of it. It remains stable as long as it is revealed through the mind, intellect, senses, or concentration. Then it is directly manifest in the form of power. Now, let's try to understand the meaning of this Mantra. The word "raṣṭrī" means the creation, maintenance, and destruction of the infinite universe, and also the lord of the world. The word "vasu" means wealth. Both material wealth like gold and silver, and non-material wealth like knowledge and wisdom, are one and the same, that is, all kinds of wealth-giving. It was previously said that I am manifest from wealth, now I say that I am also the giver of that wealth. The word "chikitsā" means Brahma-jñāna. That knowledge by which a being can experience 'I' is Brahma-jñāna. "Chikitsā" is the first among the Yajñas. Knowing the nature of "chikitsā" a little, one does not become involved in Yajñas and other forms of worship, so they become less valid. From this, it is understood that "chikitsā" is the origin of all forms of worship. It is understood that worship, the fruit of worship, and the origin of worship or the root of the Karmakāṇḍa is of the form of knowledge.

## Page 29

Devī Sukta
Only the form of 'I' consciousness shines forth. The term 'bhūri-sthātrā' signifies various states. The term 'bhūri-āveśayantī' signifies various entrances. I am in the state of infinite states. Then, within these infinite states, 'I' am eternally present. "Tāṁ Mā Devā Vyad" - in this way, I (Ātma) worship the deity. The deities, endowed with elevated knowledge, valor, and perfection, manifest as "I" in this world of infinite diversity. I am worshipped in various states, meaning wherever one sees, wherever one experiences, that very place is where they recognize "I am the true Self, thus, in a childlike simplicity, they call me Self (Mā). This is the very characteristic of a deity.
Maya so'nnamati yo vipaśyati ya prāṇiti ya i śṛṇotyuktaṁ. Aman tav upakṣīyanti shruti shruta śraddhivante vadāmi ॥4॥
Translation - The living being who consumes food and drink, perceives, and sustains life, all these actions are fulfilled by me. Those who do not see me in this way, who do not understand me, they attain weakness in the world. Oh, Soma! Listen to the essence of the truth with faith ॥4॥
Explanation - The term 'anna' means edible substance. It is for the protection of the gross body or to nourish the subtle body like the mind. The living being who consumes food or engages in various pursuits is sustained by the true Self 'I'.
Vipaśyati perceives. Is it through the eye of knowledge, or is it through the eye of desire? The living being who perceives directly, the act of direct perception is also sustained by me.
Ya prāṇiti - he who always breathes in and out, sustains life through the act of respiration.

## Page 30

14
* Devi Sukta
By the Devi, the living beings live, and I am the only "doer" of this.
He who hears, through the ears, the words of the living beings, I am the only "doer" of this.
In this way, all kinds of actions are accomplished by me. That which people do not understand, do not believe in, is called "the mother of all doubts."
Man who, day and night, scorns the actions of others, who, after understanding the "I" returns to the world, who is this man? What is the nature and function of this "I"? By a little thought, everyone can understand; yet, those who do not want to understand, even after wanting to, ignore "me," belittle "me." In the Ishopanishad, such a man is called an atma-han or self-harming person. In this way, those who belittle the truth, belittle the self, are reaped in various ways in the world.
Gratitude is seen in the form of light in the path of dharma, in the form of a deer, and in other lower castes. If a living being does not develop this virtue, he is considered inferior even to an animal. In this way, he is also ignored by everyone's eyes in the world. Suppose you are in such a difficult situation in the path that you are reaped for a rupee, but when you reach your house, a rupee will not be a lack for you; but today, you are sitting in a completely unknown place, humiliated by the lack of a rupee. If at such a time, an unknown person, without asking, gives you a rupee, you can give a hundred rupees in return when you reach your house; but as long as you live, the moment you meet him, a feeling of gratitude - a feeling of humility - will rise in your heart, if you are a man. And whoever is the inspirer of all our actions, whose light shines, is the one who gives us this feeling.

And no one is. - He is always experienced by you, He is completely direct to you, you cannot remain for a moment without Him. If you consider Him far, He is far; if not, He is near even from near. He remains true even when 'I' experience all senses. Seek refuge in His feet.
I alone declare these principles; they are served by gods and humans. 'I' whom I wish, I give Him the highest position, I make Him Brahma, I make Him a sage, I give Him the power of self-knowledge.

## Page 32

16
* Devi Sukta
Explanation - The true essence of "I" is none other than "I" itself. Who else can say this? Because, I am the knower, I am the seer, I know everything. There is no other who knows me. This is why it is said, "I alone speak of myself." And this essence - the realization of one's own self - is the ultimate prayer for both gods and humans. Brahma, Vishnu, Maheshwar, and other gods perform tapasya for eternity, as you have heard in the Puranas. Even after attaining the highest position, they seek a certain object. You will understand this without any doubt. They seek the essence of "I - this is the truth. Where there is no feeling of Brahmam, Vishnum, etc., they seek the eternal, unchanging self - 'I'." Then humans will do so too. The word "just" can also mean "worship." The suffix 'kt' is also used in the present day. Both gods and humans serve 'I' through knowledge or ignorance. The ignorant serve the 'I' of the living being, while the knowledgeable serve the 'I' free from all feelings. I am one - "Eko." Whether from the feeling of life or the feeling of divinity, or free from all feelings, I am the self, the embodiment of eternal bliss, shining forth. What I desire - I will uplift that. It is through my own desire that the living being attains the highest position. Upliftment is of two kinds. Material and spiritual. Material - happiness, wealth, fame, etc. - anyone may not understand that they have achieved it through hard work and intense effort. The only reason for that upliftment is the desire of the human form of "I." After that, spiritual - it is manifested in three ways; wisdom, sage, and Brahma. Through the desire of the self, which is the embodiment of eternal bliss, when the living being experiences the first taste of spiritual life, then he becomes wise, that is, the intellect that holds the realization of self-knowledge.

## Page 33

It is obtained. Until this Dhāraṇāvidhāya is not obtained, that knowledge and this instruction cannot be understood even by hearing. This is the first sign of spiritual life, where the jīva becomes sumedhā. After this, rishihood is obtained. "Rṣayo manttra-drṣṭāḥ." He who is established in Brahma-samvedana and ātmanubhūti everywhere, when his experience or realization comes out in the form of speech, it is then called a mantra. This mantra-drṣṭā is the sādhaka himself, the rishi. The real point is that the one who is everywhere self-realized is the true rishi. This is the second stage of spiritual advancement. After this, Brahma-Hiraṇyagarbha, the center of the creation, sustenance, and destruction of the world, is reached. At this place, the jīva reaches the third stage of spiritual advancement. Until the end of time or the end of Brahma's play does not come, the jīva has to reside in the Brahma loka. This unending threefold advancement is the desire of the one and only ātma. I am the desire that brings about all this. I am Rudra, the destroyer of Brahma's knowledge, equipped with the bow of Pranava and the arrow of my own self. Thus, I fight against the human race. I am immersed in both Svarga and mortal worlds with all my being. ||6||
Translation - I am Rudra, the destroyer of Brahma's knowledge, equipped with the bow of Pranava and the arrow of my own self. Thus, I fight against the human race. I am immersed in both Svarga and mortal worlds with all my being. ||6||
Explanation - The eleven senses and the mind (refer to the first mantra) are these enemies of Brahma, that is, enemies of Brahma's knowledge. Here, the word 'Rudra' is used in a dialogue, because the sense group is within the mind. The existence of the mind is the existence of the senses, and the dissolution of the mind is the dissolution of the senses.

## Page 34

* Devi Sukta
Laya is. Only the mind is Sharavya, meaning destructive. The place where Sharapata is done is called Sharavya. Yakāra is the Chand of Laya. Sāyanā has interpreted 'Sharavya' as violent. This meaning can also be accepted here. The mind is opposed to Brahman knowledge; therefore, the mind is called violent. The meaning of the word 'Dhanuḥ' is Pranava - Omkāra or simply Mantra. The meaning of the word 'Ātmanomi' is I am planning the arrow. The Upaniṣad says, "Pranavo Dhanuḥ, Sharohyātma Brahmatallakṣyamucyate." Pranava is Dhanuḥ, Shar is Ātma (Jīvātma Bhāva), and Brahman is the ultimate goal. The fruit of Pranava or Mantra form Dhanuḥ, rising as Shar from Jīvātma to Brahman with the intention of leaving is the Laya of the mind. This mind is Rudra. This is also the opponent of Brahman knowledge. This mind turns the undivided Chaitanya into the divided world through Jnana, making it violent or Sharavya; this is done through the Dhāraṇa or Samādhi of Yoga, Puja, Homa, Prārthana, etc. These are all means. The meaning is that even in the form of Sadhana, "I" am the one who shines. It was previously said that in the form of Rudra, "I" am exalted. Here, "I" am also the one who rises to destroy Rudra. This "I" is the action of being in the form of Jīva, the world, and the form of bondage. Then, the means to break this bond and become undivided are also "I," and so is Moksha. It is also said here that even after the five Mantras, there is a mystery in this Mantra. Those who are accustomed to seeing the Atman with all their hearts, that is, "Sarvabhūtasthamātmanam sarvabhūtāni cātmani. Saṁpashyan brahma paramaṁ yāti nānyena hetunā" - the Sadhana of this Veda Mantra, who has achieved success; he alone experiences the Prakāsha of the Dhāraṇa of the Atman's Dhanuḥ Shar to destroy Rudra or the mind.

## Page 35

In the Devi Sukta, sixteen, one becomes capable. This is first described in the initial five mantras with full devotion, and in this mantra, it is also revealed that the form of Sadhana is merely the realization of the identity of the Atman, 'I' or the Atman itself. This is clearly stated. In the case of a buddhiyogi, these states are usually attained without effort. "I shall create the world, I shall maintain it, I shall destroy it" - 'I' - the pure form of Chaitanya Atman alone fights for the life of the jiva. When the life force of the jiva rises to establish the kingdom of the Atman, then it is seen that the mind has captured everything. The life force wants to offer everything to the feet of Bhagwan to achieve the purpose of life, while the mind wants to bind the world in desires. This is the auspicious moment of the transition from life to life, only then does the battle begin. In Kurukshetra - the field of action - this kind of battle is organized, and after that, in the field of science, the battle between the gods and demons is organized (as described in the Chandi tattva), which I have also done. Therefore, in the field of Sadhana, in the field of subject, everywhere, the only controller of all actions is 'I' Atman. "I have entered Dyaus and Prithivi" - I illuminate the worlds of Yuloka and Bhuloka everywhere. The Devlok is the field of science. Here, after the completion of Atmabodh, the vision of Chaitanya Maya Brahmasattva occurs. The Bhuloka is the Annamaya Kosha or the physical body, and the other two Koshas are not mentioned separately as they are within these two worlds. The Atman's Annamaya Kosha - this is the Virat Brahmmand. The Pranamaya Kosha is the power of creation, maintenance, and destruction. The Manomaya Kosha is the resolve to manifest from many emotions. The Vijnanamaya Kosha - in which this knowledge is highly valued. The Anandamaya Kosha - in which the Atman's form is purely Anandamaya. In this place, the seed of the world is established from the unmanifest state. This is the Samashti or Virat Vijnanamaya Kosha.

Vyakhya - Jagatpita-Hiranyakashipu, from whom this Jeev jagat is said to have been created, is previously known to be the Paramatma's Manomaya Kosha or Samashti Man. Prithvi and the other five bhutas are the Adi-Akasha or Vyomatva. Above this Vyomatva is the Man. In the Man, the sankalpa of the Akasha and other bhutas remains. Just as we make various kinds of imaginations in our mind, the imagination of Samashti or Virat Man is this Brahmand. Our mind's imaginations are momentary and invisible to others; but the sankalpa of the Bhano Maya Atma is dense, long-lasting, and the experience of all living beings, this Virat Purush's name is Hiranyakashipu - he is the father of the world. This Sanchidananda Swarupa Atma has been created by " ". In this way, " " is also the mother of Jagatpita. Aham suve pitaramasya mūrdhāna mam yonirapasantḥ samudre - above this is our cause body.

## Page 37

* Devi Sukta
21
is established. Apas is situated within the ocean, in the middle of the ocean's water.
The word 'samudra' means bliss. It is also said in the scriptures that all beings are 'samudravan' or filled with bliss. The meaning of 'samudra' is also derived from the prefix 'sam' and the suffix 'udra,' which together mean 'that which is filled with water' or 'that which is filled with bliss.' The word 'samudra' is derived from the root 'samyak' meaning 'to taste' or 'to relish.' Bliss is what gives life its taste, and thus the ocean is called 'bliss.' The Acharya Sanyanadev has also interpreted this as the Paramatman. Paramatman and bliss are one and the same. The word 'ap' means expansive, intelligent, and virtuous nature, as mentioned in the Sanyabhasya. Intelligent nature is also called the 'science-filled kosha.' In the explanation of the previous mantra, the five koshas of the Paramatman were detailed. Therefore, we understand that the 'science-filled kosha' is also established within the 'bliss-filled kosha,' above Hiranyagarbha. This 'science-filled kosha' is the 'mamayoni' body of the Paramatman. Although the cause-body of the living being is known as the 'bliss-filled kosha,' it is not just the 'bliss-filled kosha' that is the cause; it is the science within it that is the true cause. Science is what sustains this world. It is a detached witness, merely a seer. It creates the infinite, varied universe of the material world through the union of its vision or light. Therefore, Hiranyagarbha, or the father of the world, is established above the 'cause-body of our soul.' Thus, I establish myself in all worlds. "I touch that which is beyond the sky, the sun, and the moon." That which is the heaven-like, distant world of ordinary beings, known as the 'science-filled kosha,' I have also touched with my body. The entire universe is the 'body' of our collective bliss; thus, it is only through ascent in this world that one can achieve it.

I alone am the wind, the originator of the worlds.
Each day, each month, each year, I am born on this earth. 
Translation - When the wind blows, the creation of the entire world begins. I am also present beyond this heaven. This is my glory.
Explanation - The wind, which flows like the wind, is the illuminator of the power of action. Among the elements, air is inactive, indifferent, and the foundation. But the wind in the form of flow is full of action. The Gita says, "As the wind is always present in the sky, so are all beings supported by the air. Just as the vast and all-pervading wind is situated in the sky, so are all beings situated in the Atman. When the living being advances towards the direct perception of the Atman, then it sees it as full of action. Until the Atman is approached through the characteristics of the body, it remains like the wind flowing. This is why the Vedanta Sutra says, 'From birth.' This is the answer to the question of Brahma-jijnasa. That from which the entire world is born, in which it is situated, and into which it dissolves, that is Brahma, that is the Atman, that am I. Whatever I want to know, the answer is only 'From birth.' There is no other simple answer. That which is the mother, nurturer, and controller of the world, the powerful mother of creation, that is 'I.' This is why the word 'str' (female) is used in the mantra. 'Adbhama' is the ultimate goal and worship of all beings. As long as the world exists, it remains 'the wind that blows' - this is the truth of the scriptures. From a state of formless, the person is. ## Page 40

Argala
Mātṛmukhī Gati
The term "argala" means "hindrance" or "obstacle." Just as a house cannot be entered when its door is locked, similarly, by reciting the Argala Stotra before the Devimahatmya, one cannot enter the realm of external subjects. For an outwardly or completely distracted mind, entering the Chandi element is harmful; therefore, great compassionate preceptors have prescribed the Argala Kalaka and Devi Kavach recitation before the Chandi Stotra to slightly direct the mind towards the maternal aspect. Without Mantra Chaitanya, the fruit of even the most elaborate stotras is at most ordinary. The Mantra Chaitanya will be explained in the Brahma Stotra of the Devimahatmya. In this stotra, from the first line like 'Jaya tvaṁ Devi,' an attempt is made to channel the mind's vrittis into the maternal aspect through the pronunciation of the word 'Jaya.' The explanation of each word in the given stuti has not been provided. This is because, while explaining Chandi, the explanation of most words will be given. Especially, the explanation of these words is not particularly difficult. Only a learned person can understand it. "Madhu Kaitabh Vidwamsi, Mahisasura Nirnashi, etc., the meanings of these words have been made clear in their respective places.

## Page 41

* Argalā
25
Astu, this stotra's nearly every mantra is in the form of "Dehi." Dehi, Yashodehi, Dvishojahi" - this is half a verse. An explanation of this part is absolutely necessary. One should understand the meaning in the same way as their qualification.
Rūpaṁ dehi -
(1) Ma, give us a beautiful form, make us healthy.
(2) Ma, show us your form.
(3) Ma, your form is the world; help me understand it.
(4) Ma, remove the form that gives us the feeling of absence.
Here, the meaning of the word "dehi" is "to fulfill the absence." The word "dehi" is used to fulfill absence.
(5) Ma, the Supreme Self is the only indescribable entity; help me understand its form. Here, "rupya nirupya iti rūpaṁ tachcha paramātma vastu - this is the meaning of the word 'rupa'."
Jayṁ dehi -
(1) Ma, give us victory.
(2) Ma, grant us victory in the battle of sadhana.
(3) Ma, make me the master of my mind and senses.
(4) Ma, let your form be victory, that is, let your form be the victory in my mind.
(5) Ma, establish us in truth. Here, the meaning of the word "jay" is truth. The Upanishad says, "Satyameva jayate nānṛtam - only truth is victorious, not falsehood. Only truth is victorious; falsehood is not victorious. Truth alone is victory. Only 'truth alone' shines everywhere in all feelings. This is the name of establishing oneself in this knowledge, which is the true experience of victory."

I am praying to Icchāśakti Devi, who is the embodiment of Prakriti and my beloved, who is dear to my heart. She is the one who is dear to my mind and the one who fulfills desires. In this way, the person who desires something, who understands the absence of a seeker like a Sadhak, should pray to Ma like a simple child. The person who is like an officer will receive the benefit in the same way. There is no prohibition on asking from Ma. The ideal man, who was among the ancient sages of our country, also used to pray happily when necessary. This did not hinder their Brahman knowledge; who else could stop them from asking? She who fulfills every desire of our life, always shining as a Kalpataru, is none other than our Ma - the Atman or 'I'. If you want - ask her; she will not be opposed; ask with simple faith. She can give everything with just a wish. Hold this faith in your heart. Just wishing will not suffice; even if you get it, you will not understand. As much faith as you take from a person, you will ultimately keep that much faith and gain certainty. Then who will know what is small and what is big. Whether it is wealth or jewels or Atmajnāna, you will get what you want. Just practice wishing. Wherever you see that you do not get the desired thing even by wishing, understand that your faith has not been fulfilled. Truly, "Ma is a Kalpataru, this is the truth, we want from her." When this understanding becomes firm, the prayer will definitely be fulfilled. Where is Ma? You do not need to search for her. She shines everywhere, in all forms, with full presence. Wherever you say, she will listen there. Remember that her ear is always raised to listen to your every word. Then it is also true that she can give the sovereignty of the entire universe with just a wish.

## Page 45

The request for Argalashtotra is the duty of children; otherwise, there is no fault in making the request. She is not displeased by the desire. Indeed, there is a special need to discuss the fulfillment of these desires in the first chapter of the Chandi path. The living being is naturally attached to objects, is self-centered, and is always burning with the fire of desire. Therefore, if one sees the natural way to fulfill the first desire, he is inevitably attracted to it. Even if one is attracted by the desire for the fruit, if one is motherly, it is the greatest fortune. And for those who have experienced the path of self-awareness, who have turned their face towards their mother, who have a thirst for material gain that has entered their lives, there is a great need for the powers that will help them. Without obtaining this power, one cannot enter the heavy Chandi element or the path of liberation. Therefore, Argalashtotra is for obtaining this power. Even then, by reciting the stotra with the consciousness of the Mantra, external mental states become internal for some time, and there is an opportunity to advance in the practice of Sadhana.

## Page 46

Kilak
Adhikar Nirnay
The term Kilak at this place means the obstacle to the desired achievement. In common language, it is called a curse. Many famous mantras like Gayatri contain curses from various sages or deities. Along with the curse, there is also a method to remove it. This Kilak Stuti is also a special curse-removal. The Kilak of the Devimahatmya, which is mantra-based, is also by Mahadeva. By removing that Kilak, the Devimahatmya text fails to bestow the desired fruit. The true mystery of this curse or Kilak is the determination of the right. How to ascend to a place, how to achieve mental elevation, how to gain the strength of Sadhana, and how to enter the realm of Chandi – all these are established in the Kilak Stuti. The exact truth of removing the curses of other mantras is the same. Listen, in this stuti, it is said: "Krishna, or Chaturdasha, or Ashtami, or Samahitah. He gives, he accepts, he remains pleased with lesser things." Thus, in this form, Kilak is established by Mahadeva. He who chants Chandi daily, free from Kilak, becomes a Siddha, a sage, and is born as Gandharva. He surely attains immortality and at the time of death, he attains liberation.

## Page 47

* Kailak
31
This means that on the fourteenth or eighth day of the Krishna Paksha, giving and receiving should take place; otherwise, Chandi will not be pleased. In this way, Mahadeva has cursed Chandi through Kailak. The person who, by being devoid of this (i.e., by giving and receiving), recites Chandi daily, becomes enlightened. Explanation - There is no mention of giving and receiving in the original text. Who will give, what will be given, to whom will it be given, and from whom will it be received - none of these are mentioned in the Mantra. Therefore, different devotees have interpreted it in different ways. However, many doubts are not resolved from these interpretations. Well, we have understood the meaning of this place, which Jnanarupini Ma has developed in our hearts; come, thirsty Sadhaka! Let us discuss the meaning of this interpretation once. Krishna Chaturdashi, Krishna Ashtami - these are the characteristics of the Sadhaka. In this place, the seventh case is used as a characteristic; it is not used in the adhikaran. Its proof is described in detail in Raghunandan's Tithitattva. In valid work, in the sankalpa sentence, when the mention of the month, paksha, tithi, etc. is made, it is a purusha-characteristic, i.e., that specific month, that specific paksha, that specific tithi, that specific person. Therefore, here too, Krishna Paksha Chaturdashi and Ashtami tithi are the specific person or Sadhaka of this Mantra. How can the Sadhaka be in a certain condition to become eligible for this characteristic? The name of the lunar month of Kali is Krishna Paksha; the moon is the deity of the mind. Chaturdashi - the single remaining moon or mind. Ashtami - half-moon or half-mind. The mind that can offer a gift to the mother in the last half, or can offer itself to me for the sake of gain, by leaving the last half of the mind, that is Krishna Ashtami.

Once there, one does not return. This is my ultimate abode. "Having gone there, they do not return" (Gita). In that state, Chandi or the battle of Sadhana ends completely; that is why only from Krishna Ashtami to Chaturdashi is mentioned in this place, and that is the composed state. In the remaining part of the Stuti, it is said, 'Enemy's destruction, but Moksha is praised, not by any human.' Vedanta and other philosophies are certainly necessary for Moksha Shastra, but they are completely detached from worldly and familial fruits, and only those who are solely devoted to Moksha are entitled to listen to these philosophies. However, Devimahatmya is the Sadhana for both fruits; this is made explicit in the Keelak Stuti. Those who only speak of praise, we cannot have unity with them. As it is said in the Keelak Stuti, by becoming detached - remaining composed, entering the Chandi element, certainly both enjoyment and liberation are obtained. But, my! What is our recourse! Kavach - bodyguard. By wearing it, self-defense from the beginningless enemies is obtained; it is called a Kavach. To enter the battle of Sadhana, one must advance wearing this Kavach; otherwise, the hope of obtaining victory is merely vain. It is said, "By chanting the Chandi for seven hundred times, your Kavach will be complete. Nirvighna will be Siddhi, born from Chandi Japa." The Kavach chapter is recited before the seven hundred Chandi chapter. Those who can be covered by this Kavach; they alone are capable of obtaining success or Siddhi from Chandi Japa without any obstacle. 
This Kavach mentions different limbs and body parts, and those covered by this Kavach are capable of obtaining success or Siddhi from Chandi Japa without any obstacle.

## Page 52

* Devi Kavach
The method for protection of places is to pray with the remembrance of different names of Ma. For example, 'Prachyaam rakshatu maamendri' - Ma in the form of Indrashakti should protect us in the eastern direction; or 'Shikhaam dyotini rakset' - Ma in the form of Prakashashakti should protect us in the place of the hair; similarly, it is applicable everywhere. In this, wherever places are mentioned, the mind should be made central and taken to those places. As the mind is controlled in the place for some time, a specific bodhashakti will be manifested there; it will not be a conceptual experience but a direct experience. Focusing on that experience, one should remember and pray to different names of Ma. In the Kavach, wherever the method of chanting a particular name is mentioned, one should try to experience the dharma or shakti associated with that name. There is no need to focus on a specific form while chanting these names; it is sufficient to focus on the dharma bodh. For example, in 'Khadgadhari' - without focusing on the form of the one holding the sword, one should try to experience the strength of holding the sword firmly. Similarly, it is applicable everywhere. Those who are adept in the practice of Jagatmaya Satyapratishta or have been initiated by a guru and have made the mind central through practice, can easily manage the operation of this bodhashakti. Even if this is not the case, a person can achieve success in this Kavach through ordinary effort. By taking this bodhashakti to different parts of the body and experiencing it as maternal shakti, the practical and real effectiveness of the Kavach is achieved. It should not be misunderstood that the remaining part of this Kavach is merely a stuti. It is filled with truth in every word.

## Page 53

* Devi Kavach
37
It has been established. Even if one does not experience the benefits of other fruits or does not have the opportunity to examine them, the verse 'Nashyanti vyadhi sarve' ensures the destruction of all physical ailments. This has been observed by many people. It is not just physical health that is gained; simultaneously, an enhancement in mental strength and spiritual progress is also inevitable. Additionally, it is mentioned that regardless of which of the various kavachas of different gods and goddesses, such as Ramakavach, Suryakavach, Shrikrishnakavach, Kalikavach, etc., are recited according to the previously mentioned rules, all fruits are obtained; there is no doubt about this. In the Devi Kavach, more limbs are mentioned compared to other kavachas. However, for those who are the actual implementers of the true fruits of Chandi Path - Dharma, Artha, Kama, and Moksha - the kavach path is extremely necessary. This is because it leads to victory in the realm of Sadhana-Samar through Nirvichara Sadhana. Therefore, recitation according to the rules is necessary; otherwise, the path to achieving the desired fruits becomes more distant. The Devi Sukta, Argala, Keel, and Devi Kavach are preparatory steps for entering the realm of Chandi in Sadhana-Samar. This is like a festival of effort that, when performed as beautifully, meticulously, and with great dedication as possible, results in a success and spiritual progress that is equally beautiful, meticulous, and faster. So, as long as our previous practices are not well-maintained, will we remain detached from the Chandi Path? No, we will not; through repeated practice, through repeated recitation of the Chandi Path, one day we will see that the previous arrangements have almost concluded due to the influence of some unknown power in our unknown circumstances; only then will we understand the true mystery of Chandi, receive the grace of the mother, and be grateful.

Gita, Chandi, and Prasad

Just as one enters a very high temple by crossing many steps, similarly, by achieving success through the practice of the seven sacred mantras mentioned in the Gita, one enters the Chandi form's liberation temple.
Those who are accustomed to the intellectual yoga of the Gita are entitled to see the Devimahatmya.
What is Chandi? It is impossible to express it in words.
Chandi is the three waves of motherly meeting. After submerging in the ocean of eternal bliss, which precisely cuts the three unbreakable knots of life, those are the three mysteries of Chandi.
In the Bhagavad Gita, the meeting in the ocean of Brahma and the complete immersion in Chandi is depicted.
When the living being has complete faith in maternal creation, when the living being, with complete feeling, expresses maternal conduct,
then he sees that "my mother, in the form of Chandi, has emerged from our heart battlefield, has destroyed the obstacles to the path of liberation in the form of evil impressions, and has merged with my body."

Second - the severing of Mahishasura, or Vishnu-pranathi. Third - the severing of Shumbha, or Rudra-pranathi. All these will be discussed in detail with respect to their specific nature. Only then does the battle between the gods and demons begin in the jīva's heart; then the jīva directly experiences - Ma herself incarnates in the battlefield to dissolve all demonic emotions. Ma's wish is to free her son from all emotions, to purify and sanctify him, and to merge with him. How can one not be satisfied by the experience of the nectar-like touch of the mother's auspicious hand? Where is there any sorrow or destruction on the blissful and divine chest? Especially, the son of the sadhaka loves to see the Chandika form of the mother. That form from which I am motivated to destroy my own ego, that form from which I am motivated to free myself from the unbreakable bond of life, that form is the most desired for the son of the sadhaka - even more than the beloved. How can a simple, fearless child be frightened or contracted by seeing the mother's angry form? No, he tries to climb the mother's lap even more quickly and run. Oh life! Are you not disturbed by the attack of death, which is beyond your control? Can you understand this terrifying, ever-changing life as nothing more than an excitement? Have you experienced the desire for eternal stability for even a moment from within your heart? Can you understand yourself, completely shaken and stunned, by the unbearable actions of anger and desire? In disease, in sorrow, in the unjust actions of a cruel person, do you not feel extremely miserable?

It is said that in ancient times, Vyasa's disciple, the great sage Jaimini, and sage Markandeya, in a sequential encounter, were eager to hear the Devimahatmya. However, due to Markandeya not having the opportunity, he had to listen to the story of Chandi from the four birds near the Bindhyachal region. Just as Markandeya Muni had previously narrated the Devimahatmya to Koshti Muni, the birds narrated the same story to Jaimini with the same sentiment. That is why it is said 'Markandeya said' at this place. Markandeya is my own person or the eye of wisdom - and Jaimini is the world or the living being. Markandeya, the seven-lucky-living being - the immortal. When a being can understand its own immortality; when they can experience consciousness, life, eternal, stable, deathless, and birthless; when they can make the past, future, and present visible in the mirror of the mind; when they are freed from the cycle of time through the grace of the knowledgeable Guru, Lord Mahadeva, and enter the field of wisdom, then only does the being become Markandeya, that is, wise or immortal. Only then can the limited, result-oriented, doubt-filled Jaimini-like materialistic world be capable of describing the great power of this Mahamaya in the form of a great laughter. From this, we see in the Chandi's story that the Devimahatmya is described in the conversation between Markandeya and Jaimini. Seeing this spiritual explanation, no one will fall into the illusion that Markandeya or Jaimini was any sage, or that the Chandi's story is a fictional narrative. Through the guise of a story, CCO. Vasishtha Tripathi Collection. Digitized By Siddhanta eGangotri Gyaan Kosha

## Page 63

Spiritual Commentary
To describe the spiritual mysteries to a person with a little intelligence is the very purpose of all the Puranas; this is something we can never say. Because vision has three types - spiritual, divine, and material. When a tree is seen as a specific material object like its branches and leaves; until then, it is material vision. When it is seen that a consciousness alone is manifested in the form of a tree, then it is called divine vision; because the vision achieved by looking at the consciousness established in the tree or the deity is called divine vision. And when the yogic eye or the third eye of the living being opens with the grace of the guru, then it sees that the self, that is, 'I' alone is manifested in the form of a tree; this type of vision is called spiritual vision. The knowledge of the living being revolves around these three levels; therefore, the development of knowledge of these three types is inevitable in every object or event of the world. Some see that the source of the river flows out; some see that the river rushes out to meet the ocean with the help of the master; then some see that our own self - our own life - our own affectionate stream is established. None of these are deluded; all are true visionaries. When knowledge revolves around which level, then the development of that level-appropriate experience takes place. Then that which is stable in the gross, that is, in the physical world, that is, in the material state, is merely an object or an event; it is reflected in the subtle as the form of specific consciousness in the kingdom of consciousness or in the divine state. Then for this reason, in the field of the self, that is, in the spiritual state, it is only reflected as the form of the self, that is, as 'I'. Whose eyes have seen in what kind of way - whose knowledge by nature revolves around what level, they will grasp that kind of meaning. The point is that from the ordinary vision, what is directly perceived in the gross, a conscious person sees.

---

## Page 64

The gross and subtle are one; they are perceived with an unchanging state of mind (as 'tyo' is to 'jyo'). From this, the saying arises, 'What is in the universe is also in the body (deha).' Gross and subtle are merely quantitative or dimensional differences; both are fundamentally identical. Therefore, it is necessary to remember with special emphasis that the story of Chandi is not merely allegorical; it is a completely true event. Then, for the sake of spiritual education, what is enacted in the physical kingdom in the gross form is similarly constructed in the field of consciousness or the kingdom of the self within each living being. In the world of living beings, there is a similar hierarchy in the gross, subtle, and causal; there are similar immutable laws. So far, no name of a life-liberated sadhak has been heard, or it is not possible for anyone whose heart has not experienced the essence of Kurukshetra's battle-song or the battle between gods and demons - the development of the Chandi principle. But as a sadhak progresses towards such a goal, and upon reaching the goal, by looking back at the events of life, they see that they have mostly come from the unknown essence of the Gita and the Chandi principle. Indeed, we will mainly enter the Chandi principle to know the primary and divine mysteries. Open our wisdom's eye (uncover it) and let the Chandi principle be revealed in our heart, and we will be fulfilled.

## Page 66

28
Manu Mystery
Vishishta Pratini is a special representative, just as Savarna or Saurashakti is a special representative of Brahmasakti. Due to the influence of this power, Bhootadhatri Vasundhara, and the infinite array of planets, encircling the Sun's mandala, is established in the Mahashunya and salutes her Varṇī Bharg-Pratini Sun deity in a clockwise manner. Due to the influence of this majestic power, the Jeev Sangh retains its existence intact and progresses towards Brahmatva - greatness. Manu is born from this majestic solar power; hence, he is known as Savarna. The word Manu is derived from Manh-dhatu. The meaning of Manh-dhatu is knowledge or wisdom. When a living being, in a conceptual form, becomes a child of consciousness or petty knowledge, and then becomes a human consciousness, then he is called Manu. Just as the collective consciousness of countless Brahmaṇḍs is Hiranyakashipu, similarly, the collective consciousness of the entire human race is Manu. Each conceptual atom of this Manu consciousness is manifested as an individual human being; hence, a human is called Manu. It is also clearly stated that within each human being, there is a constant awareness of 'I am a human'; this awareness is called individual human. The collective human consciousness that encompasses the entire human race is called Manu or collective human consciousness. As long as one remains within the awareness of 'I am a human', one remains capable of experiencing one's humanity. Just as countless microorganisms in our body are conscious due to our own consciousness, similarly, the entire human race is conscious due to the consciousness of Manu. This is why Bhagwan Manu is considered the father of the human race. Therefore, Manu is called Prajapati and Brahma is called grandfather. Manu is the Atmaj or the first creation of Brahma. Through Sadhanabal, when a human attains this.

## Page 68

40
Asana Shuddhi
You will experience the ripples of Mahasati when, by saying "I am this, I am that, I am the Purusha," you will see your Atman and Prana firmly established in the Sun, like the ancient Rishis of the Vedic era, and thus you will be able to correctly understand the essence of life. Only then will you become eligible to gain Manutva after renouncing humanity.
Sadhak! Do not think that this is impossible for you. The Rishi who discovered the eternal and simple path; by following the Guru's prescribed methods step by step on that path, only humans can attain it. In our Devi's activities, there is a practice called Asana Shuddhi, which, by following it, you can proceed on the path of experiencing Solar energy. Currently, Asana Shuddhi is limited to a mere Mantra recitation practice, and thus its true fruit is not obtained. However, here we mention the Mantra and the secret of its Sadhana:
"Pṛthvi tvayā dhṛtā lokā Devi tvam Viṣṇunā dhṛtā. Tvañca dhāraya māṁ nityaṁ pavitraṁ kurucāsanam."
Sadhak! Imagine that you are sitting on the ground like a spherical football. With the same feeling as when you sit and can remain stable for some time, sit in that feeling. 'Samakāya-shirōgrīva' means keep your body, head, and neck straight. Then imagine that everywhere around you—above, below, left, right, front, and back—is Mahāśūnya. You are sitting on the Earth-like form of your mother in the Mahākāśa mandala. In front of you, the Sun is situated in Mahāśūnya. You are held by the loving attraction of the Sun and the Earth. Imagine that the Earth is making a pradakṣiṇā of you, as if you were the Sun's vakṣ. In this situation, chant the aforementioned Mantra with Chaitanya. Its meaning is: O Earth-like Mother! You are holding this group of beings.

## Page 69

O Eighth Manu,
51
You are holding me in your embrace as the sun-like form of Vishnu. Mother!
Hold me and purify my seat; thus, experiencing it, take a bath in the solar light daily, establish truth in the sun and vital energy, and practice staying steady in the sphere of light and sky. After some days, you will see the result of this practice in your inner world; apart from the consciousness-filled light, nothing else will be visible. Gradually, when you enter that infinite, spiritual light sphere as your own self, then only will you understand that you are sitting in the lap of solar power. Then, slowly approaching the great sage Vasishtha, who is near the realization "I am a human being," pray to the incomparable power of the golden goddess for her unparalleled grace. And when that great sage, through his consciousness, makes the ignorant rise, the same knowledge merges with the divine in the form of "I am a human being." Only then will you experience the essence of this humanity. What will happen to us by obtaining this humanity? We will become the Eighth. What is the Eighth? "The Eighth is where eight kinds of siddhis and eight kinds of opulence are realized." Where the eight kinds of opulence are exactly defined, that is the Eighth. When the living being attains this humanity, then the siddhis like anima and laghima come under his control. Here, after obtaining these eight kinds of opulence, the living being experiences the Bhagwan's own form. Similarly, on the other hand, the living being becomes free from eight kinds of bonds like hatred, shame, fear, and criticism. That is why Manu is called the Eighth.
The seeker of liberation, who attains inner liberation from these three states, Mother has given him this information at the beginning of the Devimahatmya. From humanity to humanity and from humanity to Brahmanity, these three states appear before the seeker one after another with the grace of Mother.

## Page 70

Here
Mahamaya
is not mentioned separately from her distinct nature within the realm of Brahmamaya. Like the child Shishupala, who, fearless, plays happily with his companions, enjoying unbounded joy in the form of a game, experiencing indescribable happiness through the movement of each limb, similarly, when a being realizes that we are always in the womb of our father-like Manu, and that our birth, death, illness, grief, childhood, youth, old age, and all other states are mere results, then I am always situated in the blissful womb of my father. Whether I am insignificant, poor, fallen into the deep, dark well of sin, whether I am a skeptic, without faith, or ignorant, the being experiences, "I am always seated in the blissful womb of my father." When the being experiences this blissful awareness, he is constantly enlightened day and night. When this eternal connection is experienced directly, the being remains bewildered by the taste of immortality even while living in this world, and in the ordinary sense, he always experiences this world as a source of indescribable peace. Human being! Have you woken up to experience this eternal peace?
Like the sun, who, due to Mahamaya's favorable will, became the ruler of Manvantara. He became the great sage Savarni, the son of the sun. (Listen to this.)
Explanation - By attaining Manuhood, one becomes the eighth. This was previously mentioned. Now, an extraordinary benefit is mentioned - 'the ruler of Manvantara'. He, who is the embodiment of unbroken Manu-consciousness, can be established in the collective human consciousness, and in the individual human consciousness.

---

## Page 71

Mahamaya
is born from Chaitanya. The human race is the essence of Manu;
becoming Manu, one gains the sovereignty of the Manvantara. The origin, existence, and dissolution of the entire human race are established by his sign. At that time, he can manifest the subtle and causal body of each human being. As a result, each human gains the ability to perceive the latent bhavarashi within. We are human beings; within us, countless births and accumulated samskaras are hidden, which we cannot perceive directly; but when we attain satva, become the sovereign of the Manvantara, we will be able to see our own samskaras, and besides that, we will be able to perceive the accumulated sins and merits, birth, caste, lifespan, enjoyment, etc., of every human being. Sometimes we see that a particular great sage or mahapurush, upon being in our presence, understands our mental state even before we express it; this is the result of attaining partial manutva. Individual humans are the essence of Manu; by gaining sovereignty over this essence, each human can exercise their will over others, transform their inherent lowliness, and engage in spiritual elevation. Only through the experience of Mahamaya and with favorable desire and grace is this manutva attained. In the state of manutva, in the field of vijnana, there arises the illusion that 'the entire world is within our own essence,' this is the true sovereignty of the Manvantara. What is Mahamaya? In this context, many darshankaras and their followers have presented various opinions. Some say it is inert prakriti, some say it is false illusion, etc. There are many opinions! We should not engage in the thought of Maya with the help of sophistry while criticizing their views.

## Page 72

8
Mahamaya is Truth
We desire to know; because we know - 'She' cannot be comprehended. Our purpose is to attain Her, we are ardent with maternal affection, what need do we have to contemplate on Her form? When we stand near the pregnant mother and call out 'Ma', just as we do not contemplate on Her nature, but only receive the effusion of love, let us stand before the form of Mahamaya, the Mother of the universe, and call out 'Ma'. See how She reveals Her form to us with what kind of emotion, and how She sanctifies the child with the effusion of joyous love.
We see that, Mahamaya is the Truth. Apart from Mahamaya, there is nothing else, Mahamaya is the mother of life. We are born from Her womb, we are situated on Her lap, we are nourished from Her love-filled breast; then, through Her grace, we will reach the unique, eternal, and unchanging state of mother-son relationship. That is, I will perfectly merge into Mahamaya. We know - Mahamaya is life, Mahamaya is God, and Mahamaya is Brahma. Where there is no Maya, there is no truth, no falsehood; as long as Maya exists, both truth and falsehood exist; as long as the mind, speech, and senses are the ego, truth is consciousness, and joy is bliss; when the desire to manifest various emotions arises in the form of pure consciousness, then that consciousness manifests itself in the form of Maya. Because it holds the seed of these many emotions in the womb, that is why it is the mother; then, manifesting itself in the form of the world, that is, giving birth to the latent seeds, for the purpose of returning to the state of formlessness, it manifests itself as the powerful creative force, and merges the living world situated in its own nest back into the state of unity and divinity.

## Page 73

Introduction to Mahamaya
Good
Adhishwari, Jagadvidhatri, Jagatpalayintri, Jagat Santri, Mokshapradayini
Janani is Mahamaya.
Only through favorable desire and compassion towards Mahamaya, that is, through experiencing her love, does one become the master of life. Seeker! Do you want to know this? Without experiencing Mahamaya's form partially, one cannot understand the Chandika element; from this, it is said that after being protected by the mother, being nourished by seven breasts, the one who does not recognize her as the mother of her child, no matter how much success her son achieves, no matter how high he climbs the peak of knowledge, no matter how much he is honored in the world, remains a target of hatred, and thus, as a human, if one cannot recognize Mahamaya as the mother, then that human birth is meaningless. Seeker! Do you see your mother? Then look, she appears to you as the embodiment of action from your slave's breathless beginning to the ultimate state of nirvikalpa samadhi, as the embodiment of will and desire, as the embodiment of mind, as the embodiment of passion and anger, as the embodiment of states like childhood, youth, and old age, as the embodiment of birth and death, in the form of great transformation. This is our mother, Mahamaya. From whom you run away, abandoning the envious, cunning, and cruel gaze, thinking of her as an endless form of Sadhana, and from whom you try to pluck out the eyes, calling her Maya, false, binding, this is none other than her! Whether you look up, down, east, west, south, or north, whatever you see, it is our Mahamaya, the mother. The one who is bewildered by the beautiful form of a loving son, he is none other than Mahamaya; the one who is overwhelmed by the touch of a beautiful body part of a woman, he is none other than Mahamaya.

## Page 74

The love of Mahamaya
is - Mahamaya is she; she who, driven by the desire for gold, roams like deer, she is none other than Mahamaya; she who, with the fragrance of flowers, satisfies the sense of smell, she is none other than Mahamaya; she who, with various edible substances, satisfies the taste, she is none other than Mahamaya; every atom of your gross body - she is Mahamaya. Your hunger, thirst, desire, anger, happiness, sorrow, sin, virtue, birth, death, poverty, wealth, heaven, hell, ignorance - she is none other than Mahamaya. Apart from her, there is nothing anywhere; within and outside you, only she is fully revealed with pure emotion. Whomsoever you desire, whomsoever you seek - she, with a loving embrace, from the veil, comes running, mad within the infinite cycle of birth and death, with her hair undone, saying, 'Son! Come! Come!' She brings you with great respect and great effort from the inert atom to the best of the human race. For so many days, you could not recognize Mahamaya; no harm; but now, as a human, you will not recognize her! You will not see her! How can this be called humanity? She is eagerly waiting to hear the maternal call from your mouth! From this, she always teaches you to call her 'Mother' and to call her 'Mother' to you, but you will not call her! Look, you who desire, whether with knowledge or ignorance, she immediately appears in that form - in your desired form. You wanted to play the game of being important, to portray the status of a sudra, to act out the role of a result; see, the loving Mahamaya, with a pleased body, places you in the form of your nature, like a devoted servant, and fulfills your desires. When you desire fruit or flowers, then she appears in the form of fruit or flowers.

## Page 75

Mahamaya Ma
4
She approaches you. Hey! You do not recognize Me! You only recognize fruits and flowers! Who has come to your presence in the form of fruits and flowers? You do not see them with wisdom! You are only enchanted by their form! Whose name and form are these? Who is that one who manifests in countless names and forms, and you do not see Him? Do you try to throw away the great sages by calling them false, deluded, by putting their words in your throat? He is not false, not deluded, not a dream, not a study, not rigid, He is truth, He is Brahman, He is fearlessness, He is immortality, He is the soul, He is nothing else, He is Mahamaya Ma - I am.
Oh, religious people! You who have become known in the world by practicing a certain religion! Who is that? It is none other than Mahamaya Ma! Oh, non-religious people! Do you always act in the way indicated by someone else? Do you wander on the path of sin to please someone? Who, in the form of your condemned nature, wearing dirty clothes, has sat you in a corner? Think for a moment and see! It is none other than Mahamaya Ma. Violence, hatred - in the form of steadfastness, or compassion, forgiveness, generosity, in the form of sleep, thirst, laziness, or enthusiasm, determination, perseverance, in the form of indulgence in desires, or in the form of aversion to desires, in the form of earning wealth, maintaining a family, or in the form of japa, dhyana, yoga, worship, who is shining as your own self in front of you?
Look - in the form of your body-soul-intelligence! Look - in the form of a mind like a restless monkey! Look - in the form of a being who experiences happiness and sorrow! Look - in the form of a pure knower! Look - in the form of a devotee!
