## Page 77

They will be mere objects of power. They will be fortunate. We will be the sons of the sun. Infinite worlds will recognize us as the sons of Savarnā, the Prasavinī. We will be Manu and attain liberation. When pure consciousness shines through many states, then it is called Maya. Seated in the mother's lap, the child's form is how every element of the world is situated in the lap of Mahāmāyā. Suppose you see a tree; 'This is a tree' is a revealed insight. The part of that insight which says 'is' or existence is revealed. This existence, when combined with the special attribute of being a tree, becomes perceptible. A tree is merely a power. Although the tree is not experienced as power from the outside, some subtle minds, through a gazing spell, see that the tree, standing still, is not truly static; it is merely a flow of power. One power has bound the atoms with a firm resolve. Instantly, without any action, the atoms come out and new atoms come in. The flow of essence fills the branches, twigs, leaves, and flowers. Standing upright against the gravitational power of the Earth, many actions of power are within the tree; thus, some power flows are known as 'tree' by this name. These power flows are divided into three parts: one constructs the tree, another tries to keep it stable, and the third destroys it. The power of creation, sustenance, and destruction is the common name for the world or matter. In every element, at all times, only the combination of these three powers is revealed. It was previously said that existence, when combined with a special attribute, gains insight. This attribute is power, 'the world is' which is insight; it is the specific knowledge of the world; from this, the part 'world' and the part 'attribute' are separated.

## Page 78

60
Shakti Satya
Satya Ansh Is Not Suitable For Our Present Perception. Therefore, When The Perception Of The World's Existence Does Not Exist, The Perception Of Self-Existence, That Is, 'I Am', Also Does Not Remain. This Existence Or Being Is Always Located In The Intellect Of Shakti; That Is Why We Understand The World As An Existence And An Existence. This Part Is Always Shown Through The Gross Form Through Our Indriya Graha. The Common Name For This Part Is Name And Form. The Second Part, That Is, Existence, Is Not Unseen Even If It Is Not Perceived Through Gross Indriya. Shakti And Existence Are Actually Indivisible. The Existence Of Shakti Is Either The Existence Of Shakti Or The Existence Of Existence. There Is No Difference Between Shakti And Shaktiman. As Long As The Difference Is Perceived, It Is Seen That Shakti Is Holding Existence. This Shakti Is Not Stiff, It Is Only Chit Or Chaitanya. Its Name Is Mahamaya. Therefore, It Is Said Earlier That The World Of Life Is Only The Offspring Of Mahamaya. This Shakti Or Maya Is Not False, Not A Delusion - It Is Truth. It Is Not The Veil Of Brahman, But A Revealer. Swaprakasha, That Is, The Special Light Of Brahman, Is Shakti Or Maya. We Know That Maya Is Nothing But Saguna Brahman. When Mahamaya Is Not Shaken By The Vibration Of Importance, When She Is Adorned With Many States And Is Absorbed In The Light Of Shakti, She Is Established In Stability, Then She Is Described As Brahman Niranjana, Nirguna, Nirvikalpa, Etc. She Is Beyond Words And The Past Of Mind. As Long As The World Of Life Exists, As Long As It Is A Practice Of Worship, It Is Mahamaya. As Long As There Is A Benefit, It Is Shown In The Form Of Mahamaya. In This Mahamaya, The Willful Imagined Child Of Chaitanya Is The Life! From Akasha Paramanu To Hiranyagarbha, All Are Only The Offspring Of Mahamaya; Or Mahamaya Is The World Of Life In The Form Of Akasha, Nitya.

## Page 80

62
Experience
Form, taste, and sense objects, compassion, forgiveness, these are all experiences of Mahamaya. These feelings are born in the form of Mahamaya and dissolve into it. When our mother transitions from an unmanifest to a manifest state, then she appears in the form of feelings. Only when these feelings are abundant does this gross world manifest. The abundant state of feelings is gross. As long as Mahamaya remains in the form of experience, she remains only mental; she is perceived by the gross senses only when she becomes dense. Feelings are the atoms of Mahamaya, that is, her second form. Even when Mahamaya's unique form is near the living being in an unmanifest state, her form of feeling-like experience of Mahamaya appears to the living being in a moment. She is manifesting in the form of feelings near us in every moment. Feelings are my mother! Feelings always come to my mother in the form of feelings; if we understand this, we can experience true love for Mahamaya and become selfless. Seeker! The one you call feelings and then ignore by just imagining, is the form of feeling-like experience of my mother; if you can understand this, your path of practice will be easy; if you want to, you can advance in every feeling. Call feelings my mother, offer flowers at the feet of feelings, bow at the feet of feelings, feelings are not an object of neglect; this world is nothing but feelings and nothing else. At one place, there is a person named Chaitra sitting with his father, son, and a cobra friend. In front of him, a tiger is tied with a chain and sleeping. At this time, Chaitra's wife went there for some work. Seeing her, Chaitra's mind is filled with the feeling of his wife, his father's mind is filled with the feeling of his son, and the son's mind is filled with the feeling of his father.

---

## Page 81

The text describes the presence of various emotions in different states of being. In the state of welcome, there is a maternal feeling; in the state of death, there is a feeling of being a king; in the state of friendship, there is a desire; and in the state of a tiger, there is a feeling of eating. A woman form has inspired these diverse emotions. Reflect on this: is there any business here? Was this really a woman form? No, it is the welcoming feeling of each one. When a thorn pricks your foot, you feel pain. Where was that feeling? Was it in the thorn or in your inner self? Similarly, understand that you ate a mango. The sweet taste of the mango is a feeling or experience within you. Similarly, the world is everywhere. Whatever we enjoy day and night in the world is nothing but feelings. These feelings are located within us. There is nothing in the external world, and there is no way to know it. Everywhere, the ultimate state of existence is one. Its meaning or group of feelings continuously steps into our inner kingdom, then merges when the meaning is understood. This ultimate state is the great illusion, and whatever meaning or substance it has, it is a feeling; this is why it is called the experience of the great illusion. The great illusion, in the form of the embodiment of great power, the conscious supreme nature, leads us to the state of completeness and Brahman. It leads us to pure, unadulterated knowledge from the impure, subject knowledge, or ignorance. When the feeling that requires us to become emotional is understood in the mind, it reveals that kind of experience. When the feelings of sin, virtue, righteousness, unrighteousness, disease, grief, distress, suffering, laughter, and crying are favorable to us - when the feeling in which becoming emotional enhances our spiritual progress, and in which experiencing leads to the realization of the great illusion; then our mother is revealed by that feeling. Understanding it without any goal is better.

## Page 82

64
The Nature of Substance
It can be said that this feeling arises from one unmanifest field to another due to the will of some other unmanifest field, and then it merges into another unmanifest field. Its manifestation is called Tirobhava, meaning it is completely independent, i.e., it is situated far beyond the limits of the living, experiential knowledge. The one who is devoted to seeking the center of this feeling - who aims to experience the great illusion in the feeling itself - who calls the feeling Ma and runs behind it, that devotee can recognize Mahamaya as the son.

We see that in the world, many devotees, while meditating on their chosen deity, with full devotion establish their beloved god in their hearts. After advancing a little further, when they see that instead of the desired form, a universal feeling has emerged - instead of Shiva, a monkey has appeared. On the throne of Raj Rajeshwar, one of the world's dust, wealth, people, or the glory of Yash has come. Only then does the heart tremble, and after taking a long breath, the mind says, "Oh! Nothing has happened to us, sitting to meditate on Ma, what is the use of dust? Many feelings have come together and are weighing down the heart field. It is completely impossible for us to gain Bhagwan's grace. How can there be devotion to God from such a fickle mind! Devotion is only for the forest-dwelling, world-renouncing sages, great souls; it is not for us, ordinary householders like you."

But oh! If they could know that in that fickle form, in the form of the world's wealth and people, Mother has come - Mother is only in the feeling. If they could understand this - if they could see that the playful, mischievous, and joyous Mother, as long as she is engaged in the game of joy, will keep changing her form! Then there is no reason to feel frustrated. Oh, son, when you call her Ma, then there is no need to feel frustrated.

## Page 83

The Agony of Sadhana
When the son rises to establish his mother's feet on the throne of his heart, then there is no one in the infinite cosmos who can touch that throne. Son, by merely calling out to me, even Brahma, Vishnu, and Maheshvara, along with other divine beings, abandon their path. Then, the world's emotional waves are trivial. Apart from me, no one has the ability to touch my throne.
O seeker! If you focus on Bhagavat Chintamani and observe that the waves of worldly emotions arise in your cherished thoughts, then establish yourself in truth by aiming at those emotions. Do not try to eliminate each emotion; instead, recognize them as your beloved deity and honor them; offer salutations to them by calling out to me! By aiming at the feet of this emotional and changeable mother, sharpen your sword of sadhana. In the form of my mother, who is suddenly revealed as a humorous and stable form, your mind will become stable, you will attain me, and your life will become meaningful.
One more thing is said here: sadhana does not happen due to a restless mind - this statement is completely misleading. When the mind becomes stable, then the completion of sadhana occurs! Before attaining me, no one's mind can be stable, and it cannot be made stable. The mind becomes stable on its own at the sight of me, just as darkness disappears on its own at the sunrise. Before attaining any benefit, no one's mind can be made stable through any kind of forced action or through pranayama, etc. It is a special kind of jealousy - it is only a rigid samadhi. The mind becomes stable on its own when the light of true wisdom appears; no effort is needed. And if such wisdom is obtained before the mind becomes firm, that is, if the flow of vrittis is directed towards one subject,

---

## Page 84

The fickleness of the mind, when nourished by the mother's grace, produces great malevolence in the world for a long time. From the stimulation of desire and anger or the manifestation of sorrow and suffering, it can make a person a tyrant very quickly due to the fickleness of the mind; but in the ordinary state, when the mind remains stable, what kind of misfortune befalls a person from its disturbances? Consider this once. Only then is it said that the fickleness of the mind is the mother's grace.
Whether it is something or the experience of Mahamaya or Mahamaya in the form of experience, she brings humans to the essence of humanity. Then the seeker, establishing himself in this human body, speaks from the lips of the sages of the Upanishads, "I am a human, I have become a human, I have become the sun." Do not think that this is just a play of words. Do not think that this is just a high-flown language. This is absolutely true - it is absolutely suitable for a human. In the kingdom of the heart, day and night, the emotions rise one after another in sequence, without thinking of good or bad, without thinking of small or big, call each emotion "mother". Where do these emotions meet? Go to that place and seek the feet of the mother in the form of that emotion. Cry out in intense grief, be pacified by the consoling. You will be unsuccessful again and again, you will fail again and again; but do not give up hope by crying out in intense prayer. The emotions will leave you; but do not be disappointed by failure. Repeated failure will lead you to success. If you continue to do this for a few days, you will see that you are always in the lap of Mahamaya. The mother in the form of emotion will take you to the field beyond emotions.

## Page 85

Mahabhag
Tirobhav is, and that is its place. Oh, Jeev! When will you be blessed by directly experiencing the motherly form of Mahamaya in the state of Udasi, beyond Prakriti, beyond the mind, in the full light of Shanti? But that is another matter. Now, we will briefly discuss the profound meaning of the Mantra that says from Brahma to the atomic particles of Akasha, everywhere is the light of Mahamaya. Sanchidanandamayi Mahamaya is never absent anywhere. By relying on the experience of Her, that is, by understanding the worldly emotions as Mahamaya, Jeev can gain the sovereignty of Manvantar. He becomes capable of establishing himself in a pure state of Bodh. Then, in the garden of that varnini Bhargashakti, he experiences himself as Atmaj. Who else can have such good fortune? This is why the word Mahabhag is mentioned in the Mantra (Mahabhag vee yasya sah iti Mahabhag). Then he becomes Anantavi and Amrita Parakum. He becomes Ashtam, that is, Asht Siddhi Shivar and Asht Pash Vimukta, and attains Bhagwat Sarupya. The wisdom of the entire human mandali follows his sign. In this way, from the beginning of Chandi, our mother Mahaphal gives us the news - she increases the strength of her sons to enter the Chandi element, and with pure love, she attracts them. Those who will fall within the limits of this attraction will be blessed. Even without desire, they will gradually move towards the mother's garden. Many times, even when we do good work out of desirelessness in the world, by falling within the limits of this mother's attraction, the same kind of desireless movement towards motherly progress begins. When a human being experiences this progress with a soft-soft feeling, then and then only does his ignorance and darkness disappear. Nitya

## Page 87

When Susumna awakens, he sees that his inner kingdom is peaceful, illuminated by a pure white light. Not just the inner realm, but the entire universe merges into that light. The various emotions of the world meet in that maternal light. Living in that all-encompassing, multidimensional light, the soul experiences the supreme blissful being. The only simple, unchanging path to such an experience is truthfulness. The one who, with the help of the guru's instructions and intellectual effort, has practiced the vision of the mother everywhere, soon realizes their inner peace.

Yoga scriptures call this the awakening of the Susumna channel, Tantra calls it the awakening of Kundalini, Patanjali calls it the Vishoka or Light of Wisdom, and Vedanta calls it the Chidakasha. Each of these statements is true. Yogis achieve this Chidakasha through severe yoga practices, ascetics through severe renunciation vows, and tantrics through a kind of conceptual exercise, awakening Kundalini. Rajayogi, by practicing asanas, pranayama, and other disciplines, transforms their life, achieving this inner peace effortlessly and without effort. No severity is required for this; no need for strict discipline, no need for the difficult path of renunciation, no need for the deep search of a sage, no need for any kind of skill or art. Only the simple practice of faith in the great glory of the sages of the Vedic age is needed.

## Page 89

Does the soul within us attain the state of being self-luminous? Gaining the ability to see this inner dimension clears many lifetimes of accumulated ignorance from humans. This ignorance is the perception of the difference between inner and outer. Normally, when we speak of the inner, it extends to the body and encompasses the visible world; this is one form of self-ignorance. In reality, there is no difference between inner and outer; rather, "all is inner" can be said. The world we experience is merely our inner. That distant sky, that light, that sun and moon, that vast ocean, those high mountains, all are merely our inner. Wealth, family, danger, children—all are merely our inner. This physical body made of flesh and blood is our inner. Oh! How great I am! How big I am! How vast is my reach! A million salutations to my mother's feet! Try to understand this further with clear understanding; the body is not within the mind—it is within the mind. Some part of the mind has condensed into this physical form. Just as some part of water freezes into ice, it is the same. It is also said in the science of perception that within the mind-made sheath is the life-sheath, and within that is the food-made sheath or physical body. Just reading it and memorizing it does not yield any special fruit; understanding, experiencing, and realizing are required. Then ignorance is cleared, peace comes to the vital forces, and the taste of immortality is experienced. In the explanation of the previous mantra, it was said that feeling is the world. What we perceive and experience through the senses day and night is all merely feeling. Feeling is the duty of the mind. Therefore, the visible world is merely our mind or inner. Seeing a flower, it is not actually outside; it is within you.

## Page 90

32
The mind is atomic.
The mind has taken the form of a flower, from which your business of seeing flowers has been organized. Understand this everywhere. Call them wife, son, wealth, jewel, or the distant moon and sun; they are all within you or your mind. This is also said in Vedanta philosophy. Knowledge of objects is achieved through the unity of subjectivity, non-dual consciousness of the senses, non-dual consciousness of the internal organs, and non-dual consciousness of the observer. However, we will not make the object hard within the philosophical language. But those who have studied law may have a doubt in their mind. They say that the mind is atomic in size. If the entire world is the mind, then its atomicity is not proven. The statement is correct, "Of all knowledge, that which is atomic is here." - One cannot hold two types of knowledge at the same time, which is why the mind is called atomic. In reality, even if the mind is atomic, it is also great. Even in atomic size, its vastness is proven by the extent of its influence in all scriptures and direct experience. There is no doubt about the validity of the subject in direct experience. "What we see, what we perceive through the senses, and what we understand by describing it externally, is actually within our own inner world. We wander in our own inner kingdom day and night." Until this kind of experience shines, the fear of death for living beings does not disappear. Seekers of truth should try to understand this world as the inner world of Ma. By holding this kind of understanding, the first step is to remember the 'I' of the living being, from which this kind of illusion arises. Therefore, in the realm of practice, "I give up the word everywhere, I act as Ma, or as God, without any name." This makes the path of practice easy.

The Creation of the World

It is said in the scriptures: "As it was previously created - this world is merely a creation of Ma. The sun is not the sun, it is merely a feeling of Ma's mind; Ma understands that I am the sun. The moon is not the moon; Ma understands that I am the moon. The tree is not a tree; Ma understands that I am the tree. The earth is not the earth; Ma understands that I am the earth. The wind is not the wind; Ma understands that I am the wind. The fire is not the fire; Ma understands that I am the fire. The son is not the son; Ma understands that I am the son. Understand in this way everywhere. The world is the feeling or mind of Ma. Our feelings are very fleeting and temporary, but Ma's mind is infinite and of infinite value! Therefore, her feelings are so dense and so long-lasting that we cannot comprehend them by adding more feelings. In reality, we are born within Ma's mind, and we wander within Ma's mind, and then we die within Ma's mind. We are all situated within Ma's mind in all conditions. Just as entering a well-kept house, seeing various kinds of objects, one sees the appearance of a single house; similarly, in this world, seeing countless differences, countless names and forms, it should be understood that all are like a single house within Ma's mind. This understanding leads to great wisdom, the wisdom of differences gradually moves towards unity, and the correct understanding of the thing called 'difference' is obtained. The previous statement about the experience of Mahamaya is relative to this knowledge of the inner world.

## Page 92

Seeker and Sought
He is the abode of the world; therefore, he is the abode. We are within the Mother; therefore, we are all devoted to her. She is the abode - the only abode, the ultimate abode. Thus, the relationship of abode and devotion is the main support for the path of Sadhana. We often think that without God, we cannot see Mother; but even without God, all our work continues. This is merely our ignorance. If we think that the loss of a tree will cause us what harm, if we think that the loss of air will cause us what harm, if we think that the loss of water will cause us what harm, if we think that the loss of body will cause us what harm, then this kind of thinking, rooted in ignorance, is established. In the same way, those who consider their existence superior by abandoning God are called ignorant children. When the distinction between inner and outer disappears, when we realize that our own inner light is everywhere, then this knowledge of abode and devotion becomes inevitable.
Indeed, when the inner world is illuminated by the divine light of Mother's beautiful form, then the inner becomes the self-luminous, and then the life becomes the king of the entire universe. Surath is in this way the self-luminous soul of the special seeker. The Katha Upanishad says: "Know the self as the charioteer and the body as the chariot." The self is the charioteer; and the body is the chariot. When this body-chariot is adorned with beautiful feelings, then the life is called Surath. Until this self-luminosity is not achieved, until this divine light is not illuminated in the heart; until the life does not hold the world-mother in the heart with faith.

## Page 93

Born of the Chaturvanshi dynasty; until the heart of the ignorant beings is illuminated by the radiant light of the knowledge of full existence, life cannot be a journey, and without being a journey, the hope of becoming a human being does not remain. Describing the feeling with which I, with my love, bring my children, the beings, into this journey form, the sage Markandeya mentions - "Chaturvanshi Samudbhava" (Chitrani Chaitra). Wandering in countless forms - from the inert atom to the flower, tree, insect, bird, animal, wild, semi-civilized, and countless other forms, the being becomes a journey, becomes a human being. Holding my children, the beings, in my loving embrace, I bring them into this world of countless forms and countless lineages, and when I present the best lineage in the human race, when the human being comes close to perfect knowledge, when, after countless births and deaths, after repeatedly being hit by the blows of death and the threefold suffering, he gains faith in the existence of the mother; when the honest inquiry about the ultimate solution to the threefold suffering and the ultimate solution begins, then only does the being become a journey. In transition, as long as the being cannot have faith in the divine existence - as long as he cannot understand this incarnation of the world as Mahamaya, his body remains a mere chariot; he does not become a journey. O Man! Once look towards your past life. See on which day you had the desire to act out the role of a sudra from the desire for the first taste of happiness, on which day you became greedy for the happiness of multiplicity from the joy of infinite unity, on that day - at that moment, Mother Mahamaya, residing in the form of your nature, brought you into her embrace, made you play various kinds of games in countless forms, and presented you in the best lineage of human beings.

## Page 94

Matr-sneha
You will become a charioteer, your body will become a vehicle of all senses, full of harmony, the foundation of knowledge. Like a mad elephant, you are being dragged by me. Until you progressed only through the activities of the lower species (insects, birds, snakes, etc.), I could not recognize you. Now, I have embraced you with both my active and inactive hands, your body has been purified, the Annamaya Kosha has been completed, you have become a charioteer. He who brought us to the full field of knowledge from the atomic to the human race, who revealed our inner world, whose divine light has illuminated our heart kingdom; in our pride of being the doer, not even a dot of flaw has appeared. Therefore, he who completes all our actions with his own hands, still hides his own doership from us; he who shines with boundless love from the heart, but becomes invisible to us from the love of him. Oh! For a day, we cannot call her Ma! For a day, we cannot experience her simple, heartfelt love, her respect, her experience! He who is with us in birth and death, who is the companion of our joys and sorrows, who is the unique companion of our infinite journey, who is merely a charioteer of our body, from whose auspicious wish we have become human, a charioteer, we cannot leave any gratitude towards the loving Mahamaya Ma! Our human life is a reminder! It is a reminder of our gratitude!

## Page 95

Earth Mandala
द
The light of gratitude has remained far away. Without it, there is nothing else, whose existence is our existence, and in whose existence we cannot have perfect faith today! We have not even seen its presence in our simple life! Alas! How much respect and love Mother has for us! She knows who she is, she does not want to change her unparalleled love. Her work of love is milk donation. She always does it and will always do it. We are ungrateful children, yet she cannot look at us with a disdainful eye, but she always nourishes us with the life-giving stream of immortal love and will continue to do so. Alas! Can this love be expressed through words? But that is another matter. When life is born in the Chaitra dynasty, that is, after wandering through many unusual wombs and many lineages, it incarnates in the human race, when it is illuminated by the intercontinental divine light - illuminated by the pure light of knowledge - then life becomes Suratha, and with the becoming of Suratha, it becomes the ruler of the entire Earth Mandala. The term Earth Mandala refers to earthly elements. With the becoming of Suratha, the ability to rule over earthly elements is born. The Annamaya Kosha or the gross body becomes the field suitable for the expansion of infinite knowledge. All senses become harmonious, the center of intellectual development develops, the distinction between gross and subtle becomes perceptible - the most important thing is that faith in God arises. Here, an indication of Sadhana is given - the meaning of the term Earth Mandala is the Muladhara Chakra. It is situated within the quadrilateral surrounded by the Ashtashula, outside the Tripurakshetra, which is of a deep red color. This is the ultimate manifestation of unmanifest Prakriti. Its essence is smell. In the region of form, its presence is felt.

## Page 96

Prajapalana

The enemies of the king, Kolavidhwan, were destroyed. ||4||
Translation - He destroyed the people like his own sons. But they themselves became his enemies, gained independence, and rose up to destroy the city of Kolā.
Explanation - "By attraction, they are born, they manifest, or they are called Praja." The word Praja means Vritti or Bhava. By wandering in various wombs, when the life becomes Suratha, when the earthy body or gross substances gain authority, in other words, when the life reaches the ultimate limit of experiential 'I' knowledge, then it takes care of all those mental Vrittis or Bhavas by understanding them as self-born sons. Whatever kinds of Bhavas arise within or outside, "they are all our Bhavas, they are all self-born, they are all born from us, therefore nurturing them is an absolute duty." From this sense of duty, one rises to action, from the firm hands of ego, and nurtures the Bhavas.

## Page 101

Defeat in Battle
Explanation - When a being sees their limbs bound by the chains of emotions with full intensity, they become extremely agitated in their attempt to gain freedom. How can one escape from the excess of emotions? This thought arises from a powerful emotion. Then, they attempt to depict the strength of both sides. First, by looking at their own strength, they consider themselves to be a very powerful disciplinarian. Because the crowd or emotions are born from our own desire, they are fulfilled by our own efforts, and they are empowered by our own strength! If I stand against them, if I impose a harsh punishment on them, that is, remove emotions from the mind field, or completely destroy them by denying them the opportunity for emotional development, then with a little effort, my desired goal can be achieved. After that, by seeing the strength of the other side, it is known that the emotional side is weaker - it is weak; because they have received their existence from our existence, they act in the way we want, and if we do not give them such an opportunity, then where is the strength of the emotional side? In this way, when the strength of both sides is considered, and when the self-side is seen as powerful and the opposing side as weak, then the effort to engage in a great battle remains. Many types of yoga, physical exercises, pranayama, jyotirdharana, nirāmish food, renunciation of household life, and dependence on sannyas are undertaken to eliminate emotions. Mostly, all of it is in vain. Alas! Even the foolish (mohit) being cannot understand that the ocean of emotions is the experience of Mahamaya. Victory is not achieved in this fierce battle of emotions without the grace of Mahamaya. I too, once, by planning in this way, emerged victorious in the battle of emotions and was determined to establish the sovereignty of the self. Many efforts were made.

One day, at night, when I went to sleep, what did I see? A new beautiful dream, my mind was in it, at that time some deep thoughts about my bond came. Thinking about the past, future, and present of life, I fell asleep or became drowsy, or I was awake, it was not clear. Then suddenly, what did I see? In front of me stood an unprecedented radiant motherly figure, standing with a gentle smile on her face. She laughed and said to me - "Look, how are you tied up?" Her laughter and melodious voice, even though it was full of love and kindness, was in the form of a joke. I saw that - truly, I am tied up with strong emotions, how terrifying is this bond. Both feet, both hands, both arms, chest, neck, both legs, and head, each part is tied up with strong ropes from different emotions. Not only this, the end of each rope is tied to a strong nail and fixed in the ground. I have no way to move, I do not have the strength to move even a single part of my body. I am tied up by such emotions. - In such a situation, I do not feel any fear or hardship - rather, I laugh a little because in front of me is CCO. Vasishtha Tripathi Collection. ## Page 103

Dream Vision

Break it and see! If I do not break it, then you will not be able to do anything.' I then said, 'What more is there to say. Look, all the bonds are breaking now.' Saying this, I was ready to use strength, but at that moment, my throat stopped, my breath was held. The bonds became even more difficult, the pain became unstable from an invisible pain; a great fear arose. The light of the lamp became so bright that the sleep of the revered mother who was sleeping in the nearby house was disturbed. She came running and caught me. Then, the goddess form laughed and laughed and became invisible, not even the sign of the bond remained; but from fear and pain, my throat was parched. I fell into a deep sleep for a long time.

## Page 108

60
Swapuragaman
The meaning of the word "Hriday" is "that which is in the heart." The heart is the special place of the soul's experience. This heart is the Swapur of the Surath. Previously, he expanded his kingdom from here, and in sequence, he crossed Manipur and Swadhishthan, reaching the Chitmandal - Muladhara, all the way to the base. By nature, the mind of the living being wanders in these three centers. When the full development of the living being's nature occurs in these places, then Bhavavidroha (the destruction of emotions) appears due to the grace of Mahamaya. Overwhelmed in the battle of emotions, the living being then advances towards his place. Gradually defeated everywhere, he then takes refuge in his Swapur (his own city). Due to the fruits of many good deeds - due to the boundless grace of Ma - the living being comes to know this Swapur (his own city). Normally, the living being forgets what "Sw" is, and he is lost. The world's fascination with the game of the importance of pleasure takes the living being far away from his Swapur. The world's cultural series (lines) are like the attacks of dacoits - when they capture everything, when the power of the soul, the kingdom of the soul, the remembrance of the soul is forgotten, then the living being becomes diligent to revive that lost memory. Then, once, to save himself from the excesses of emotions, he takes refuge in his Swapur - he makes an attempt to remember "Who am I" once. In the world, too, we see that - repeatedly seeing the fruitlessness of good deeds, being repeatedly broken by hope and the pain of unforeseen events, being troubled by the thorns of birth and death, the living being becomes a devotee of Bhagwan - he becomes a believer in God. Being a believer in God and searching for oneself is the same thing. Only when the remembrance of the soul is awakened does the living being become determined to enter his Swapur.

## Page 109

The Difficulty of Sadhana
61
It is difficult to regain the great form of Kali. To avoid the attack from the series of worldly impressions, even if one is far away, if they cannot become established, the life climbs up to the extremely low rung of hopelessness (or despair). Alas! How many sadhus have fallen on their knees in this field, how many have been enveloped in the terrible darkness of despair, and how many have declared that "attaining Bhagwan" is a very bad business upon reaching here. Most sadhus have not been successful in establishing their chosen deity in their heart with the help of meditation due to the fickleness of the mind. Those who have been successful in this have also lost the great desire and deep respect for the divine form and become hopeless. Those who try to become established in their own form by transcending the illusion of a specific form also become engrossed in worldly matters for a moment when they immerse themselves in the great ocean of consciousness beyond the light of calm prana. Those who gain the ability to enter that ocean of consciousness also get distracted from meditation. In this way, the story of the misconduct of the emotional and the masses is heard from the mouths of the sadhus. To remove this misconduct and this emotional fickleness, many types of arrangements and undertakings are prescribed. Abstaining from worldly activities, physical yoga, pranayama, and other methods are mentioned in various scriptures. Different groups of sadhus, up to this point, have taken refuge in their own mind, but when they are tormented by the series of impressions, they cannot abandon their Kosha. CO. Vasishtha Tripath

Listen, "Akrantah sa mahabhagah" - because he (Surath) is extremely fortunate. That is why there is an attack by an enemy even in his own kingdom. In the same way, the enemy's attack on the self in one's own kingdom makes the living being extremely fortunate. Only the seeker is destined to be attacked in this way by the Bhavaraashi, and this attack itself is a sign of good fortune. The sage did not announce Surath as unfortunate, but if seen from a normal perspective - Surath is extremely unlucky; because he is tormented by the excesses of the kingdom's enemies; he cannot find a way to remain healthy even in his own kingdom; there is also an unusual attack by the offspring protected by his wife. Compared to this, what kind of misfortune can there be? But even so, Surath was called "mahabhagah" (extremely fortunate).

## Page 111

The state of a Sadhaka only comes to this extent. Some become Bhagwat Mukhi, and due to the genuine search for the Mother in their Prana, some of the feelings of devotion are lost. From all around, various kinds of upadras (obstacles) appear. Diseases, sorrow, poverty, separation from loved ones, and other events come and make the Sadhaka unstable. Overcoming those instabilities, slowly and gradually, as the Sadhaka tastes the bliss of Bhagwatras, another strange problem arises: on one hand, the world no longer seems good to him; it feels as if the world has been drugged with a strong medicine. Because of this, there is an intense desire to reject the pleasures of the world; yet, on the other hand, the path of Bhagwat Mukhi also does not come easily to the mind. On one side, he does not find the Mother, and on the other side, the world also does not seem good to him. In this way, the Sadhaka's self is torn into pieces from both sides. In such a field, the mantra of the desperate hearted Sadhaka mentions the word "Mahabhag" (great fortune) for the transmission of full courage and hope.

Those who have become Mother Mukhi, who have set the gain of the Mother as the only purpose of life, do not become hopeless in this problem-filled field. I have sent you the opposite feeling - a group of emotions - because you are Mahasoubhagyavan (extremely fortunate). And another thing is that to stop that attack, apart from holding the Mother's feet with a firm heart, do not weaken your spiritual path by any means. Obstacles, hindrances, and severe sufferings will come; they will do their own work. The rebellious people will rebel; they will not show me their backs. You, holding a sword in your hand, have risen in the battle to stop the vow.

The opponent of the Mantrivarag

अमात्यैर्वेलिभिदुष्टै दुर्बलस्य दुरात्मभिः ।
कोपोवलं चापहृतं तत्रापि स्वपुरे ततः ॥७॥
Translation: And in that Svapura, the powerful, wicked, and evil natured Mantrivarag had already seized the treasury (khaana) of the weak Suratha, and taken away his strength.
Explanation: When the life is defeated in the battle of emotions and takes refuge in Svapura, when all the impressions of the world are drowned in the deep waters of forgetfulness and enters the best ancient scripture, the ultimate pad in all the caves, then even there it sees that it cannot cross the bounds of bad conduct. Even there, powerful opposing ministers are present. Who are these Mantrivarag? They are the group of scholarly orders. By following the rules and prohibitions, and by performing the rituals of their own declared dharma, the life finds the path to the heart cave; these are the ritualistic duties that are the powerful path to the life's attainment of self-realization.

## Page 114

66
Maa is the ultimate obstacle to Dharma. After many births, the observance of Japa, Puja, Vrat, Upavasa, and other instructions mentioned in the scriptures leads to the manifestation of Bhavavidroha. The search for the Atma in the form of Maa becomes acute due to the fruits of severe tapasya and rigorous Yoga practice. How many can know the number of sages who, during the time of establishing a Dharma Rajya, act as supportive and advisory ministers like slow-moving ministers who are favorable to the Dharma Rajya, protecting the life of the living being from the path of Adharma and consecrating them in the nectar of peace, thereby establishing a prosperous Dharma Rajya and gaining a pure character and a spotless reputation? Overwhelmed by Bhavavidroha, they seek refuge in their Svapura and, at the time of establishing their Rajya, they see Suratha as their powerful enemy. Those who were previously virtuous, upon entering their Svapura, become aware that they are also evil and wicked. In reality, to prevent the life from going astray and guide it towards the path of Dharma, valid actions like removing a thorn with another thorn are the main helpers. While observing the prohibitions of the scriptures, a strong desire for maternal and paternal love awakens in the human being. Maa is beyond our Dharma, beyond Adharma, beyond Karma; she is an indestructible, blissful, and unique entity. A being understands this only after observing the scriptures for many days. If one sees a person who, despite not following valid actions, is a true seeker of the mother, then understand that in previous births, their Karma-Kanda Sadhana has been completed. First, a Dharma Rajya, then an Atma Rajya. First, Dharma, then Maa. That is why Dharma is considered a step towards Moksha. A being who is free from desires, complete, and blissful.

The great saying of Bhagwatnitya, "Ishwarah sarvabhootanam hridaydeshe arjuna tishthati" "Tameva sharanam gachch sarva bhavena Bharat," this peaceful and fearless speech will inevitably be heard in the life of life. But when life enters the self, it sees that the samskaras - born from vaidhik karma - are tightly bound with a strong resolve. They are very powerful. Removing the samskaras of adharma is not as difficult as removing the samskaras of shastra vidhi; life suffers much more difficulty in removing them. The samskara of drinking alcohol, which is removed quickly, is easier to remove than the samskara of a dedicated brahmin performing sandhya prayers. Similarly, compared to the samskara of adharma, the samskara of dharma is a powerful and fierce enemy, and its movement is very high. But one day comes, the flow of maternal compassion comes, which makes these powerful samskaras like a straw in a strong flood. That very day - that is the auspicious day of life, that very day, in the melody of Rama Prasad's song, the sadhak says - "Dharma-dharma do bakriyaan tuchch khunte se band raklo, yadi na mane (ore man) jnana khadg se bali de di."

## Page 117

Golokadhama
εε
Sadhana, a certain practice, continues until one attains a special realization within the Anandamaya Kosha. The Sadhana of taking Atmabodh to the Anandamaya Kosha is itself Sadhana. The Atmabodh that is abundant in the Annamaya, Pranamaya, and other gross Koshas must be withdrawn and taken to the Anandamaya Kosha; this is the end of Sadhana. From the fall from the Sutrapata of Sadhana, the Atmabodh of the Jiva begins to be withdrawn from the Annamaya Kosha or the gross body. Sequentially, it transcends the Pranamaya, Manomaya, and Vijñānamaya Koshas, reaching the Anandamaya Kosha. Upon reaching the realization, "I am the eternal blissful Mahāchaitanya, my very form," Sadhana comes to an end. This is where one can reach through Upasana. It is also known as Hiranyagarbha, Parameshvara, and other names. This is the Akṣar Puruṣa, where the seed of the world's creation is present, "like the seed of a tree in a kernel." Reaching this place, the Jagadveja or the creation of the world cannot bind the Jiva. He attains a glimpse of eternal freedom. Just as Parameshvara remains unbound even with the creation of countless worlds, and remains eternally free even in the work of dissolution, similarly, the Jiva remains unbound when he is situated in the Anandamaya Kosha; the world becomes his mere play. In this state, the taste of eternal bliss is always experienced. This is where the Jiva is worthy of being attained through Sadhana - this is the Jiva's true abode of peace. The eternal Rāsmandal or Golokadhama of the Vaishnava scriptures is this place. Beyond this, those who are situated are described as "unseen, unperceived, and beyond the mind," referring to the past beyond the mind, the past beyond the mind, and the past beyond Sadhana, being only self-realized. By ascending to the Anandamaya Kosha, one can reach the state beyond this. It is a special state of its own. (Even the term 'state' does not fully describe it. CCO. Vasishtha Tripathi Collection. Digitized By Siddhanta eGangotri Gyaan Kosha)

The signs of the destruction of the people or the destruction of the mind due to the conflict of emotions appear in the scientific knowledge kosha or the destruction of the ministers due to the impressions of dharma-karma in the joy-may kosha. The higher the knowledge or the right, the higher the level of sadness. A child cries when his kite breaks; but a knowledgeable person is not disturbed by the death of an appropriate son; what should we understand from this? That he does not suffer? When alone, climbing up a scary mountain and running away, or when Surath's extreme sadness informs us, we see that it is a special kind of sadhana; the external sign of sadness is not just that. Then, with love and respect for our mother, she leads her son on a simple path. Until then, she did not want him to want anything, if he wanted, he would want discipline, if he wanted, he would want yoga meditation, if he wanted, he would want the power of achievement; that is why until now he has not seen the simple path from the perspective of the seers. With a similar mistake, after many unsuccessful attempts, the true search for the mother has developed in his life; from this, at this time, the journey of disappearance into the forest of objects and the search for the mother has begun.

The objects are insignificant, our Ma is infinite; the objects are a dense state of emotions, our Ma is beyond emotions. The objects are mere ignorance; our Ma is full of knowledge. Therefore, is there any chance of finding Ma, who is extremely contradictory to the objects, by thinking about the objects? But what is done! When the immortal is not found in the inner kingdom, then what harm is there in searching from object to object in that kingdom? Therefore, pretending and deceiving, with the help of the senses, start searching for Ma from object to object; then after some days, we see that this is not a trick, it is a real search. Then it is said here that the deer is self-inquiry. This is external inquiry.

## Page 125

True Establishment
107
Even when it is displayed in this form, the real benefit of the Self is revealed here. Because, from the realization of the mother in gross objects, the actual attainment of the mother begins. Through contemplation on objects through the senses, how is the search for the mother or the attainment of the mother organized? Whatever thing is present before you - whether it is a horse in the form of a sense, or if someone brings any object before you, you should call it 'mother' and accept it as true. If someone presents a form before your eyes, you should perceive it as the form of the mother. If someone is near your ears, you should perceive it as the mother's invitation or as the mother's voice. If someone brings you near a fragrance, you should perceive it as the fragrance that has come out of the mother's body. If someone brings you near a taste, you should say 'raso vai sah' and experience the nectar of maternal taste. If someone touches you with a soft touch, you should be delighted by the mother's affectionate touch. In this way, whatever you do from one sunrise to the next, consider it as an offering to the mother. Understand this. "Whatever I do, I worship the mother of the world." Experience this from your own experience. Merely saying it with your mouth will not bring real results. Fight against your emotions, against your objects, against your impressions, be defeated in battle, be destroyed; now walk in harmony, and still be repeatedly enlightened by the mother's awareness within them. For many days, many births, many ages, the practice of worldliness has been going on, walking in worldliness, being liberated in worldliness; for this reason, enjoy the world; but understand the mother. Whatever you see, whatever you do, whatever you understand, all of these are different forms of the great illusion.

Establish the truth everywhere with the help of intelligence - have faith in the omnipresent motherly nature, then see what sweet results it brings. **Satya Darshan**
**106**
Oh Mother, how you wish to see me - in whichever form you desire to see me, let your life be dedicated to that form of mine, and think in your mind that Mother is present in that form in front of you. Then, as you treat me, treat every inert object in the same manner. Like the boy of the gadi (a type of cart), say falsely, "Mother! I have found you," "Mother! I have caught you." Saying this, catch the tree, the earth, the stone, and try to attain the same sense of self-surrender from them. Without thinking, offer your heart's desire to the Mother face-to-face. In this way, begin with false devotion, false motherly love, false attachment. Then, soon you will reach true devotion. If the desire for true Mother is in your life, then do not rely on thought, discussion, scientific reasoning, etc., but rely on this path through simple life. From this moment, begin to gain Mother through this feeling. Look at Mother in the form of the world - enjoy Mother in the form of the world; then you will know Mother beyond the world. Leave the world - leave the body - leave the mind, and the day of understanding Mother is far away. First, catch Mother in the gross - in the visible form, then in the subtle - in the field of the unmanifest, you will become absorbed. See, God is not difficult to obtain, but we are difficult. Because we do not want Him. In the world, a man puts in as much effort to obtain wealth or any other object, but there is no need for such effort to obtain God; He is that near and that visible. If there is anything most easily obtainable, it is Mother. It is obtained without any effort. Those who say that without hard yoga, meditation, renunciation, etc., one cannot see or gain Him, those people are wrong. They have read books.

## Page 129

The Mystery of Buddhiyoga
111
The mind is; therefore, in every element of the world, the vision of Brahman is essentially a worship of the mind. Indeed, we do not wish to elaborate further with many proofs and arguments. For the thirsty seekers, the need for proof and argument does not exist.
Union with God through the intellect is called Buddhiyoga. Compared to our other elements, intellect is the most subtle and pure. Intellect or greatness is where the first light of consciousness appears; therefore, the more easily one is united with God through the intellect, the less easily is the mind or senses united; because they are much coarser and more rigid in nature than intellect. The meeting of two elements with similar properties can easily be achieved in simplicity, but two elements with different properties cannot be easily united in simplicity. As much effort is needed to mix water and earth, mixing water with water requires much less effort. As much labor is needed to mix air with water, mixing air with air can be achieved with much greater ease. Similarly, the union of space with space requires no effort at all. Just like this, the union of the self with the intellect is achieved with minimal effort. The self - our mother - is subtle even from the subtle; therefore, to unite with it, our most subtle part will be the one to unite. First, if the mind or senses unite with the mother, the desire of the mind will not be fulfilled; because the mother is stable and unchanging, and the mind is extremely restless and full of desires. But first, it is very easy to unite with the mother through stable intellect. Similarly, in Buddhiyoga, practice occurs.

Just by seeing it, the practitioner's life force is immersed in the nectar of immortality, their restless mind becomes stable, and they become bewildered in that true light. The long-standing anguish in the heart is hidden in that moment. Initially, it becomes a transient, unstable feeling of consciousness, then, as it becomes accustomed to the truth, it becomes stable, white, pure, and long-lasting. It is seen just by the will. At that time, the practitioner gains great joy. Like a drunkard who drinks wine out of greed, he advances with a restless desire. Forgetting the entire world, he is in a natural state, thinking only of that thing. He realizes this. Gradually, with the grace of Ma, the sky, painted in many colors, attracts the practitioner's mind. Sometimes it shines with a bright red color, sometimes with a deep red, through the eyes. Gradually, the light becomes extremely bright, calm, and pure, gaining infinite expansion and covering the world with its existence. This is the vision of the Medhas Ashram between the forest and the Surath. The meaning of the word Medhas is cloud or memory power. That which wakes up the soul's memory is called Medhas. Ascending in wisdom, that is, when the wisdom light is ignited - the soul's memory is present; that is why it is called the place of wisdom. The meaning of the word Dwijvarjya is the best Brahmin. Brahmin, Kshatriya, and Vaishya,

When this intelligence first dawns, it appears in the form of memory. Therefore, here, instead of calling it wisdom or intelligence, it is called medha. The first manifestation of Brahman or the formless consciousness occurs in wisdom. Only when the living being can comprehend the self within this wisdom-filled field can he know his Brahman form. That is why intelligence is called a Brahmin. The Brahmin caste of the world also acquires this ghee power and is revered. Brahmins pray to this ghee power daily through the Gayatri Mantra, and gradually sow the seed of Brahma knowledge in the hearts of all beings, attracting the living being towards Mahasatyam. That is why Brahmins are so revered; that is why the Brahmin position is adorned on the chest of the Kaustubha-marked Vishnu. Brahmin children are naked in the mother's lap. The Brahmin's vow is to bring good fortune to the world. How high is the seat of a Brahmin, what benefit do Brahmins bring to us, we cannot comprehend. Brahman is unperceivable and unreachable; but Brahmins are eternal abodes. The Brahmin form is the central pivot, and because of this, the living being's creation cycle remains stable; otherwise, like the great beings who fall from their delusion path, they would become invisible. When the sages have given the seat of the devotees higher than even Bhagwan, it is not mere praise. Bhagwan always shines in the hearts of the devotees. The devotee is the manifest form of Bhagwan. When the devotee appears, Bhagwan appears. The devotee is the Brahmin, or the Brahmin is the devotee. Brahma knowledge and para-bhakti or love are one and the same. The Brahmin, that is, the Brahma-jnani man, is indeed a devotee or a lover. But there is another matter:

## Page 135

The Tranquil Breath Pad
1170
We will understand Medha as the abode or the field of wisdom. This very place is the open door to Brahman knowledge. The seeker can be present in this field as soon as the Suṣumnā flow is illuminated. In the language of Sāṁkhya philosophy, this place is called Mahat. The attainment of direct experience of this Mahat makes the veil of ignorance of the living being completely slack. This is also the sign of Kundalini awakening in tantric practitioners.
This Medha ashram has two special characteristics; one is 'Prashāntaśvāpadākī' and the other is 'Muniśiṣyaupashobhita'. In that place, hunting animals live peacefully with each other, forgetting their enmity and hatred. Deer, birds, serpents, and other creatures leave their natural animosity and live with friendly feelings. In all four directions, the silent students are immersed in Brahman meditation. When the students' silence is broken, they start reciting great hymns. Sometimes, their offerings are thrown into the fire, and the fragrance of sacred incense spreads everywhere, even reaching the pure Satvic feeling in the lives of people sitting far away. Oh! Can we ever see such an ashram! Where, upon entering, even heaven becomes insignificant! Where every tree is filled with the life of truth! Where the very soil is always alive with the sensation of truth! Where the air only carries the truth of feelings! Where the sky is always agitated by the truth of the truth's vibration! Can we ever see the ashram of such sages? The India that was proud, by the end of the Dvāpara age, had lost its pride, from the tip of its hair to its waist. Can the divine, with the touch of the sacred feet of the pure sages, ever descend in the form of a sage on the pure body of India? Whether they are householders or ascetics, students or wanderers, all are equal.

Now, they are merely witnesses to the development of the light of wisdom, residing like an indifferent observer. It is as if they are living in a city that is a mirror image of a stable state. The fickleness of worldly activities seems to have gone somewhere. What used to confuse the sadhak has now settled like a shadow under the light of wisdom, residing in a stable and peaceful state. This is the experience. This is the peaceful state filled with the essence of life. Worldly activities like desire and anger are local to the essence of life. With the realization of wisdom, these emotions settle into a peaceful state. Not only this, that place is adorned by silent-minded disciples. It was previously said that with the light of wisdom, emotions and activities become calm. Where is the root of these emotions? In words; emotions do not exist without words. Think of a tree; try to focus your mind on a stable emotion - you will see that "tree, tree, tree" becomes a word in your mind. Or when you hear a song, focus your mind on a deep emotion at that time - you will see that the song is already playing inside your mind. This is the experience.

Some have heard of this for a long time, and in the Ashram of the Sage, they have pondered and thought about it. Then, in that Ashram, the mind becomes free of ego. ||10||
Translation - Oh Sage! King Suratha, after being purified by Medhas in that Ashram, had stayed there for some time, moving back and forth. At that time, he, being enticed by ego, started thinking about various things.
Explanation - With the help of Yogic Wisdom, after achieving the fruit of truth, one is bound to stay for some time in that wisdom field or Medhas Ashram. This is because Medhas purifies. It awakens the memory of the Satva form. Even if one was not aware of Sat before, here, through the light of the wisdom flame, one can understand from Medhas' reservoir of firm memory, "I am Sat in the three times!" This is why the word "satkrita" in the Mantra implies the lost sense of the past in the word.

## Page 140

122
To Become Holy
When Ma, in the heart of the living being, says, "I am Brahman," she dispels the Dhruva Smriti, and the living being attains such joy that it cannot be described in words. It is a memory that clings to the heart - there is no desire to let go. After experiencing the burden of many lifetimes and enjoying the pleasures of the senses, when the living being comes to this ashram near the memory of this realization of Brahman, where there is no life in enlightenment, no servitude in knowledge, no limits in joy, no fear of death, no sorrow in the separation of loved ones, no pain in the hatred of the wicked, there is only existence, only knowledge, only joy, and only unbroken, complete self-realization. If the living being could somehow come to that place, would there then be any desire to quickly leave that place? But so far, the living being has not reached that perfect state. Only a memory of that state has been shown to the living being, like the light of a star for a moment in the vast, dark expanse of the mind. But whatever happens, this blissful memory keeps the living being enchanted with joy for some time. That is why the mantra says, "For a little while, he stood there." Why does the living being become so enchanted when he comes to this place? Why cannot the living being give up the temptation of the Medha Ashram? The living being becomes holy in this place. And he understands that I am born from 'Sat', I always remain in 'Sat', and 'Sat' is my ultimate destination. I remain in the eternal, present form of 'Sat' in the three times. First, the living being makes an effort to achieve the realization of Satya or Sat (which we have seen in the story of the deer being caught by a hunter and leaving the forest), this is only a false pursuit of Sat. That false pursuit of truth is like a counterfeit attempt to find Sat.

## Page 143

The Taste of Action
125
If only the living being is the seeker, and desire alone is the means, and success is inevitable according to the means, then, seeker! If you want to taste and experience immortality in this life itself, then you should vitalize the means - make it vital. All kinds of seekers, whether they are from the Saura, Vaishnava, Shaiva, Shakta, Jain, Buddhist, Yavana, or Mleccha traditions, can truly attain immortality. If the systems of their respective means are established on the foundation of truth, then they can achieve the desired result in a short time. Whatever tradition a seeker belongs to, the means of that tradition is sufficient to obtain an unique object, if the seeker can establish a connection with the vital force. Without the vital force, the path to the vital force cannot be found. Without the vital force, the fear of death cannot be dispelled. The more vital the means, the sooner it yields results. A means devoid of the vital force is like a dead body. Even if a dead body is adorned with ornaments and clothes, it cannot develop any kind of beauty; rather, it only darkens the already dark shadow. Similarly, external rituals and practices devoid of the vital force can never be successful in attaining the light of the supreme self; rather, they only deepen the ignorance. Just as the sap that vitalizes the tree flows equally through all parts of the tree, in the field of means, understand it in the same way. Traditions can differ, names can differ, forms can differ, practices can differ, rituals can differ, and there can be countless differences and various peculiarities, and it is also appropriate for them to remain so.

## Page 144

126
Misdirected Speculation
Amidst that great diversity, a continuous stream of bliss, consciousness, and joy, pervading everywhere, is being pursued (manifested). Focusing on that stream of bliss, all practices appear to lead to the same result. Only by forgetting (rejecting) this true essence do various sectarianism, establishing the primacy of one's own views, and efforts to dismantle others' views become evident.
Four boys, each from different castes, were studying the four varnas. In one book's varna introduction, there is a picture of a horse next to the letter 'अ'; in another book, there is a picture of a cobra, in another, of a deer, and in another, of a goat. The boys, seeing these pictures, learn the letter 'अ'; they engrave the shape of the letter in their minds. This is the purpose of the castes; but the boys have stained that 'अ' letter, covered it with ink, and made it invisible. Now they are quarreling with each other. One says, "Our book is very good, it has a picture of a horse, look how good it is!" Another says, "No, no, our book is not good; look at the picture of the cobra." The third says, "Oh, our book is no match for anyone's, it has a picture of a deer." What is a deer? How good is it! Then the fourth says, go, go, your three books are not as good as mine - look at the picture. What is a goat? You know - a sheep. In this way, sectarian quarrels exist in our religion - opposition. We have forgotten the purpose and the goal. We are harboring hatred towards each other based only on external appearances.
The purpose of practice and the goal of life is the attainment of realized bliss.

## Page 145

The Jīva is the only Sadhaka.
127
Nanda is the true form of Jīva! Despite any reason, we are Asat, Achet, and Nirānanda. We are always frightened by the fear of death - from which our very existence is lost. This fear is only in the Jīva; that is why we are Asat! Our knowledge is so muddled that it cannot illuminate all knowable objects; that is why we are Achet. The joy we experience is mixed with sorrow; that is why we are Nirānanda. Only by being freed from these three states, the Jīva will attain the realization of Sachidananda. This is the purpose of Sadhana. That form is not obtained by seeking it from another place, but it is hidden within each one. The process of revealing that unmanifest Brahman is called Sadhana. The Jīva alone performs this Sadhana through knowledge and ignorance. Because everyone wants our existence to be eternal, we want to remain forever; this is called the worship of Sat. But we do not want to remain in a state that we cannot understand. If someone says, "You will live for a long time," but we cannot understand, "I am," then we should not want to remain in that state. We want to remain and understand, "I will live for a long time and understand that I am." This is called the Sadhana of Chit. Then, if remaining is unconditioned and full of sorrow, we do not want to remain like that; therefore, we want to remain in a state where "I am" is in harmony, and my remaining is full of joy. In this way, every Jīva is in search of Sachidananda. Some seek it through intoxication, theft, and austerity; and some serve it through compassion, forgiveness, generosity, Bhagavad-preeti, or Bhajana. That is why the Jīva alone is the Sadhaka, and the Sadhana alone is the effort. This was said before. As long as we perform actions without knowing this, we remain ordinary Jīvas.

## Page 146

It is said that one must aim towards that flow of the essence of Sachchidananda hidden within all kinds of practices. From this experience of the true nature, the being realizes an unbroken eternal existence. This is what is represented by the word 'Satkrit' in this mantra. Regardless of the practice system of a particular tradition, if a Sādhaka establishes it in truth, makes it vital and full of essence, they can find this unbroken Sat-entity. The different results of Sadhana due to differences in practices and rituals would be born from ignorance. Here, if the sequence of practices of different traditions were mentioned and the method of establishing truth in them were shown, the size of the book would increase greatly; therefore, it has not been described. Or, no one can fully experience the mystery of Sadhana from reading the book, nor will they be able to. With mature understanding and proper faith, one should hear it from the Guru's mouth; and if the Guru is merciful, they should hear it from them.

The reason for not being able to stay for a long time in the extremely subtle field is pride in the gross. Until the gross is abandoned, it feels as if the ego is being humbled. This is why, even when climbing to subtlety, one wanders in the gross koshas repeatedly. This is Surath's wandering here and there.
With the help of Buddhi Yoga, suddenly one day a light of intelligence shines in each gross substance due to the result of the perception of Bhatri Satva. The seeker cannot stay for a long time in that light because then answers come to the body and mind; because - a heavy mind is tied to the lower. The weight of God is a mind. ## Page 150

Subject of Meditation
The joy of a son is a play. Once, Mother takes your hand, stands you up, then lets go of your hand, teaching you to stand and walk; she then pulls your hand back. You fall, get hurt, and feel pain. How does she, effortlessly, engage in the play of mother and son's joy, understanding the life of the jeeva as the Guru? So, contemplate - when such a state of life comes to the jeeva due to the power of constant practice, where it can sometimes remain in the intellectual field; even then, due to the attraction of ego-knowledge, bound by the strong attraction of samskaras, various gross subject matters of thought are born. They are tormented by the memory of those subjects. Ascending to the level of intellect, forgetting the subject, they become bewildered by the light of that deluding intellect. The more time they spend in the subtlety, the more they remain there, yet gross thoughts of body and other subjects seem to be present with them. For many days, if a bird, bound in a cage, suddenly gets the opportunity to explore the open sky, it may not go very far, but due to the habit of long practice, it returns to its nest, the cage. Similarly, a jeeva, entangled in body-consciousness for many days, if suddenly gets the opportunity to engage with Mother, may not go very far, but due to the habit of long practice, it returns to its dwelling, the cage.

## Page 151

Even with the grace of Deh-pur, one cannot stay there for long. Always restless, always impure, the mind remains in that vastness, that purity, that detachment, that harshness, that stability. It cannot endure for long. Then, memories of the body and its concerns arise. Or, I, with my compassion, guide them up and down, removing the complexity of the life force, gradually increasing their strength, and enabling them to move towards vastness. The city that was previously protected by the law of the mind is now mine. It is protected by the law of immortality, stability, and righteous conduct. Not by anyone else. Translation of our previous people who used to protect that city with great effort, we have left that city. Are not these wicked servants protecting that city according to its law? Explanation - When Surath stops at Meghasashram, due to the influence of predestined impressions and the attraction of the body, and the ego - the sharp edge of the mind, he gets entangled in these thoughts. It is natural for humans to think in this way. Before settling in the light of the mind, a certain increase in thoughts about the body happens. The word 'pur' means body. In this new door city, the soul takes position, and it is called a man. The soul is present in the mind field before abandoning the body. From the view of tranquil and elevated light, all kinds of contraction have been removed for some time; but for a long time, the thoughts about the body remain.

## Page 152

134
Deh Pur
Birth, true consciousness, and the body are not free from the knowledge of attachment. Until the nature of Chandi is not clearly revealed in the heart - until the slaying of Shumbha is not completed - until the fruits of the three types of karma are not fully established, the complete destruction of ego does not occur. As long as the body exists, one must understand that ego is certain. As long as the faults of the living being are seen under the influence of this ego, it can be understood that the root of ego will be destroyed soon. When a human being can properly grasp their faults, it is understood that the appropriate time for correcting those faults has arrived. Even with the fruitless results of buddhiyoga, the body collects the self-knowledge from the body and installs it in the mind, but remains attached to the subject of the body through memory. This is why, even after reaching Surath Medhas Ashram, one thinks, "What was previously preserved was previously mine. With the firm resolve of countless previous births, the Deh Pur that was perfected, now, due to our great efforts, we have to abandon this Deh Pur! Don't we know that the asad swabhav (unrighteous nature) of those servants - the group of senses - will continue to protect the previous body according to their dharma? After death, when we can take on the construction of a new body, the only reason is the firm resolve about the body from previous births. At the time of death, the desire to abandon this body is so intense that people leave it and go in search of a new body with three vasanas (desires). Due to this, the new body is constructed based on previous firm resolutions. The body is constructed and perfected by the self-knowledge of previous births. This is why it is said, 'Mat purvah paalitam.' Here, even if one wants to remain dispassionate, there is a need to mention one thing.

## Page 153

Suicide is a great sin.

It is said in our scriptures that suicide is a great sin,
because of the absence of intense desire for the body. The suicide
has intense hatred towards the body at the time of death, which is why
even after death, for a long time, he cannot reveal the desire for the body.
The soul stays for a long time in the abode of the ghost body or the
transmigratory body. It is bound by the accumulated true hopes and
fears of the life time. Even then, without the body, a desire cannot be
fulfilled; it has to be left behind due to intense compulsion. Rituals like
Shradha, which are meant for the upward journey of the soul, are
special helpers for the soul to quickly obtain the enjoyment body
(i.e., to be freed from the ghost world and then obtain the enjoyment
field); but for the suicide, due to intense hatred towards the enjoyment
body, the rituals performed with the intention of achieving that goal
are not of much help. That is why the scriptures prohibit the rituals of
suicide. Then, if a true seer sadhak, with the intention of achieving the
sinful goal of suicide, i.e., to obtain the enjoyment body, performs
penance and rituals with firm resolve, then he can be freed from the
ghost world. And those who attain death due to natural causes or diseases,
at the time of death, the intense desire for the body rises in the
consciousness field, they do not want to leave their most beloved body;
this intense desire can continue up to the point where it can quickly
construct a body. Rituals like upward journey help in obtaining that
enjoyment body.
Moreover, even when trying to ascend to the intelligent field, the
soul is bound by the infinite birth awareness of ego and is attracted
towards the body that has been carefully preserved, and in that state,
for some time, it remains attached to the body.

## Page 154

136
Body Nourishment
Self-awareness is often lost, leading to the thought that "I am devoid of Maya." I have abandoned that body. Will my senses, which were running wild in my childhood, now take care of that body properly or not? The senses, by constantly bringing various objects to the body, nourish it. Although the senses do not nourish the body directly, they nourish the mind. However, the nourishment of the mind is spent on maintaining the large body. Therefore, the senses are called the caretakers of the body. The senses are asat (unreal). The word 'asat' means opposite to 'sat' (real), nothing exists; because there is no other existence apart from one 'sat' thing. In the Gita, Lord Krishna says, "Nāsatō vidyate bhāvaḥ" (There is no existence of bhāva without sat). There is no substance called 'asat'. Here, the word 'asat' is used minimally. Everywhere, the same unbroken sat (real) substance, when manifested with a little bhāva (feeling), is called 'asat'. The name and form-specific manifestation of sat is a kind of development or play of sat, which is also called 'asat'. Our senses like eyes and ears, which are always moving from one object to another, nourish our asat (unreal) feeling; because objects are asat. As long as our senses perceive objects only as objects, the body is not maintained according to dharma. Without perceiving 'asat' as 'sat', the essence of 'sat' is not known. Without knowing the essence of 'sat', a person does not get rid of the knowledge of the destruction of life, and the fear of death does not disappear. Suratha (the jīva) has been purified by Meghas (the mind); he has found the true sat (real) substance. Therefore, now the senses, which were previously object-oriented, are now purified.

## Page 155

The vain senses
137
he understands as having no inherent nature. He who remains bound by the usual nature of unrighteousness, he alone is unrighteous. Only then can the living being understand here that those who do not see the world in the form of Bhagavan, they are like the two eyes of a peacock. Those who hear only the word of the mother and do not understand her call, their ears are like mere holes; those whose nose smells the fragrance of virtue but does not recognize the mother's limb, their nose is like always inhaling and exhaling meaningless breaths. Those whose tongue is always turned away from the name of the mother, they are like the tongue of a snake, vain. Those whose skin is not enchanted by the touch of the mother, their skin is like a vain covering of the body. In this way, the senses, devoid of objects, are servants of the unrighteous nature, they do not nourish the body in a righteous manner, they always nourish unrighteousness; for this reason, they are merely a transformation of nourishment into depletion - they always lead to destruction. A living being elevated by wisdom desires that my senses should be like that by which I became happy knowing the nature of a righteous being. Why should they remain unrighteous? Why do they then call objects as objects and nourish and purify my beloved enjoyment field with unrighteousness? These thoughts naturally arise in the mental field of the living being from attaining the foundation of righteousness. Not knowing, my main enemy, the powerful hand, always attacks my senses.

What kind of enjoyable objects he will obtain by holding them in his hands (by gaining control over them) is unknown.
