We keep saying, "I am a body," in this way, we continue to feel pride, because of which the body remains active. The moment this body pride stops (not just for a moment), the body becomes inactive at that moment. Therefore, pride is the main element in the fourteen actions; that is why the word "pradhana" is used in the mantra. Then, this body pride does not want to be completely far away, it remains in one's own feeling. Because of this body pride, one always experiences joy and pride, that is why the word "sad mad" is used in the mantra. It is a big evil to not defeat or conquer this body pride; that is why it is called "shur." This "ahankar" is only ignorance; that is why it is known by the name 'hasti.' The hasti, despite being immensely powerful, is bound by the servitude of a human being, just as it cannot understand how much 'atmabal' is in it, similarly, 'I' was once immensely powerful, on that day, 'I' took the form of Virat and became the creator, sustainer, and destroyer of the universe, - on that day, with the desire to play a great game from a free will. That great 'I' is now completely insignificant, having become attached to a mere flesh and blood body, I have forgotten all power and strength. The servitude of the body - by serving various things, I am satisfied; that is why it can only be called hasti-bhurkha. Even when establishing oneself in the field of conscious life, one first falls into this kind of body pride thought. In many births,

## Page 159

Prasad-Dhana Bhog
141
From the observance of the Adi Karma Kanda, an extraordinary mental peace is obtained. By serving Kamakanchana, the being who enjoys fulfillment, becomes motivated to seek a special kind of satisfaction, which surpasses ordinary fulfillment. Humans, inspired by the strength of following scriptural commands, are uplifted. The word 'Dhana' means the powers of Siddhi and other mothers - spiritual elevation, which one cannot attain without desire, the happiness of heaven in the other world, or love and liberation in the mother. Among these, one or the other fruit, that is, the hope of material gain, leads humans to engage in spiritual practices. The word 'Bhog' means enjoying with the mother and nourishing the five sheaths. First, the practice of special rituals with the help of a specific deity is done to experience motherly embrace. Until "Sarveṣṭaḥ Saṁplutodaka" (Gita 20:46) is achieved, until the divine form of the Supreme Being is seen in all things with all feelings, until full entry into the ocean of the mother's mercy is made, until then, by digging wells and other water sources in the form of special rituals, thirst is quenched or the experience of motherly embrace is obtained. This is why the previous teachers have arranged for various kinds of worship and Parvans monthly, bringing great benefit to us. Here, these are aids to timely motherly embrace, and on the other hand, they are the rules for nourishing all our components - they provide food. The group of spiritual practitioners, whether knowledgeable or ignorant, nourishes the body, prana, mind, knowledge, and bliss. By following scriptural commands with strength, health remains unbroken and life increases. This is the food for the Annamaya and Pranamaya sheaths! They are conducive to all karma, mental peace, and stability, and support introspective thinking ability; therefore, the path to gaining knowledge is opened. The amount of knowledge gained is equal to the amount of bliss obtained.

## Page 161

Karma Anandaprad
143
Our mother, in her form as Shankara, has presented karma as mere ignorance; then, in her form as Shri Gauranga, she has glorified the necessity of the karma-kanda; on one hand, it has been contracted by the brilliance of knowledge, and on the other, by the sharp edge of intense devotion, it has become almost dead. Has the current Vaidhik karma thus come to an end in mere lifeless rituals? If one becomes a knower like Shankara and a lover like Chaitanya, the root of karma-kanda necessarily becomes weak; this is true; but those who follow them without understanding that knowledge and love, if they abandon the Vedically prescribed karma-kanda as unnecessary, will surely understand that they are on a path full of delusion. The guide of karma is the Vedas. The Vedas are impersonal. They are born from the self-realization of the sages, free from delusion and error; therefore, karma-kanda is fruitless and yields little fruit, this is the introduction to ignorance. One day will come when no need for karma-kanda will arise in the mind. Then some will perform karma for the sake of education, and some will abandon karma. In that era, karma will become personal. The child's question falls on itself; but if someone cuts the question before it falls, the child's death is inevitable. Our Vedically prescribed karma-kanda is so sweet, so blissful, that even the most extreme dislikers cannot help being enchanted for a moment when they see the system of performing karma. By establishing the essence of the previously mentioned prana-rasa in each karma, one gains the pleasure of the mind, the glory of the mother, and the experience of the mother's embrace. No vow or ritual observance can be compared to this.

## Page 162

144
Unprecedented Unity
It is possible to achieve a lesser extent of these experiences. Those who perform the Karma Kanda rituals, their case is different; even the spectators are delighted with great joy and sattvic feelings. Each one should see that how much our mind has become peaceful, how much we have seen the glory of Ma, how much time we have spent in the enjoyment of maternal embrace while being deluded by the world's game. Without keeping the vision towards this effectiveness, one becomes devoid of life. Most people's belief is that whatever daily rituals we perform, such as Sandhya Bandana, or the worship and vows performed at home, do not lead to the attainment of Bhagwan. To attain Bhagwan, one has to receive a secret instruction from a yogi or a sannyasi and then perform the Sadhana according to that. Even after a lot of Sadhana, sometimes by fate, one may get the experience of self-realization. This belief has been prevalent in this country for many days. But this belief did not exist in the Vedic era. Even now, our daily actions are beneficial for the attainment of Bhagwan. Fasting, Sun worship, Asana Shuddhi, Japa of Ishta Mantra, etc., if any one of these is performed properly, then a human being can find the foundation of immortality; there is no doubt about this. In our scriptures, where we see the rules of various types of Karma Kanda, the reason for this is that there is a difference in rights but not in the Sadhana system. This is the speciality of Hindu Dharma that the difference in rights is known in the Sadhana system. In other religions, no such speciality exists; in no other country does this speciality exist. In other countries, all the worship systems are of one kind. Only in the Hindu community.

## Page 163

The Mystery of Action
145
Here, despite the differences in tradition, individuality, rights, and various forms of worship, the unique, sweet meeting, and incomparable importance are established. Due to the lesser differences in qualities, each person's nature is becoming distinct; therefore, it is extremely natural for each person's spiritual practice to be different. Apart from this, the purpose of many types of ritualistic actions is also to make our mind extremely restless; it is very difficult to keep the mind stable by clinging to one action system for a long time. We do not want to be satisfied with the taste of one kind of pleasure; we want to enjoy the same thing with new feelings. Japa, meditation, worship, homa, kirtan, etc., are known to be ritualistic actions only for the natural transformation of our mind. Food, drink, and nourishment are the three essential elements of the ritualistic actions. These three purposes are regulated with special intent, and the ritualistic actions follow us, are favorable to us; but when the living being attains a little wisdom (wisdom is perceived only in the intellectual field), then he sees that the group of ritualistic actions that have been nourished so far by food, wealth, and nourishment are following other kings. Here, the word 'king' (Mahi-bhrit) refers to the nourisher of the earth, the gross body - considered to be the physical activities of the senses. This group of ritualistic actions only follows gross earthy feelings, the first flaw of the living being's ritualistic action is that it cannot experience the defect. The mind understands that it is just fine. Rituals like sandhya bandhan, which are to be performed, are just performed; but even then, they cannot see - cannot understand that they are nourishing earthy feelings. Rituals are performed only for the service of the mind and senses.

## Page 164

146
Brahma-Karma
is. Once the attainment of Chaitanya is achieved, and a ray of wisdom becomes visible, the fault in the work becomes clear. Your life becomes extremely restless upon seeing the gross faults in this Karmakanda and it attempts to find a way to make all actions knowledge-based, sweet, and self-realization-oriented. Then one becomes diligent.
Here, it is necessary to discuss some aspects of the mystery of Karma. Bhakti Karma cannot be fruitful unless it is knowledge-based, and knowledge cannot be fruitful unless it is devotion-based. Whatever valid, practical Karma, from which their development originates and where they meet, if not performed, it remains merely ignorant. Only when each part of Karma is treated as a mother does Karma become fruitful. Karma is performed in the form of "Brahma Rna Brahma Havih Brahmaanau Brahmanahurt." The six agents—Karta, Karma, Karana, Sampradana, Apadana, Adhikarana—are all 'Brahma' 'Ma.' Ma is our Karta, Ma is our Karma, Ma is our Karana, Ma is our fruit. In all parts of Karma, one experiences the motherhood of Ma. Thus, Karma becomes knowledge-based. O Sadhak! When meditating, see that Ma meditates on Ma! When worshipping, see that Ma worships Ma! Even in the form of worship rituals, Ma is the one who is honored. When seated to perform Homa, see that Ma is formless, Ma is in form, Ma is in the form of Havi, Ma is in the form of Arpana, Ma is in the form of Adhira Swar, and start by calling out Ma in the form of Adhira. In this way, in the various parts of Karma, practice seeing Ma. Then Karma becomes knowledge-based. Knowledge and Karma are one and the same thing. Karma is not ignorance; it is the abundant development of knowledge. Whatever knowledge you attain, it is Ma who is your attainment.

Only by serving these senses, repeatedly taking refuge in the jaws of death,
only death is not the only thing, but due to the infinite loss of life force in life,
due to various kinds of diseases, machines, and suffering, the burden of death is greater.
You should be agitated seeing the shadow of death in every moment in the earthly enjoyment,
soon you will get the solution of immortality, and be blessed like Surath.
To stop this improper destruction of life force, in the world of dharma,
the practice of pranayama, hathayoga, nabhikriya, and various kinds of skills is the method.
From the practice of different actions, we see a difference in our breathless movement.
Generally, we exhale as much external air as we inhale,
but in breathlessness, a little more air comes out in comparison.
This excess is our CCO.

## Page 169

Worship is merely action.
151
It is a part of accumulated vital energy. Breath is not pure vital energy; it is merely the gross manifestation of vital energy. In a healthy body, the natural breath moves twelve finger-widths. Its movement increases significantly due to excessive eating, sleep, sexual activity, running, etc. To prevent this increase, i.e., excessive loss, one must practice restraint in food, sleep, and sexual activity. Then, by laughing at the natural movement, one practices nasal breathing and abdominal breathing sequentially. Finally, with the help of kumbhak (holding the breath), they practice sitting motionless for a very long time, becoming proficient through rigorous practice. After spending a long time with a suitable skilled guru, they practice this. The results of this practice can lead to a healthy body, long life, and two siddhis; but can a human be satisfied with just this? No matter how much effort is not made, no matter how much yoga skill is not utilized, there is no other way to be saved from the hands of death. Therefore, it is extremely suitable to fully practice this complete practice for the enjoyment of the fearlessness and immortality of the motherly love of the great goddess. Only a sadhaka who sees the greatness of the Praneshvari Mahapranamayi Mahamaya Ma and witnesses this vast Brahma-yajna is capable of obtaining immortality. The sadhaka who sees that every sign, every effort, every thought, every limb's operation, every inhalation and exhalation is the worship of Mahamaya alone, who has understood in their heart - "From morning to night, from night to morning, whatever I do is the worship of your mother." Only those people are capable of protecting themselves from this treasury - destruction. Whose all actions are mother-like.

## Page 170

152
Those who have been initiated with the "Brahmaarpan" Mantra are free from the illusion of birth and death for all time; therefore, they do not need any separate skill for Kosakshay-nirodh. As long as the group of Dharmic actions, or even a single Dharmic action, is not imbued with knowledge—meaning that knowledge alone manifests in the form of this action—until knowledge is not attained, all actions are performed out of the sense of 'I'. Actions performed out of the sense of 'I' will lead to the destruction of life, because life is a perishable being. Destruction or decay is the natural dharma of life. Without attachment, even if you practice Pranayama, until you find the limit of the imperishable being, there will be no way to stop this destruction. 

Oh, the Pranamayakosha, composed of the appropriate state of mind and enhanced by the tumultuous cries of many births, the fervent aspirations of many births, countless lives of suffering, and countless deaths, when viewed with thought, will only lead to the life being contracted in vain. The expenditure of this will only be in the service of the senses and the adherence to the orders of the mind. Compared to this, what can be more pitiful? When a life is fortunate, it engages in various activities day and night in the body, mind, senses, etc., aiming at a goal. Only then does it try to prevent events contrary to self-realization; but it sees that all these events are perishable; they are all ready to depart! Great vows have been abandoned! The mind is always entangled in the pleasure of transient subjects! The body is being plundered! The life force is being consumed by the activities of the senses! Both friend and enemy are contrary!

Vishistadarsana
Oh Merciful One! You will come to us even if we do not wish for it! Such grace, such love, O Mother! It is in your heart! By taking a pinch of your love, the world's mothers forget their own children's love. Then you are the ocean of love, how much more is your love! You are our mother! Oh Mother! Where there is no sin in speaking, you are our mother. Seeing the faults of our descendants, you are our blind mother. You will come! You will make us forget ourselves. With a little hold, "Son, come," you will say with respect! Then I will turn my face away in pride and say - "Now I will not call you mother, nor will I call you mother."  
In these four mantras, the thoughts of Suratha are mentioned, let us now briefly discuss them. The first is the thought related to the body - the improper nourishment of the senses, the second is the thought related to the vast enjoyment of the body's pride, the third is the thought related to the external nature of the karma-kanda, and the fourth is the thought related to the wasteful destruction of the true vital body from great effort. To those who have gained the opportunity to enter the field of wisdom, these thoughts are a very natural thing.  
Thus, Partha constantly thought about these.  
There, in the practice of the Brahmin's ashram, he saw a single Vaishya.  
Commentary - In this way, when a person becomes extremely agitated due to many thoughts, what is the way to counteract the opposition of this body-sense?

## Page 173

The Fourfold Mystery
155
After being saved, I will always find a place in my mother's abode of fearlessness. In this way, when thoughts become extremely agitated, then our mother reveals her presence to us in the company of a Vaishya. The word Vaishya has been established through the entrance of the seven primary elements. Entrance - a righteous person is a Vaishya. By transcending the kingdom of intelligence, whoever enters the abode of my mother - the abode of fearlessness - is called a Vaishya. This special introduction will come later. Here, discussing the mystery of caste is not entirely irrelevant. There is no caste of the soul, nor of the body; but the caste of a specific living being, which is identified with body-soul awareness, is well-known among all. The difference in caste comes from the difference in qualities and duties. Qualities are countless; therefore, caste is also endless. This is not a social system created by humans. The variety in the subtle body is what shows different castes or varnas. Sadhana - the levels of rights in the world - have been represented by the four varnas. As long as a being remains satisfied by serving Bhagwan by identifying with a specific form or feeling, they are considered a Shudra-level sadhak; when a being can recognize Bhagwan as their own ultimate essence and, with the hope of achieving various desired fruits, approaches any specific form or feeling with full power, they are called a Vaishya-level sadhak. When a being is capable of making a full-hearted self-sacrifice to Bhagwan, knowing their ultimate spiritual knowledge, to protect life from suffering, they are considered a Kshatriya-level sadhak. And those who recognize Brahma as their own essence, that is, who fully express the life force in the footsteps of the Chit-mahashakti, experiencing eternal bliss, and continuously protect the world, are not called Mahas.

## Page 174

156
Social Status
In the Sharirak commentary, the meaning of the word "Shudra" is explained as follows:
"Shuchā dravati iti Shudraḥ." A person who is agitated by sorrow and distress is considered a Shudra. Those who are freed from this Shudrata and begin to enter the realm of Atmaramya are considered Vaishyas. According to Vedic scriptures and the Matri-Samvedana, the first group of sadhakas to enter are the Vaishya caste. Those who are engaged in Atmalabha, that is, self-surrender, are considered Kshatriyas. And those who have reached Atmaprapti, that is, perfection, are considered Brahmanas. This division in the spiritual world is reflected in the social world in the categorization of the Brahmanadि varnas! All run towards the same great goal - the goal of attaining the blissful Paramatma. Among those who are running, the one who is the farthest ahead is a Brahmana; the one who is behind them is a Kshatriya. Similarly, those behind them are Vaishyas and those behind them are Shudras. There is no hatred or violence here; it is mutual understanding. Even if a Shudra or Vaishya acquires Brahmanic qualities, they should remain detached from the desire to create a social hierarchy; this was the firm resolve of Lord Krishna. This is why he specially mentions the anishtakarita of Varnashankara in the Gita. Although the caste system is visible in the current era, it is still necessary for humans to remain fully dedicated to preserving their caste-specific duties. A person should remain in the state in which they are placed and seek refuge in the Lord's feet, regardless of whether their work is criticized or praised. With all their heart, seeking refuge at the feet of the Lord and advancing in the field of action, even if they have little strength, they will grow in power, and in their mind, there will always be an unparalleled, pure, and eternal peace.

## Page 177

The Name of Samadhi
Vaisya said,
I am a Vaisya named Samadhi, born into a wealthy family.
But I was expelled by my wealth-loving, unrighteous wife and sons. ||18||
156
Translation - Vaisya said, "I am a Vaisya named Samadhi, born into a wealthy family. But I was expelled by my wealth-loving, unrighteous wife and sons."
Explanation - Through the fruits of many births and sattvic good deeds, the jiva attains Samadhi. Jaya, Svapna, and Sushupti are three states that ordinary beings are always entitled to experience. Besides these three, there is another state, known as Turiya or Samadhi. Occasionally, some jivas experience this state. In this state, the mind, intellect, consciousness, ego, eyes, ears, and so on, the five sense organs and the five organs of action, these sixteen organs each perform their own tasks. This state is called the Jagrat state. When only four internal organs are active and the remaining ten organs are in a state of inactivity, it is the Svapna state. When all sixteen organs are inactive, it is called the Supta state. In this Supta state, we forget ourselves. Then, the knowledge of the world and "I am" is not directly experienced. This is usually a state of death. But in the Samadhi state, it is not so - the knowledge of the world does not remain, but the sense of self remains. Those who are called 'sleeping in the world' are completely asleep from the world's perspective; but they are awake in the self's perspective, this is called Samadhi. After practicing the fruits of meditation to reside in the Chaitanya Maya Mahakasha Mandala, this state becomes present on its own. This is the door to entering the kingdom of the self or the mother's lap. Therefore, this is the door to becoming a Vaisya or CCO. Vasishtha Tripathi Collection. Samadhi - This is the final limb of Ashtanga Yoga. Yama, Niyama, Asana, Pranayama, Pratyahara, Dharana, Dhyana, and Samadhi; these eight limbs of Yoga are mentioned in the Yoga Shastra. These are only useful in the pursuit of Bhagwan, not otherwise. Without Yoga, no work in the world can be accomplished. The meaning of the word Yoga is to meet. Is it the meeting of senses with objects, the meeting of mind with intellect, the meeting of intellect with soul, the meeting of individual soul with Paramatma, or the meeting of devotees with Bhagwan or the meeting of mother with son? All these are Yogapadas. This meeting or Yoga is nothing but the sum total of the eight limbs mentioned earlier. The union of senses with objects is called Kamya; therefore, only Karma is Yoga, and only Jeeva is a Yogi. The great Yogini Yogamaya, our mother's imagined every Paramanu, is always united in this great Yoga. The great Yogini Yogeshwari, the heart-dwelling of Maheshwar, cannot remain in the sky to the Paramanu without the loss of Yoga or relationship. The end of this Yoga is in the perfect meeting with the mother and in Maha Mukti. In which past era, on the days of the first light of Chaitanya, did this Yoga begin, and until when will it end? Who other than our Yogarani Ma will understand this? Now we should see how Yoga happens in just a little. Suppose you are eating, at that time

## Page 179

Yoga is merely action.
161
It is necessary to restrain one's mind from other activities according to need, and this is called Yama. While eating, washing hands and feet, placing food in its proper place, etc., certain rules are followed; these are the rules. Just as sitting in a way that allows one to eat well is called Asana, similarly, the way one sits for a particular activity is appropriate for that activity. Running or sleeping in a certain way, if one's body is not shaped correctly, food cannot be eaten properly. Rather, the posture that is suitable and comfortable for the activity one is doing is the appropriate Asana for that activity. After that comes Pranayama. Prana means expansion. The concept of Pranayama will be explained later. Generally, when we talk about Pranayama, we should understand it as the control of inhalation and exhalation. The harmony in the speed and quantity of our breath due to different activities is due to the expansion or contraction of Prana. The activity that causes Prana to expand should be performed with a gentle breath. And the activity that causes Prana to contract should be performed with a strong breath. The goal of the alternation of inhalation and exhalation is only for humans; but Pranayama is suitable for contemplation by seekers. The goal should be to see how far a person can expand or contract their Prana; it is only by setting this goal that the previous teachers have established the system of good and bad actions and prohibitions. The activity that causes Prana to naturally expand is described as good in the scriptures; this is the essence of the method. And the activity that causes Prana to contract is called a bad action or a forbidden act by the sages. Good, bad, and prohibited actions are all related to Pranayama.

## Page 180

162
Natural Yoga
Depends on the principle. Yes, this is another matter. In our previously proposed activities, in the same way, the natural balance of breath and exhalation is always established. Continual intake - restraining sensory activities from other subjects and directing them towards the desired activity is called intake. There is also some intake required to complete the activity of eating. After that comes retention - holding the mind towards food, satisfaction, and the cessation of hunger. This is how the activity of eating ends when hunger is satisfied. In this way, some attention or thought and a brief momentary meditation occurs in the matter of food - for a moment, the mind touches the command center, and as a result of this, the activity of eating is completed. This is the same everywhere. Without knowing, this eight-limbed yoga practice remains within all our activities. Our worldly activities have become such a habit that even if we cannot focus on each activity, it becomes complete after eight or so attempts. According to the rule of one hundred leaves (1), we come to know that these are completed together with one effort. No activity can be completed without meditation; when the mind is settled and established in the correct state of consciousness and determination, then only is meditation achieved. A thorn has entered your shoe, the senses have brought it near the mind, but the mind cannot say what it is; from this, it then brings it near the intellect, which is the one that needs to be near. (1) One hundred leaves are placed on the ground, and ninety-nine are cut; it is said that we have cut all the leaves together, but in reality, they are cut one after another in this manner.

## Page 181

### Description of Samadhi
**163**
This state is called Samadhi. At this time, the mind merges with the Ajna Chakra along with the intellect. Then the intellect says, "Its name is 'Kanakavdh,' and it causes some pain." Only then does the mind say, "Oh, it's a great pain," and experiences the suffering of Kanakavdh. Similarly, everywhere. This merging of the mind with the intellect is the root of all material existence. The living being remains in this Samadhi day and night; therefore, the Ashtanga Yoga, which is embodied, Yama, Niyama, etc., are naturally achieved, but this is not true Samadhi because it depends on the impressions of the mind. True Samadhi is the union of the mind with Pragna. The Rigvedic Mahavakya "Prajnanam Brahma" - Pragna is Brahma. When the mind is shaped like Pragna or merges into Pragna, then true Samadhi occurs. Initially, it becomes Savikalpa Bhava, which is the triad of Jnata, Jnana, and Jneya. Then, through the grace of the mother and the practice, it becomes Nirvikalpa, that is, without the triad, it is revealed as pure - Bodha. This is called Asamprajnata Samadhi. Samadhi is the door to meeting the mother. The system of meeting the embodied life - feeling-consciousness with the unbroken Chit-samudra is Samadhi. Always, in the living-consciousness, its presence and manifestation are established, but as long as it cannot be directly experienced or felt, the living being cannot escape the clutches of death, suffering, pain, sorrow, and heat. Only when the mother appears in the form of Samadhi, does she unite the living being with the ocean of the self and makes it selfless. This is the highest and ultimate purpose of human life. Initially, this Samadhi is impure and feeling-conscious, which is why the terms 'sorrow' and 'du' are used in the Mantra. CCO. Vasishtha Tripathi Collection. ## Page 182

164
Description of Pralaya
Well, Suratha did not even get the glimpse of Samadhi for so many days, but due to the fruit of great fortune and the grace of the mother, he has been blessed with her vision. Then, if he becomes mired in attachment, it will be fine. The mire will be cut, sorrow will be dispelled, and sorrow will turn into joy. You will understand all these things later. Now let's see why Samadhi is called 'sorrowless' and 'free from malice' in the first direct experience? It is said in the Mantra: "Puthradara nirastashva dhanalobhad sadhobhih." Wealth lures one into unrighteousness, and attachment to children is the door to hell. This is why the mire of attachment leads to sorrow and malice. The son of Samadhi is Dhyana (meditation) and the daughter is Dharana (concentration). Some detailed discussion on this point is necessary.
From a gross perspective, it is known that after the limbs of Yama and Niyama, the ripened previous states appear in sequence. That is, when the Yama practice is completed, Niyama appears. When Niyama is completed, the time for Asana practice comes. Similarly, in sequence, when Dharana is practiced, Dhyana appears, and when Dhyana becomes profound, Samadhi appears. But this is not the case; we see that when the time for Samadhi comes, the other limbs of Yoga automatically become complete. Samadhi is an eternal accomplished thing; it is not another substance. Dhyana does not come from Samadhi; rather, Dhyana becomes complete when Samadhi appears. When darkness is dispelled, the sun does not rise; rather, the sun rises, and then darkness departs.
Just as creation happens in a sequential order, Pralaya also happens in the same manner. From Prakriti comes Mahat, from Mahat comes Ahankara, from Ahankara comes Pancha Mahabhuta; in this way, creation happens in a sequential order. At the time of liberation or Pralaya, from a subtle perspective, the same sequential order is seen. When Prakriti contemplates,

---

## Page 183

It is said that once I do not see the result, the process of drawing up begins; that is, the attempt to merge into the greatness of Prakriti, to draw back the great ego, the ego from the five elements, the five elements from the five Mahabhutas, this way the attraction happens in a proper order; but the external manifestation of avilobhagati occurs. It is realized that the destruction has begun from below, that is, entering into the five Mahabhutas, the five elements into the ego, the ego into the great, and the great into Prakriti; in this way, Prakriti merges into Purusha or moves away from Purusha. This which happens in reverse is actually the manifestation or fruit of the hidden anulomagati. As seen during fever, the water of the ocean, the river, the branch river, the canal, etc., enters and nourishes their body, then due to the attraction of the ocean of rice, the water of the river channels starts flowing towards the ocean. First, the rice is cooked in the ocean; this is why the water of the river channels starts flowing towards the ocean. Similarly, Samadhi and other Yoga limbs are also like this. Anulomagati is everywhere in the world. Samadhi is the source of Dhyana, Dhyana is the source of Dharana, and Dharana is the source of Pratyahara. Similarly, understand the other Yoga limbs. Although the Yoga Shastra does not mention this exact sequence, although the sadhakas try to go up from below, wise people can understand with some deep reflection that - Samadhi is an eternal, accomplished thing or state; it is always in a balanced state. It is not born from Dhyana and Dharana, but Dhyana, Dharana, etc., are always in the pursuit of Samadhi. When Samadhi is realized, it remains in the form of simple rules.

No matter how harsh the time, no matter how dense the darkness, Mother has kept the ocean of compassion as deep as her son's love; she is drowning the world with compassion. ## Page 187

Historical Truth

Our mother, who, emerging from the heart of each living being with a certain feeling, attracts the living being to the temple of liberation, which is secretly formed in the hearts of the seekers, appears in a special form on the earth to reveal this feeling. In this way, she appears in the form of Suratha Samadhi or by destroying countless asuras, she teaches the living world. It was said from the beginning that the story of Chandi is not merely a parable. There is no historical basis for Suratha and Samadhi. False ideas do not have a place in the scriptures. Since ancient times, this land has been protected by many kings. Every human life has some history. The sages have not combined everyone's history. They have only described those stories in which spiritual mysteries are embedded. Only such stories are described. Those whose names and descriptions are few, on one hand, can be history and education for the masses, and on the other hand, spiritual principles can also be created. The purpose of the sages was to write the stories of such people. From this, it is seen in the Arsh Pranth that spiritual mysteries are beautifully arranged in the vicinity of historical truth.

## Page 188

170
Ananda Dhan
Explanation - Ananda is the wealth of Samadhi. Son, wife, and other friends, that is, Dhyana, Dharana, and other limbs of Yoga, have taken away that wealth. The living being always practices this Ashtanga Yoga in every action. It is practiced with the limited purpose of experiencing limited Ananda due to the connection with the senses. That limited Ananda is obtained through Samadhi, it is merely a drop from the ocean of Ananda. From Brahma to a chinti, everyone is searching for Ananda. The one who runs in this world, seeing the light of dawn, is running only with the hope of fulfilling the wish and the fulfillment of gold. The group of living beings is running like madmen, like blind people, without any direction, without any knowledge, with the sole aim of getting a little Ananda. Proud people get Ananda by performing religious rituals, sinners get Ananda by performing hated actions. Both are the same in the aspect of Ananda; because "Ananda is Brahman" - Ananda is Brahman - Ananda is "Ma." The true form of Ma, who is Sachchidananda, is manifesting in inert matter with a special feeling. Ma, who is Sat-Kharup, that is, Ma who is, is always manifest in these visible inert matters to explain to us in a certain way. Our Ma is Chit, with a special feeling, she is manifesting everywhere in the form of a living being. We see the special development of the state of consciousness in living beings. Then, Ananda Dhan is manifesting only in her. Ananda is nowhere else. Only Ma is always manifesting as the Ananda Dhan-murti everywhere. Whatever Ananda is seen in every living being, it is merely a specific drop from that Ananda-ocean. How does the living being experience this Ananda. When we try to collect any desired object, then our senses and mind are motivated by that purpose, at that time, even the intellect gets entangled in the waves of the Ananda-ocean and becomes subject-oriented.

## Page 189

Within the realm of bliss,
171
it occurs. Then, when the effort is successful, meaning the desired object is obtained, the senses, mind, and intellect remain stable for a moment. Only then does the reflection of bliss appear in the intellect; thus, bliss is obtained. But we think that the desired object alone gives us bliss. This is ignorance. Bliss is not in the object; the shadow of bliss always falls in the intellect. When the intellect runs towards the object instead of towards that reflection, then we fall from bliss. Only then is the experience of bliss possible in the stability of the intellect. Here, there is a possibility of doubt that if bliss is an unbroken form, then how can we experience different kinds of bliss from different acquisitions? How is the experience of bliss different when we acquire gold compared to when we acquire it through sensory indulgence? How is it that we experience different kinds of bliss when we see a flower compared to when we hear music? The answer is that even if the object of bliss is one and unbroken, the variety in the conjunction of the subject and the senses makes us experience different kinds of bliss. When our senses are satisfied with a special feeling, the variety in the satisfaction of those senses is the cause of the appearance of the variety of bliss. The absence of bliss is nowhere. This world is filled with bliss. "From bliss alone are living beings born, and from bliss alone they are revived, and in bliss alone is the end of life." This saying has been repeatedly highlighted by the sages. Even in the current era, those who have understood this are echoing the same thing. The compassionate weeping of a bereaved person, the long breathless suffering of a sick person, the torment of hunger, all these are nothing but manifestations of the same thing.

Oh, living being! When will you drink the stream of that Bliss and attain eternal peace? Yes, Samadhi is the search for this unbroken ocean of Bliss; but my mind is captivated by the joy of meditation. It is situated within the limits of intellect. However, the Bhagvatattva is situated much beyond intellect (in the past). The word "so'" means 'that one.' It is not 'I' but 'that one.' I have three forms or states: one is the jiva 'I,' another is the Ishwara 'I,' and the third is the supreme 'I.' That one is the supreme essence or the best state of I. This word is completely indirect for the jiva's experience. Therefore, the name-purush or the 'so' word is used. According to the rules of grammar, the word 'tat' is also used for an indirect object. Therefore, the 'so' word is understood to mean the Paramatma. I and the Atma are one and the same thing. Jivatma and Paramatma are not two different substances. The part of the Atma from which the jiva's experience develops is called Jivatma, and the part where no experience develops is Paramatma. What is this supreme essence of the Atma? Try to understand it a little. It is that state from which the Atma is only enlightened as pure consciousness and bliss, or where asat, achit, and niranda never experience anything. This is the supreme essence. It is described in language using words like bhava, avastha, swarupa, etc. Thinking about it, these words are not suitable for describing it; because bhava, avastha, swarupa, etc., are separate substances. Understand it in a gross way that this is one such state.

The creation and its sustenance happen within that "I." Then, when he wishes not to manifest in many states, then creation will break apart - it will be pralaya. Therefore, he is the creator, sustainer, and destroyer of creation. This part, that is, Ishwar, is called the manifestation of the Paramatma, as previously mentioned. This supreme part is called Shaktiman, and starting from the knowledge of "I am," it shines in many states; its sustenance and destroyer aspect is called Shakti. This Shakti and Shaktiman are essentially the same substance. Just as the light of the sun and the heat of fire, which are only mentioned superficially, cannot be experienced differently when described, similarly, the difference between Shakti and Shaktiman can only be used in a verbal sense. Just as when the head of Rahu is mentioned, Rahu and the head appear as an indivisible state, similarly, Paramatma and Shakti can appear as an indivisible state. 
Oh, now I understand that "I" has three forms. A jeeva is "I am Ishwar," the second is "I am Param," and the third is "I am Param Param." This Param is called "Sah" because it is indirect. And the jeeva's "I" is called "Ahankar." When these two merge, then only does the jeeva manifest as Brahman. The door to this merging is Samadhi, Samadhi makes "Ahankar" into "Sah," and this is why Samadhi is known as "So'."

## Page 201

Sadya Ishwara

In the same way, we should understand the meaning of "I am He";

See, the statement "I am a living being" which is understood, does not contain anything real; because, if we understand the example of the lamp, "I" am just one person. "If I say 'I', then it can only be said to Ishwara." Dehaatmavid - the separate identity of the individual living being is just ignorance. In reality, it is the same. That which meets with "I" is the same as meeting with Ishwara in the Supreme. When it is said that they meet, it should not be understood that two different things have come together. The meeting of the living being with the light of ignorance, and the meeting of Ishwara with the light of truth, is when the meeting of the living being and Ishwara is established. Then, when 'I' is revealed in the light of Ishwara, and is sublimated in the Supreme, the ultimate goal or Kaivalya is achieved. When the living being is revealed in the light of the Supreme, it cannot transcend the middle form of Ishwara. The living being must first be Ishwara, and Ishwara must be sublimated in the Supreme, this is liberation; this is the ultimate truth.
Therefore, now it is understood that the living being is the Sadya Ishwara, but the Supreme is not Sadya. It is past all kinds of states from Sadhana. Therefore, worship, Sadhana, etc., which are something, are realized in the middle state. If the living being can be in the form of Ishwara in any way, then it truly finds the foundation of the 'I' as a thing. The 'I' revealed from the living being is just a shadow. Therefore, it is said in Chandi further - "Or Devi is established in the form of a shadow in all beings." In truth, 'I' is creation, existence, and the destroyer; He is the all-knowing, all-powerful, infinite, ocean of compassion, loving, and capable of both restraint and granting; He is the ultimate refuge - this is the Akshara Purusha.

---

## Page 202

828
He is the enjoyer and the ephemeral being; because if the light form of 'I' hidden within the glass were to depart, it cannot remain as a shadow form of life. Therefore, it is said, 'I am the only mother.' Our mother is 'I' in the form of Maya. From this, I am manifesting in all its limbs; in each atom, the word 'I' 'I' develops. In that vast ocean, countless small birds like sparrows emerge from the waves; the name for finding that is Sadhana. The name for its desire is Bhakti or Love. The name for knowing it is Jnana. Until Sadhana is not this substance-based, not soul-searching, it remains in a despondent state, advancing with soft words. Some are in the realm of self-awareness. "Pujyadhyanajapadini namasankirtanani / Aham deva viyuktani vikalanyah brahmavit." Puja, Dhyana, Japa, Nama Kirtana, etc., remain incomplete fruit-bearing until they are connected with 'I'. 'I' alone is the one to be realized, 'I' alone is the one to be worshipped, 'I' alone is the one to be revered. Until the seeker leaves this, they continue to worship me; but without rituals. Therefore, the Lord said in the Gita, "Even these other deities are worshipped with faith. They too worship Me, the one and only, with rituals." Thus. Listen,
- I am the Lord, the only enjoyer and the only master of all sacrifices. Whoever performs any action, I alone enjoy it. O life! Until you recognize me, until you honor me, you will continue to be caught in the wheel of birth and death, suffering and torment. ("Seeing the spinning wheel, Kabir weeps. Even entering two of its cogs, Savitri was saved.") I am present in the hearts of all beings in the form of life. There is no one in humanity who does not recognize me. Therefore, even a wicked person can sing my praises.

## Page 203

All are the experiencers. I am Radha Krishna for the Vaishnavas, Shiva for the Shaivas, Shakti for the Shakta, Ganesha for the Ganapatyas, and Surya for the Sauras. I manifest in various forms for specific devotees. I am formless and unmanifest. Until the jiva remains in the state of "I am the jiva" and worships me with a separate sense of "I am Ishwara," they will not find me - there is no way to find me. Always remember, "I am not the jiva." The one who feels pride by saying "I am the jiva" is not the jiva; he will never leave the jiva state. Listen, from birth to birth, I remain in your heart with knowledge and in all forms with ignorance, nourishing you, respecting you, and growing you (increasing) from the shelter of affection. If you cannot understand now, there is no harm; now that you are a human, will you not recognize me as "mother"? With great sorrow, I said, "Now, foolish human beings, I am with you, attached to your body. (Gita 6.11) O humans! You are showing great disrespect to me. The more disrespect you show, the more I hide myself from you, blushing and secretly shedding tears, calling you my son and following you behind. I am only in this hope that one day you will respect me - one day you will call me mother! You say 'my, my' day and night - pride in my name is pride in my name. Saying 'my, my' (come to me) will never make you look at me. Son! How long will you remain a child? Call me mother, find me from me."

The secret of abandoning all dharmas will disappear. Different forms appear as reflections based on different foundations, but they all find their light in the one "I". That one "I" is my refuge. "Having abandoned all dharmas, I seek refuge in Krishna" (Bhagavad Gita 18/66). In the forms in which I see my reflection, abandon them; seek refuge in the one that is present within all. "I will cleanse you of all sins, O sinless one" (Bhagavad Gita 18/66). "I will free you from all forms of sin, meaning from the very core of sin, in the tranquil and elevated land of liberation; I will install you in my kingdom; I will lead you, O son! Do not be sad! ## Page 205

Household Talk
28
Explanation - The house of meditation and concentration is the mind. Do we not know that Kshema Kuri's Shri Padm Padm Samsparsa (touch of the lotus feet of Shri) makes Kshema (prosperity) shine? Or do the desires born from Akshema (misfortune) still rule over us? Kshema or Mangal (prosperity or good fortune) is only Ma (the Mother). She shines in the form of Ma in all beings. How fortunate is the mind that has obtained Her? How will the mind obtain Ma? The mind is that chanchala (fickle) state of our samskara (impressions) and avastha (condition). When truth is established in every object, every feeling, as a result of the fruit of the vision of the mother, then only will the motherhood be revealed from all feelings. Only then will you understand that the foot of Kshema Kuri has touched the mind. Until then, the unchanchala (unchanging) desires will further increase the chanchala (fickle) nature of the mind. Until the fire of desire burns the mind to ashes, Akshema will continue to shine in the mind. To know which one rules over the mind, this is the cry of Samadhi. That which has been removed from the kingdom of the mind and is present in the buddhimag (intellectual) field; because of the fall with the mind, the current state of the mind cannot be known, even then, that which has a previous sashakti (attachment) towards the mind; that also cannot be abandoned. It is also necessary to know that until the limbs of Yoga, such as Yama Niyama, are not practiced with full bhava (feeling) towards the goal of Matr Prapti (obtaining the Mother), but only for physical health, stability of the mind, or acquisition of a specific power, then Akshema will continue to shine in the mind. Apart from the putragan (sons), that is, the limbs of Yoga such as Dhyana, are they in accordance with the current sadaachara (righteous conduct) or asadachara (unrighteous conduct)? This is also the cry of Samadhi.

## Page 206

228
Doubt about Samadhi
There is only one Atma-Ma (the divine mother). Remaining in the present is called Sadaachara (righteous conduct), and contemplating only in the subject of desire, devoid of maternal affection, is called Asadaachara (unrighteous conduct)! The limbs of Yoga are practiced either with the purpose of achieving Atma-Prapti (union with the divine) or merely to stabilize the mind or to be detached from the subject alone. This is the doubt. This doubt about Samadhi is very natural in the first stage.

Raja Surath asked:
You, who are devoid of all desires, are abandoned by your wife, children, and wealth.
How can your mind be so affectionate towards them! (What a marvel!)

Explanation - Although always being tormented by the behavior of those who are engrossed in worldly desires and are detached from meditation and concentration, and despite the desire to abandon Samadhi and live in the Ashram of Meghasashram, he could not abandon his affection for them. This is the fruit of spending many days together. The nature of Samadhi is to have a dharmic self-realization, while the nature of the mind is to seek desires. Due to this opposition, the powerful mind is defeated in the first attempt at Samadhi, but it cannot abandon the previous affection; because it is only through fickleness and detachment that self-awareness was awakened. Those who are the main helpers in keeping our ego awake, even if they cause obstacles in the practice, are still held with utmost affection.

## Page 207

Vaisya's words
186
Reaching Samadhi in the first state is a great difficult task.
Without being fully immersed in maternal love, without being completely relaxed in body and mind, it is not possible. Even when the life force is awakened (awakened to oneself), one is not capable of renouncing Maya. Otherwise, what part of Sadhana, what part of Yoga, what part of the dharma of the senses, nothing is renounced. This will be made clearer later.
Vaisya said:
Thus, as you have spoken to me, what should I do? My mind does not remain steadfast. 
Commentary - Yogangas had introduced the concept of steadfastness by shaking Samadhi through attachment to objects, but Samadhi can never be such a steadfast state. Because Samadhi is born from the Satva guna; therefore, compassion is its very nature. Even if thousands of people are suffering, it is impossible to increase hatred towards them and keep something in the mind for Samadhi. The second name of Samadhi is Love. The universal, love-filled self-realization, whose purpose it is, cannot be achieved without lovelessness.

## Page 208

Attachment
Those who have abandoned their filial love, greedy for wealth, have left my husband, my beloved family, and my heart is still attached to them.  
Translation - Those sons, wives, and relatives who are greedy for wealth have abandoned my filial love, my husband's love, and my love for my family; my heart is still deeply attached to them.  
Explanation - The natural affection that was towards the father in meditation, towards the husband in contemplation, and towards relatives in the rules of Yama was completely abandoned by them! They kept meditation in a state of triviality, but when he advanced into self-realization, he abandoned his natural attraction. Even while trying to gain knowledge, the root of attachment to them was not destroyed. Thus, to become agitated by contradictory feelings, to be tainted with feelings, and to remain in a temporary state of meditation is very natural; because he is still in the field of intellect. He has not yet been able to enter the field of Brahman very well. Although sometimes he reaches the proximity of the Supreme Being for a moment, he is helpless to descend into the mental field at that moment; for this reason, the previously mentioned mind is naturally inclined towards the materialistic state of meditation.  
How much do I know, even if I know, O great sage,  
That the mind, which is the source of love, even in the company of those who are devoid of virtues,

## Page 209

The Corruption of Intellect
161
Because I cannot stabilize; for their sake, this long breathlessness and the despondency of the mind have appeared. My heart cannot be steadfast towards my own relatives without affection. Explanation - It was previously said that the corrupt actions of the people and the ministers caused the great king Surath to abandon his kingdom, despite coming to the forest of Ban. He began to worry excessively for the kingdom, ministers, people, servants, and treasury. Samadhi is also in a similar state. It was taken away by greedy wives and sons; even then, he is agitated by thoughts of their welfare. Both are in the same state; therefore, affection and love towards each other is natural. Because of this, the Vaishya did not keep any secret about the weakness of his mind and, with a simple life, revealed Surath to Asakoche. When Samadhi is first experienced in the field of intellect along with the Jiva-atma, it is seen in this way; because in the field of intellect, complete purity does not manifest, only entering into wisdom gives freedom from the hands of all kinds of emotions. Intellect is a special, world-facing, determined nature. Although intellect is relatively pure and indifferent to senses, mind, etc., when stimulated by the mind, a large amount of impressions appear one after another, which makes the intellect appear changeable. A person sitting on a fast-moving cart perceives the stationary land on both sides moving, just like this. A constantly changeable mind, bringing one impression after another, illuminates the intellect light; because of this, the stable intellect also appears changeable due to its constant association with the extremely changeable mind. Many lifetimes of accumulated impressions from the world.

## Page 210

162
The Destruction of the Mind
When the living being, abandoning the material field of mind, forgets himself in the pure light of the reflection of Chitabhas and attains a glimpse of certain Samadhi, then he experiences the taste of bliss that has never been experienced before; yet, due to the inability to remain stable for a longer time, he returns to the field of mind. However, he prepares to completely erase the impressions by the mere memory of that experience, but practically he is unable to do so. Seeing his weakness, he becomes extremely frustrated, and with a deep and hot resolve, he plunges deeper into his own marmadah. Alas! For weak-minded beings like me, the attainment of the Mother is far beyond reach—understanding this, the sadhak becomes extremely dejected. On the other hand, why does this happen? The reason is that they are unable to make a decision, and because of this, they become even more weak. In the world, when a person is tormented by the cycle of any severe suffering, if the cause can be understood, then the intensity of that suffering somewhat decreases; but "suffering even without knowing the cause," this is extremely unbearable for a person. Knowing that—Kamini, Kanchan, Vasana, Yama, Niyam, Asana, and other sadhanas will not bring us to Ma; knowing that they are ignorant in isolation; knowing that they are not our well-wishers; knowing that they desire limited sensory pleasures, while we desire—unlimited, unbounded, elevated maternal love, the soul's transcendental, unbounded state of mind and intellect; still, we see that the mind, seeing high hopes and the efforts following them, is determined to keep us in the state of slavery through the help of Dhyana, Dharana, and other limbs of Yoga or the Karma Kanda. We are tormented so much by the excitement of the mind that we forget our invaluable life and our many.

## Page 211

Unmādinī Mā
163

Remember that my pearl has become soft for you, my milk is flowing from my large breasts due to the intensity of motherly love, and for many days I have not been able to show respect to my child, who I have been holding in my lap. Therefore, today, in the guise of Unmādinī, I am descending rapidly from the Truth Realm. Seeker, understand how much agitation I am in for you! For so many days, you did not want me, you wanted subjects - form, taste, sound, touch; because of this, I appeared in the form of your subjects. Concealing my true form with great severity, I fulfilled your senses in the form of subjects. In the form of golden Kamini, how much of your life have I spent?

## Page 213

Markandeya said:
165

Markandeya said:
Then, with those two, the sage Meghas, they sat together.
One was named Samadhi, and the other was the most exalted king, Surath.

They began to speak some stories.
Translation - Markandeya said, "O Brahmin! (Krishnakshtri) After that, Samadhi, the Vaishya, and the most exalted king, Surath, met and sat near the sage Meghas. They sat together in accordance with the scriptures and the rules of yoga, and at an appropriate time, they began to speak some stories.
Explanation - Due to the distraction of the mind, the living being cannot make a decision. With the help of Samadhi, they again took refuge in the light of wisdom and became a seeker of knowledge. Previously, Surath was one, and when Meghas came to him, he did not meet Medhas face to face. Now, with the help of Samadhi, he has become a seeker of yoga. Previously, Surath understood Medhas as a form of memory, a mere flow of knowledge. Now he sees him as a teacher in the form of wisdom. Previously, he thought, "I am Brahma, this is memory, and this is indirect knowledge," and he lived in Medhas' ashram. Now he sees Medhas as the one who removes all doubts, the one who alleviates the heat of the heart, and the one who gives infinite peace.

## Page 215

Thus, there are two kinds of people. One is a famous human being. The other is a famous king, the Seventh King, who is the best among living beings. The term "Seventh" means established in truth. He who has experienced only the Sat-svarupa of Mother Maya, who is saturated with Sanchidananda, "Our Mother," this thought that has been etched in our hearts, in which there is no doubt in the mind of the Astik-buddhi, he is the Seventh. This is not at all something to be neglected. Only Astik-buddhi is the real essence of Sadhana. This essence, the more one has, the more benefits one reaps. "I do not know anything about Mother, her form, qualities, love, respect, greatness, etc. We only know that Mother is a person" - from this thought, such a belief should arise that no number of injuries, attacks, doubts, and debates can shake our truth-knowledge, our realization of existence. With such a feeling, he who is established in the Sat-svarupa Sadhana is the Seventh King, that is, he has the right to the best position among all the beings on Earth. Along with him, he sat down with righteous and appropriate conduct. The word "Yathanya" means methodically, and the word "Yatha" means 'as appropriate'. How conduct becomes righteous and appropriate, here is a glimpse of it. When one is in the presence of the Guru, this feeling should arise in the mind that we are present before the Lord himself. ## Page 217

The Practice of Being a Student
166
Your presence itself illuminates the heart. Leave aside all kinds of vision, grudges, and laughter, and always keep your ears open to listen carefully to every word that comes out of his holy mouth. The Guru, a blissful great soul, always shines with a simple, childlike demeanor; sometimes, this can make someone laugh, and you should laugh along with him so that your fickleness is revealed. The great truth is that with a steady, calm mind, you should be ready to listen, be disciplined, be well-mannered, and be obedient; these five qualities are the illuminating qualities of a student.
Today, in the country, there is a contrary attitude that no one wants to accept the practice of being a student; instead, they want to sit and become a teacher first. In the practice of being a student, when one becomes proficient, everything is gained. People have forgotten this; listen, when one becomes proficient in being a student, even if one becomes a teacher, liberation is inevitable. The practice of being a student was done by famous sages like Satyakama, Upamanyu, Aruni, Ved, and Kautsa. There is also a bright example in the Mahabharata - the son of a Chandala, Ekalavya. Disparaged by his guru, Dronacharya, he established a guru-murti of mud and learned to use an unprecedented weapon, which one day made even the most skilled archer, Arjuna, bow his head before him. It is fortunate to practice the practice of being a student! First, create the posture of a guru in your heart. Gather the qualification to receive the gift of being a student. You should not be afraid of the guru; the absence of the guru does not exist! The guru always remains in your expectation, waiting for you to come and serve you. You should not be confused by the thoughts of the guru; instead, see if you are a student or not. The guru can be anyone. It is said in the Bhagavata that Dattatreya, the avadhoot, was a student.

## Page 218

200
Var Varan
were teachers to all - animals, birds, and even the most learned. Therefore, seeking discipleship is the ultimate Sadhana.
Look at what Hindu women do in their homes! For ten to eleven years, they stay in their father's house, nurtured with maternal and paternal love, spending time with their siblings, relatives, and other close friends. Then one day, in a moment, an unknown person arrives, and even a meeting with four friends at night is not possible.
The priest Mahashaya utters four Sanskrit verses. After night, at dawn, he leaves the woman, abandoning her previously known mother, father, brother, and sister, to go with that unknown man. She understands in her heart that he is our ultimate goal. He is our path for this life, and whoever he is, he is an essential part of us; but no one else is as dear to us. She has never seen before the person with whom she goes, whether he is blind or deaf, foolish or wise, a sage or a laborer; without any thought, without any doubt, he is our ultimate treasure. How beautiful this moment of transformation is! What a severe Sadhana it is! It is astonishing to see it with understanding. Why does it happen? How is such a drastic transformation possible? There is no reason for it. That girl had been practicing the Sadhana of marriage for many days and had achieved success. She knew that I would become someone's wife. Whatever he may be, he would be my ultimate preference. Due to the result of such a belief for many days, such an abrupt transformation is possible. Exactly in this way, arrange the seat of the Guru in your life. Become a disciple. One day, such an opportunity will come that you will leave to think about your Guru.

## Page 219

Guru Labh
201

Astu, the jiva realizes the direct experience of samadhi through the fruits of many births' good deeds and can understand the absence of one in the other. What absence? The absence of knowledge. How much severe tapasya the jiva has to undergo to obtain a bouquet of knowledge. Normally, the jiva who understands glory does not understand desire, and the one who understands desire cannot grasp glory. The knowledge that dispels the problem of glory and desire is obtained by approaching a guru in the form of wisdom or pure understanding. In the heart of every jiva, the guru shines eternally in the form of a guru. He is the ultimate divine person. Until the jiva realizes the guru present in the heart, he cannot find the true center of peace. Until the external human form of the guru is revealed as the divine form of knowledge, the true guru is not obtained. After obtaining the guru, the jiva no longer has any fear, his liberation is certain. With the help of mind yoga, one can reach this heart-based guru. A little. ## Page 221

Verse 203
Commentary: With the help of Samadhi, the Jivatma approached the Bodhimanda Guru and first addressed him as "Bhagwan." It is clarified here that the disciple should see the Guru with a particular bhava. In spiritual vision, it is also seen that when the Jivatma is near wisdom, he is bound to call the Guru "Bhagwan"; because wisdom is Brahman. Guru and Brahman are one; therefore, calling the Guru "Bhagwan" is extremely natural in that state. In the practical world too, when a disciple is near the Guru, he should also see the Guru in the form of a direct Ishwar. The use of the word "Bhagwan" in the Mantra is for this purpose. With the help of thought and discrimination, visualizing the Guru in the form of Ishwar is a sign of lower authority. Accepting the Guru in the form of a simple child in the heart is appropriate when one sees the Guru's form or remembers or hears his name. Just as we call our mother "our mother" even when she is a widow, this shows a simple, invisible truth relationship; similarly, the Guru, whether he is or not, is our Ishwar, our path in this life, the creator, sustainer, and destroyer of the entire world, appearing only to bestow grace on us in human form. It is possible that he is the Guru of many people, but we do not need to see this; he is our Guru-Brahman. This has to be realized, not just imagined; in reality, no one other than Bhagwan can be a Guru. If a living being, a human being, considers himself a Guru, he abandons the path of knowledge (becoming a Guru or a beast); because he is ignorant. Each Mantra of the Guru Gita can be understood by reading the text that the Guru is Brahman.

---

## Page 222

204
Characteristics of a Guru
Who is a Guru? The human body is merely the seat of a Guru, just as the throne is the seat of the queen. The seat on which the queen reigns is also revered by us. The body that serves as the abode of the Guru's power is also revered by us. A Guru is a person. Never criticize anyone's Guru; because your Guru and my Guru are not different. The external appearance of a Guru, whether Vaishnava, Shaiva, or Shakta, is not a distinguishing mark. Just as there are many chandeliers of glass, all of them contain the same kind of light, but due to the different colors of the chandeliers, the light appears in various colors. Similarly, the same Guru, established in different bases, shines with different feelings for different authorities. Always remember in your mind "Madguruḥ Śrī Jagdguruḥ." Here, the scriptural characteristics of a Guru are mentioned. A person who is well-versed in the Vedas and is a Brahmanishtha is called a Sadguru. Even if one has scriptural knowledge but is not a Brahmanishtha, or if one is a Brahmanishtha but lacks scriptural knowledge, they are not capable of properly removing the ignorance of a disciple. By the power of scriptural knowledge and logical ability, by establishing the identity of life and Brahman, and by the power of Sadhana, they illuminate the disciple's heart fully; these two powers, when manifested with full devotion, are capable of breaking the bonds of many accumulated karmas of the disciple; such a Guru is obtained through great good fortune. A Guru who, according to the rules of Kaula, only gives tantric mantras, also first guides the disciple onto the path of dharma, opening the path of the self-realization of the life; for this reason, they are also revered as direct knowledge of Ishvara. Two types of light are seen and experienced in the Guru lineage: the light of a Guru who, as a mantra-giver, manifests as a divine power and then gives a worldly initiation to the benefit of the life; and the light of a Guru who, as a liberator, gives liberation.

Offering the body to the feet of the revered Shri Guru with complete devotion for the sake of following the guru's instructions is called Tanvapen. Seeing the Lord directly is called Manapen. The fruit of serving and worshipping Lord Shri Ishwar is often indirect; but the fruit of serving and worshipping the unmanifested guru in the body of a human being is both direct and indirect. Therefore, the guru can also be given a higher seat than Lord Shri Ishwar. If the guru is a householder, then wealth, clothing, ornaments, and animals which are his own, all of these should be offered to his feet, which is called Dhanapen. There is no fear! A Brahmanishth guru will not make you a beggar even if he takes your entire property. If he does that, then also bear it with pleasantness! Bah! Without any effort, Brahmajnana does not come! This thing is not at all easy. That which you will become immortal by obtaining, you will enjoy eternal bliss, and remain an aparthiv jeev on earth, that cannot be obtained merely by oral devotion. ## Page 227

The Pain of Sadhana

Through knowledge, we can clearly understand that the world is not ours, our body and senses are not ours, and we can clearly speak and understand this when explaining to others; but during action, we all remain ignorant. What is understood through knowledge cannot be repeatedly transformed into action. Only the sadhaka experiences such a distressed situation. The heart is enlightened by the pure light of the intellect, but even when the world's impressions become completely enlightened, the mind's long-standing attachment to desires does not cease in any way. Our mother enlightens the heart of the sadhaka with the light of knowledge, revealing the eternal nature of things, but he sees it in the same way - that his mind remains as confused as before, confused by body-knowledge, as if he is still a fool. Through knowledge, we can clearly understand that the body is nothing, the world is nothing, impressions are nothing, all of this is just a game of servitude by our mother's will; but the mind remains foolish in this triviality, and there is no way to free it! This is not to say that these faults did not exist before, but they were not so painful then. At that time, they lived in a completely dark place filled with poison from the air of the well, but now the life has entered the Medhas Ashram - with the help of Samadhi, it has been enlightened with pure light, and it has been shown an elevated, infinite, and divine sky. Now, it is not in the first situation anymore! "To renounce, to enjoy, these are the painful ones who remain night and day." In this situation, the disappearance of the power of desire or the joy of fearlessness is the only way to gain happiness, and the absence of both leads to the suffering of life.

## Page 236

218
Jnanaloka
is, that which is subject to objects is called knowledge. O Vasishtha! You who consider yourself knowledgeable, this knowledge is only for living beings. Food, sleep, fear, and sexual desire are common to all living beings. All their knowledge, which is revealed through the connection of objects and senses, your knowledge about kingdoms, or the knowledge of your wife and children in meditation, is also of the same kind, knowledge subject to objects. Without knowing the knowledge that is beyond objects, to consider oneself knowledgeable is mere ignorance.
Here we discuss the nature of knowledge that remains in all ordinary living beings. What is it? Look, the living being goes from one sunrise to another, experiencing three states: waking, dreaming, and deep sleep. Let's take the waking state - this state is only a collection of specific knowledge, like seeing, hearing, eating, playing, earning, etc., which are all kinds of knowledge. Knowledge about form, taste, touch, etc. The same knowledge, with different attributes, appears in various forms. By giving up that attribute part, the unbroken pure knowledge remains, which is the nature of knowledge in the waking state. This is the state in the dreaming state. At that time, only the four functions of the inner organ are active. Even then, activities like seeing remain; therefore, knowledge appears with the attributes of form and taste. By giving up that attribute part, the nature of unbroken pure knowledge is revealed. After that comes the deep sleep state. In this state, all senses, including the inner organ, merge. In some way, there is no light of knowledge.

remains, but when sleep is broken, such a realization arises that "I was sleeping in happiness, but this event happened and I knew nothing." The memory of that which was never experienced before is impossible, therefore, it can be understood that in the three states of being, the living being is situated in knowledge. The absence of knowledge never happens. From day to week to month to year to age to birth to birth, the group of living beings is situated in this one unbroken knowledge. From this, the sages say, "I am knowledge of all beings." But this knowledge is subjective, that is, it is only revealed when it is specific or particular. Knowledge of a particular thing, knowledge of a particular object, knowledge of a particular son, etc., when shaped in form, is obtained from day to night in different forms of this one knowledge. This specific form of knowledge is called subjective knowledge. Now, try to understand this with a calm mind. In the waking, dreaming, and deep sleep states, if you look at the specific aspect of knowledge, by abandoning it and reaching the unbroken one essence of knowledge, that is yours! Oh, do you need to grasp it from someone else? It is yours. From your birth to your death, that is one unbroken knowledge, which is differentiated by many specific forms and is revealed. Sometimes it is the essence of gold, sometimes the essence of dharma, artha, kama, moksha, that is, enjoyment and liberation, these are only different forms of the same knowledge's light. In this way, birth and death are only parts of this knowledge. You are born, exist, and die on the heart of knowledge. The Bhagavad Gita says, "Having gained knowledge, one attains peace not long after."

## Page 239

Shiva Pancavaktra
221
All are established in this knowledge from ignorance; all strive for knowledge, either through knowledge or ignorance; but it is a subject of perception. As long as the world's illusion or the limited nature of knowledge is dear to them, it appears in fragmented form due to the subject of knowledge and sensory involvement. Despite being limited by countless attributes, when divided into categories, only five categories are seen. Through the five senses, five types of knowledge waves always shine within each individual. Understand it as an unbroken ocean of knowledge, with countless waves; our five senses are for grasping those waves. The name of this knowledge is Guru or Shiva. The gross body is the dense manifestation of this knowledge. Knowledge appears in five forms, which are the five faces of Shiva. Here it is also said that no one should think that the five-faced Shiva is a deity. Through the practice of this knowledge, when sincere devotion arises in the devotee's mind, with the intense cold of devotion, it becomes dense like a mountain of silver, emerging as the embodiment of Shiva, with a white complexion, free from all fear, full of joy, five-faced, three-eyed, and as the embodiment of blessings, opening the blind eyes of the practitioner with the grace of compassion. The second name of this knowledge is Chit. We meet this in our Chinnamayi Ma, the Guru who opens our blind eyes, every moment. We grasp it in the inhalation and exhalation of breath, in the operation of the senses; but tell me, has anyone ever shown respect to her by calling her Ma? Now, you are our everything, now without you, nothing remains for us! Please, just stay a little, a humble salutation to you.

## Page 240

222
Knowledge is unbroken.
So go, has it ever been said that you will find it?
It comes, its manifestation occurs at every step!
But we do not honor it. It is left unattended, pursued by a cunning, sharp-eyed gaze, then bound by the pain of love, it returns, and then departs without honor. How many ages have passed like this, now that humans can understand it, will you honor it now! Once, try to meet it through the senses, you will find it. Sit in this expectation, although you may fail many times; but this failure will bring you success. It will not stop coming! Day and night it comes and day and night it goes. Once, with determination, you can obtain it. If you cannot obtain it, then experience its arrival and departure, and sit in the sensory path crying. It comes through this path and goes through this path. If it does not appear when it comes and goes, then its path of coming and going is purified by its footprints. Sit there and cry, your life will be blessed by the dust of this path! It will show you the vision. Surath had some pride in knowledge, this is why the sage removed that pride from him in just one statement, which was the great principle he faced, from which both Surath and Samadhi were blessed. After many ages, we too have been blessed by tasting one of its points. Whatever happens, life usually contemplates in this subject. Until you do not find the unbroken, simple knowledge, how can so many great scholars, how can so many ascetics, how can so many yogis, how can so many powerful ones not be, they are ignorant children. Without this one unbroken knowledge, whatever knowledge is, it is only special knowledge or a play of knowledge. The difference between the ocean and the waves is the same as the difference in knowledge.

## Page 241

Knowledge is unbroken and contains specific (distinctive) knowledge. Until it is attained, man does not have a special superiority over ordinary beings from an spiritual perspective. The sage used the word "sam stasya jantoḥ" to explain this. These waves of the unbroken ocean of knowledge manifest in various forms. This is why the Mantra says, "Vishaya shcha mahabhaga yati chaiva prathak prathak. What is a vishaya? It is an object perceived by the senses. The root 'pin' means bond. That which specifically binds is called a vishaya. Sound, touch, sight, taste, smell, these are the five vishayas. These are perceived by the five kinds of sense organs. It was previously said that knowledge is unbroken. How do the five aspects of this unbroken substance manifest? Just as in the ocean, no matter how many waves rise, they all appear as water, similarly, the five kinds of waves that are parts of the ocean of knowledge should also manifest in the form of knowledge. Even if they do not, why do they have an experience in the form of appearance, taste, etc.? Should they not appear as knowledge, that is, in the form of knowledge of appearance, knowledge of taste, etc.? In response to this expectation, it is said that although such an appearance is indeed true, knowledge is usually perceived in the form of appearance, taste, etc. Because knowledge has a special aspect that is withdrawn or concealed; only the aspect of the special characteristic appears to all beings in a common form. What is the reason for this? We want to play the game of muchness with the desire for joy. This was the intention behind saying this; therefore, unbroken knowledge and one with the taste of nectar should also be experienced in many forms. Until we desire greatly, even if it is one, it appears in many names and forms.
