A tree made of a piece of wood (message) is prepared in various forms, hence it is known by many names and many shapes. Little boys say, "We don't want a round message, we will take a square one." In their view, only its geometrical peculiarity is the subject of attraction or aversion; but a young man sees the same thing in the peculiarity of its form. ## Page 244

226
Arshajnana
The second category is that which is night-blind, meaning it sees during the day.
This category is known as the Mithyavadis. Because they claim that gaining true, ultimate knowledge of Satya (the Absolute Truth) is possible only before this, saying that the subjectified form of Mahashakti, which is always manifest as a wave in the ocean of unbroken knowledge, is false or illusory; therefore, it is not fit for contemplation. Thus, the visible aspect of the world appears to them as night-like, i.e., unknown. The special point is that they attempt to elevate themselves by declaring the world and even Jagadishvara (the Lord of the Universe) as false. However, they are bound to contemplate the world in ignorance, and they are truly night-blind.
The third category has a dual vision completed. They are known as the Satyadarshis (seers of truth). Their practice is to see everywhere the unbroken, undivided Paramatmasattva (Supreme Self). Because of this, they are equally aware of day and night, seeing them as non-different and equal. They can experience ignorance, which is itself a form of the light of knowledge. They can directly perceive the unbroken knowledge, which is unbrokenly powerful, and that unbroken power which, in the form of name, form, and activity, manifests as the living world in the play of bliss. These Rishis are always established in this fundamental principle of all scriptures; therefore, they have a dual vision completed in both day and night, i.e., in knowledge and ignorance, light and darkness. The Bhagavad Gita also has a verse that expresses this same sentiment: "That night is for all beings, in which the sage is awake. In that night, all beings sleep" (21.66). The night that is darkness for all beings is the eternal, self-luminous, and self-aware nature of the sage, who is always awake. They always contemplate in knowledge. And all beings, who are engaged in contemplation in the form of knowledge, are in that night.

This vision has been flowing from time immemorial and will continue to flow for eternity.
Among these three types of seers, there is no delusion in any of their visions, no suffering has arisen in any of their eyes that would cause them to see delusion.
Delusion is our own. Therefore, it is said that all are truth-seers.
Those who are blind to knowledge, seeing only subjects, are day-blind!
In their presence, the Devi shines in the same form. Those who see only through knowledge, declaring subjects false, are night-blind; in their presence.

## Page 246

228
Equal Vision
Mother manifests Herself with the same feeling. And the third one, who sees truth everywhere—where both knowledge and ignorance are equally perceived as indicators of Brahman—Mother has manifested Herself with the same kind of feeling in their presence. This is how it should be understood. The progression of living beings also happens in a similar manner. Initially, you become attached to a subject, and you become happy by touching the dust of the world with your body. Then, you leave the subject and become determined to grasp only pure consciousness. This is the second stage of knowledge. Ultimately, when true knowledge is attained, you see that everything is one—everything is honey. Nothing is to be renounced, nothing is to be grasped. By saying renunciation and dedication, nothing remains; our own infinite blissful existence is fully manifested everywhere. Our Mother is full of eternal bliss. She manifests Her special form through Her own inherent nature. As long as the jiva is satisfied with the service to this solid form and Mother's abundant inherent nature, he remains a daydreamer or a first-class jiva. Secondly, our Mother manifests Her special form through Her consciousness in the form of a living being. When the jiva renounces that inherent form and becomes determined to see only Mother's pure consciousness, he becomes a king or a second-class jiva. Then, those who have found Mother's blissful form see the true form of Mother in knowledge, ignorance, matter, and consciousness everywhere. These are the jivas who have completed the third stage of equal vision, both day and night. Only the jiva attains Brahman by experiencing these three kinds of vision. In this process, one does not renounce the other; that is why no criticism or praise is mentioned in this mantra. In the previous mantra, the unbroken essence of knowledge was mentioned.

## Page 247

All are wise
226
It has been established that wisdom manifests in the living world and is capable of experience, and the sage's intention is to illuminate this in the following mantra.
Man's wise: Truth, but not only that.
Wherever there is wisdom: peace
Animals, birds, deer, etc. ॥ 34 ॥
Translation - O Suratha! Man is wise; this is true; but only they are wise, this is not a statement; because wisdom is present in all living beings like animals, birds, deer, etc.
Explanation - The group of living beings is in only one state of wisdom among three states, this has been said previously with a special characteristic. Wisdom is not only in humans, animals like birds are wise beings. It appears in a subject-oriented manner through food, sleep, fear, sex, etc. In one word, the world is born, exists, and merges only in wisdom. Apart from wisdom, there is nothing else. Whatever appears to us near us in a solid form, it is also a dense state of wisdom. Wisdom appears in the form of the world. Our mother, the wise form, appears everywhere, but other animals like animals, birds cannot experience her; because they have not yet reached such a high and complete development of senses. But the human race has been brought to this field by her, she has provided such a complete offering that he can achieve immortality by directly experiencing that divine form. In reality, this is the ordinary and unbroken wise form of mother in living beings, which man can understand - "They do not burn in fire, nor are they destroyed by weapons. Neither do they quench the fire of desire."

## Page 249

The Knowledge of Man and Animal
231
Animals also eat many times; then they try to eat. They fall asleep when their senses are tired. They are always afraid of death. Even without knowing, the fear of death arises in their hearts when they remember their own death. Apart from this, there is only one activity - the purpose of the senses, which is that they see that the spread of knowledge and science among humans has become widespread and astonishing in all directions due to the greater promotion of Western civilization. From the perspective of the sages, it also appears as animal-like knowledge. That knowledge which is limited to the physical world alone; no matter how much it is degraded, refined, and completed, it is famous as ignorance. The word ignorance means the absence of knowledge or an indescribable something that is the opposite of knowledge. Knowledge that is manifested from the desire to know is called ignorance. When the unbroken natural knowledge, which is the same as mentioned earlier, is manifested in a limited form, that is, with names and forms, then it is called ignorance. In this ignorance or partial knowledge, both animals and humans are equal. The senses of animals are incomplete and devoid of desire; therefore, the knowledge that is manifested in them is called ignorance in the eyes of humans. Humans are more advanced in terms of actions, so knowledge is also manifested with a refined and elevated state of mind. In reality, both are manifestations of knowledge; therefore, they are only ignorance. This is the perfect junction between the human and animal planes. On one side is the divine plane, and on the other is the animal plane. A human becomes a human only when they become complete, just as a god becomes complete and an animal becomes complete. This completeness is only a part of knowledge; therefore, it is said in the science of ethics - "Ignorant are the animals, equal to them are the wise." We have as many divine and goddess forms as we have, and most of them are animals, birds, and other reptiles. Hinduism is the science of knowledge.

---

## Page 250

232
The Mystery of the Vehicle
This is a beautiful and unique mystery. It would be highly inappropriate to discuss the matter of the vehicle here. The divine power that is established and controlled above the animal power is called the vehicle of that deity. Let us take Ganesha, the first Siddhi-granter, as an example. His vehicle is the mouse. The Atharvashirsha's Sayana commentary states: "The mouse consumes and takes away the fruits of actions, hence it is called the mouse." Until the powerful obstructive form of the fruit of action remains, Siddhi is not attained. This is why Siddhi is established on the fruit of action being taken away. When a human being reaches a state where they want to destroy the obstructive form of the fruit of action without enjoying it, then they become a mouse-dharma. Only when one is fit for the attainment of the ultimate Siddhi, which is in the form of Brahma-jnana, does the life subtly begin to cut away its extremely harsh fruits of action. That is, a human being becomes blessed by attaining ultimate Siddhi by becoming a mouse-dharma. Similarly, the vehicle of Lakshmi is the mongoose. The mongoose is blind, meaning it is blind to Atmajnana; that is, it is a mongoose-dharma. As long as the life remains a mongoose-dharma, it worships the Brahmanic power that is the controller of material happiness, which is heavy with both knowledge and ignorance. Or, our wealth goddess Lakshmi's form is established above blind animals. Saraswati's vehicle is the swan. Saraswati is Brahma-vidya. The one who attains Siddhi through the Ajapa mantra day and night is called a swan-dharma. A human being, with a healthy body, chants "Hamsa" (swan) in the form of the Ajapa mantra, day and night, 21,600 times. A human being cannot become a swan-dharma until they do not experience this natural chanting; for this reason, Brahma-vidya is the key.

## Page 251

Garuda Principle
233
It is not known who is more. Apart from this, the special dharma of the swan is that, after mixing with water, it discards the water and retains the milk. Similarly, when a human being is capable of extracting the essence of knowledge from the material world, then only does he become accomplished after acquiring Brahman Vidya. This is why Saraswati is seated on the swan's head.
Garuda is the vehicle of Vishnu. It is said in the twelfth canto of Shrimad Bhagavata - "Nivrit Vedah Suparnastu Yajnam Vahati Purusham. The Vedas are the Garuda bird, which carries the Yajna (sacrifice) to the Purusha (man). Vishnu, the all-encompassing consciousness, is the giver of liberation. Knowledge and action are the dual means of achieving the all-encompassing deity of Vishnu. Yoga Vasishtha says - "By the two wings of the bird, just as the bird flies in the sky, by the two means of knowledge and action, one attains the supreme state. Only through action, not through knowledge, does one attain liberation. But we become liberated through both means of action and knowledge." Just as the bird is capable of traversing the sky through its two wings, similarly, the sadhakas attain the supreme state of Vishnu through the dual means of knowledge and action. Liberation is not achieved through action alone or through knowledge alone; but it is achieved through both. Therefore, these dual means are the means of sadhana (1). When the jiva acquires the knowledge of the karma-kanda of the Vedas, there will inevitably be a doubt in someone's mind that if knowledge and action alone are the means of liberation, where is the place for bhakti? This will be explained in detail later, but here is a brief analysis of its meaning. What is meant by bhakti? Aren't knowledge and action impossible without bhakti? Or, in today's times, when books like "Worship your mother and father" are widely promoted, isn't there a lot of promotion of scriptures that teach devotion to God?

## Page 253

The Bull and the Lion Principles

By acquiring the knowledge of this unbroken truth, the ominous fear of death is always kept away. This is why it is called Shiva or Mangala. The word Bull means Dharma. The rise of the virtuous quality of Shubhr Satva leads to the development of Dharma; this is why the Bull is white. The Bull has four legs. Tapas, Shauca, Daya, and Dana are the forms of the four legs of Dharma. When a human being acquires the capability to practice this four-legged Dharma as much as possible, only then does he gain the vision of Shiva or the Guru; this is why the Bull (calf) has the establishment of Shiva on its back.
Durga's outer form is the Lion - violence is the main principle of the Lion. The person who is capable of violence in his life, that is, who tries to develop Brahmatva by dissolving life, is called a Lion Dharma. The Lion is the king of beasts, and the human being is the best among beasts. The point is that when a human being becomes capable of transforming from the lordship of beastly nature to true humanity, only then is he called a Lion Dharma. In such beings, our mother, the compassionate and benevolent form of Durga, is manifested; this is why our mother is called the Lion's outer form.
If we were to write a book about the outer forms of all the deities, its size would increase greatly.

The feeling of the Chitmati Mahatva Shakti when it becomes abundant in the devotee's bhakti, as if it gains light in a specific form, is what we have discussed here. How can I explain it? What is the nature of that form, that sweet form of that ocean of Chaitanya-Ananda? It is a divine light! It bewilders the mind with unparalleled beauty! It is the same affection as Prana! Can it be described in a picture? Indeed, now we are approaching the subject we have proposed; both humans and ascetics are usually equally endowed with subject-oriented knowledge. There is a Mantra before this statement. It is said in the first chapter of the Para that the knowledge that is dear to humans is also dear to animals. What kind of knowledge is this? The subject-oriented nature of knowledge was mentioned in the first part; repeating it causes repetition. Especially, looking at the meanings of several Mantras written later, it becomes clear that the goal here is to focus on Jnanapriya. The conclusion is that the knowledge that is dear to humans is also dear to other species. What is the meaning of the word Jnanapriya? That knowledge which, though unbroken and self-luminous, appears in different forms through various impressions, is specific knowledge that is suitable for the perception of humans and other living beings. This part of knowledge is dear to humans, and similarly, other species also find it dear. Above this specific knowledge is the knowledge of 'I' or Aham. This 'I' knowledge is the cause of love. Only the living being loves itself the most. 'I' is as dear to us as anything else in this world. Humans and animals are equally subject to the fear of death. From this, it is clear that

---

## Page 255

Equality of Man and Animal
237
Love for oneself is the same in all. Why are living beings so afraid of death? They forget the knowledge "I am." Whatever exists in the world is for this one point of knowledge. Food, sleep, earning, and sensory desires are all expressions of that 'I' knowledge. Loving this knowledge brings the fruit of love. Ordinary humans and superhuman beings in the world, whoever has received this much knowledge (that is, whatever portion of knowledge is manifested within the senses), love that portion more and are diligent in fully protecting it. "Tulyamanyatthobhayoḥ" - this is the remaining part of the Mantra. Otherwise, in other words, something other than knowledge. Although nothing exists anywhere without knowledge, from a common perspective, something other than knowledge is understood as a solid, inert thing. In living beings, there are only two parts. One is knowledge, and the other is the inert body formed by the union of earth and the like. In the commentary of the Vedanta, this part is called ajnana. It was first said that both man and animal are equal in the part of knowledge. Then it was said that both are equally dear to this knowledge. Now the sage says that the other, that is, apart from the part of knowledge and whatever else exists, man, animal, bird, etc., are all equal. In the real, living world, only two things are seen and experienced - knowledge or consciousness, and the other is inert or unconscious. Both are equally present in all living beings. Some interpret this part of the Mantra in another way. They say that the meaning of the word 'other' is undivided knowledge. That is, what is always shining everywhere - no sense organ can perceive it; it is the same as the taste of self-knowledge. This part is unattainable for ordinary humans as well as for animals, that is, both are blind in the matter of knowledge.

---

## Page 256

238
Food Offering
However, we do not like this interpretation. The reason is that in the human world, anyone can gain knowledge; but in the animal world, no one can.
Thus, now we discuss Medhaswaky's brief analysis. Meghas says, "Oh Suratha! The knowledge you claim to have, think about it once; you do not have the qualification for that pride. You are a human, it is fine to say that you have knowledge; but that knowledge is limited by the connection with the senses. The knowledge of animals and birds is also revealed in the same limited way. You consider your conditioned fragment of knowledge as 'I' and love it. Animals and birds also love their own knowledge or 'I'. Apart from this knowledge, whatever exists is rigid, physical body or ignorance; even in that part, there is no difference between humans and animals."
Even if they see these, the birds, in their delusion, offer food to their chicks, even when they are hungry.
Anuvad - Oh Suratha! See, even if they remain attached to self-love knowledge, these birds, even when they are severely hungry from drinking nectar, offer food to their chicks with great respect.
Vyaakhyana - Even the lower species love their own the most. Hunger has come, they are satisfied by eating, but without doing so, they put food in the mouths of their chicks in their mouths. Humans, on the other hand, hope for a return; that is why, even suffering their own pain, they take care of their children. Other living beings do not even have that hope. Then why do they do this?

---

## Page 257

Self-satisfaction is the root.
236
From this, there is an unobserved (hidden) self-satisfaction. The satisfaction you get from eating is less than the self-satisfaction you get from sowing a seed. This is why beings act in this way. There is some attachment and ignorance in this. Therefore, the word 'moha' (attachment) is mentioned in the Mantra. The being does not know that, in reality, it is loving itself. Whatever one does in the world is for self-satisfaction. In the Brihadaranyaka Upanishad, Yajnavalkya the Brahmarshi has said - "A wife does not love her husband for his satisfaction, but her own satisfaction comes from loving her husband; therefore, she loves her husband. Similarly, a husband does not love his wife for her satisfaction, but he becomes happy by loving his wife; therefore, he loves his wife. A father does not love his son for his satisfaction, but he loves his son for his own satisfaction. In this way, everyone loves each other for their own satisfaction, but no one loves anyone for their own satisfaction." This is called knowledge. The person who knows this, understands it, and experiences it is a knower. He loves everyone; he helps everyone; but he knows that I love myself. Until this soul does not find itself in Ma or in itself, even if he remains in knowledge, his attachment does not go away; therefore, it is said in the Mantra, 'even in knowledge, there is attachment.' The bird, even when hungry, puts food in its mouth in the cage of the hawk without knowing, and it is deluded. Similarly, a human being is also deluded when he thinks in his mind that I am nourishing my wife and son to satisfy them, or I am doing good deeds for the world's benefit.

## Page 258

240
Hope for Recompense
It should be understood that his attachment has not been removed. This matter will be elaborated further.
Humans, like Manu, are devoted to their children.
Driven by desire, they hope for recompense. What do you not see? 
Translation - O, noble human! Humans are devoted to their children, meaning they are affectionate. Those who hope for recompense due to desire, do you not see this?
Explanation - Animals, birds, etc., which are carnivorous, are affectionate towards their children. They are indifferent to recompense, but in the future, when these offspring grow up, they will protect us. They do not nurture this hope in their hearts, yet they endure great hardships to protect their offspring; because protecting offspring is their natural (inherent) nature. Humans too, due to this natural affectionate nature towards offspring, are devoted to their children; but the desire for recompense from offspring is also hidden within them. This is what is special. Compared to carnivorous species, the human species is much elevated in terms of knowledge; they also see a little of the future. Ordinary humans hope for earthly benefits from their children; and those who are relatively advanced hope for otherworldly or spiritual benefits. But attachment is in both. Whether hoping for recompense or being indifferent to recompense, they are entangled in the form of a son or a wife. They do not know this.
Sadhak! You have come from a distant land and are feeding sweet milk to your child. The child happily eats it! At that time, have you ever looked at your chest with your hand and seen how completely satisfied you are in your heart? Who are you in the form of a son?

## Page 259

The Plan for the Cultivation of Self-love
241
O Mother of the Self! Who are you in the form of dear companions? O Mother of the Self! Who are you in all forms? You are our Mother of the Self. I am adorned with many emotions in the form of wife, son, etc. I wanted to play the game of greatness, and therefore, I serve in many forms. I am radiant as Vishnu in the form of the universe. Without knowing this, without recognizing you, without remembering me, what are you doing? Service to wife and son! That is "My service alone!" Until you do not worship me by saying "Salutations to the many-formed Vishnu, the Supreme Self," even if you worship me, it will be the worship of another deity in action. This is called ignorance, this is called attachment.
Listen, on the boundless ocean of consciousness, a few waves have been gathered and a counterfeit wife has been seated on them; she is the first mother of ignorance. When I see in the purest part of knowledge, I appear as a vast ocean of knowledge alone, and self-awareness blossoms. The various waves of the ocean of knowledge have risen like wife, son, etc. This world, which is saturated with self-consciousness, not knowing it is called attachment. This attachment, which is the result of love born from the hope of reciprocation of ignorance, what is the result? The result is knowledge. Ignorance or Eshat gives rise to the complete, boundless knowledge. How is this possible? Suppose you love your son thinking he is a son; in action, a special impression of your love is prepared. After a few days, when your son is absent, an eternal feeling of love is presented in your heart, and that has not disappeared! In this way, it is everywhere in the world. By loving small things, becoming bewildered by the complexity of the world, and being attached only to names and forms, the living being accumulates love and affection. ## Page 260

Knowledge and love are one,
that we love ourselves. Only then does the life become self-satisfied, self-playful, and self-mate, bathing in the unbroken ocean of love. Ordinary people's belief is that what we love in this world of the unenlightened, this is our bond, without being free from this bond, there is no hope of getting God. There is no fear, if you think that you are helpless in giving up attachment towards the world, you should not understand that getting Maya is very far for you. "You count this as a bond, but you will see it as a liberator later. What you call a bond now, after some days you will see that it is the means of liberation. Oh! First prepare an object from the name of love - then you will see that you have loved it. Love is its very form! Love is the very form of our mother's joy. There is no difference between love and the lover there! Start spreading yourself free, then you can experience the form of love. The form of love is self-donation. Donate to the love-filled great Maya. Then you will see that you have drowned in the ocean of love. For the practice of love - to become a lover, you should not do any kind of ritual or attribute love to an imagined person, etc. What love is in a person? Can it not be? From one thing.

## Page 261

7
Mamatavat

Understand this - what was previously said about the ocean of knowledge is not just a dry ocean of knowledge, it is an ocean of love. Knowledge and love are one and the same!
Many seekers, who do not find love in God, think it is a cause of sorrow. But Mother who is full of love takes us on a different path. We understand that the attempt to have faith in God, who naturally has love for us, is more fruitful than the effort to seek love in God! Seeker! Think a little. Yet, the delusions of ego are destroyed. By the power of Mahamaya, who is the cause of the world's existence. ||38||
Translation - (In reality, all are dear to the Self). Yet, due to the influence of Mahamaya, the cause of the world's existence, living beings are deluded in the cycle of ego and fall into the trap of attachment.
Explanation - It was previously said that are all beings, both humans and animals, ignorant of their true nature, dear to the Self. In the human race, love for children like sons is understood as a special form of self-love due to the hope of reciprocation. But in reality, humans love themselves and serve themselves; the satisfaction of their desires is the only goal of all living beings. Although this is the true reality; yet, in loving themselves, the living being gets confused about 'I'; some things become 'ours'. This 'ours' word is the cause of so much ego. That is, 'ours' means 'mine', and when the suffix 'ta' is added to the meaning of 'mine', the word 'ego' is formed. That is, the meaning of the word 'ego' - 'mine', 'ours' - this feeling. Ego is a cycle, like the cycle of water in a whirlpool; just as grass and other things fall into the whirlpool and keep spinning around and fall into the same place.

---

## Page 262

244
Ajneya Moh
remains, similarly, in this cycle of mamata, humans also
mostly remain stuck in the same place. For many days, caught in this cycle of mamata, that is, in the knowledge of "my home," "my son," "my wife," "my body," etc., humans fall into the trap of Moha. They mostly see that the water, after spinning for a long time, takes the shape of a pot (garth). The grass and other things that initially spin with the water's froth ultimately merge into the same water hole (chidr). The same is the case with life. For a long time, by saying 'mine' and 'mine,' ultimately, they lose the subject 'I'; this is called Moha. This Moha is similar to the pot. When a human cannot find 'me,' then he becomes completely engrossed in Moha; then he becomes 'nar' naraka. The word 'naraka' is established by adding the suffix '-ka' to the word 'nar.' When a human becomes big-small - extremely entangled, then he goes to naraka. Just as no object falls inside the pot and is not visible from outside, similarly, after separating from 'our world' and 'our family' due to this feeling, life becomes so confused that it cannot see who 'I' am. Day and night, saying 'I, I,' still does not know who 'I' am - this is called Ajnana - this is called Moha. Due to this Ajnana, he again and again wanders in the cycle of the world. For many days, caught in this cycle of mamata, we have made the world so ours that we consider it cursed if even a little of the world (home) is lost. - Even if the soil from the front of the house falls, it remains covered. Such a situation has come; but they do not understand that even if everything in the world is lost, 'I remain eternal.' This world - this body, senses, mind, prana, and intellect, all these are mine.

## Page 264

The Power of Mahamaya
Stability is necessary. Without being enchanted, this world's game does not run. How can a game of hide-and-seek be played with eyes closed! Our true form gains realization in a moment of enlightenment, then this world is no longer a game. From this, Medhas says that the living being, enchanted by this, should not experience sorrow or suffering. Oh, how am I an unrighteous living being! I could not free myself from enchantment even with effort! I will not gain self-realization now, from this feeling, I will not be unhappy. No matter how much enchantment there is, understand that it is the power of Mahamaya. You are enchanted by her will. By her will, someone is virtuous, someone is sinful. On the stage, those who perform, whether the king or the fool, have no difference. In the first act, both were made into beggars, in the next act, perhaps they perform as Gaur Nita. Among them, there is no small, no big, no sinner, no virtuous, no beloved, no hated; all are the same, all are part of our Mahamaya's game.

Who is Mahamaya! Before this, much about her has been hinted at. Here, we will try to discuss that matter. The one who, as a witness to the three states of waking, dreaming, and deep sleep, said that all living beings are ordinary, unbroken, and universal knowledge, how can we understand that knowledge! By looking at the waves - through the subject. By form - taste, the subject one after another understands the existence of that knowledge. When we become detached from the subject, that is, when we become subtle, then the existence of knowledge is not experienced. Therefore, understand that - even if knowledge is self-luminous in nature, until it is shaped by the subject, it is not manifested near us.

## Page 265

Great Power
247
It is. These topics are merely the flow of power, which has already been described earlier.
Scholars refer to this flow of power as gross, referring to the effects of birth, death, and other transformations. For example, at the time of birth, one overcomes the opposing force of existence by maintaining one's identity, gains growth, achieves perfection, and ultimately reaches the state of destruction, meaning it gradually diminishes and finally disappears. These six transformations occur in every substance every moment. These six transformations are also briefly described as creation, preservation, and destruction, which are three types of actions. Therefore, when we talk about the world, we should understand that it is a manifestation of a great power. If you take a fruit or a flower in your hand and observe it, you will see that the creation, preservation, and dissolution of the form are flowing in it. This same form exists everywhere in the world.
Here, one may wonder that power is not a static substance but is always changing and flowing. Then, how do we perceive this world as static? We solve this through an example. If you take a piece of wood and burn one of its edges with fire and then quickly rotate it, a straight, stable line appears before you. The stability and identity of the world are also the same; that is why the form, which is merely a flow of power, appears so static. This power, despite appearing as an infinite variety in the visible universe, is essentially one. Seeing the diversity in light, the assumption of diversity in power is not in harmony. The same power manifests in countless ways, such as shining light, causing a fan to rotate, performing printing, running a tram, and so on.

---

## Page 266

258
All are truth-seers.
In this varied world, one and the same power is always manifest.
This power is in the previously mentioned state of knowledge and desire. It is through this power that knowledge is revealed. These two things are the subject of as many thoughts of the philosophers in the world. One is knowledge and one is power. Are these two one or different? Sankhya says they are different; knowledge is inactive, desireless, and conscious Purusha; and power is inert, productive Prakriti. It is only through the discrimination of Prakriti-Purusha that Purusha, united with Prakriti, attains the state of being free. Union with Prakriti is the bond or the feeling of being a living being for Purusha. This is the doctrine of duality. Vishishtadvaita says that Prakriti and Purusha are not different. Prakriti is merely the power or part of Purusha. The difference between a limb and the body is the same as this. For Purusha, to be face-to-face is liberation, and his opposition is bondage. According to Vedanta, knowledge and power are absolutely identical substances. There is no difference between power and the powerful; but a part of that power is called Maya; it is false, like a spider's web. It has no real existence; it is only the ultimate truth of knowledge. The power that is revealed from the world is like a rope snake, only an illusion. This is the current non-dual doctrine. All are truth-seers. Only the sadhaka experiences this. First, the doctrine of duality, then the doctrine of qualified non-duality, and then the doctrine of absolute non-duality, where truth and falsehood are united. But ultimately, the sadhaka is initiated into the knowledge of the Upanishads or the teachings of the sages. That is the complete and wondrous vision. Earlier seers like Kashyapa and the Brahmarshis of the Vedic era reached this doctrine. Even the Pramana Upanishads, after reading them slowly, remain steadfast in this doctrine when explaining their simple meaning. The loving incarnation Shri Gauranga Deva has also said - "Adwaita Jnana Tatva Brajendrananda." Apart from this, they have said.

## Page 267

Aristotelian Philosophy
246
By discussing the topic of 'Infinite Diversity in Unity', the true nature of Knowledge and Power has been revealed. That which is beyond the realm of speech and mind, that which is situated outside the scope of intellect, cannot be defined by saying, "It is this and nothing else." What is it? Who knows? One can only speak about what they experience. Indeed, we understand that Knowledge and Power are inseparable from all existence. Each atom in the unbroken ocean of Knowledge is itself Power. From this, it is known that the sages have depicted this through the metaphorical language of Power. When the light of Power does not shine, then it is known by names such as Prajnabrahman, Niranjana, etc. Power is Knowledge, or Knowledge is Power, or both are one, but this cannot be expressed through language. However, it is certain that Knowledge and Power are not two separate things, but one entity. When the Power of Creation, Preservation, and Destruction manifests from the Unmanifest, then it is known as Saguna Brahman. No middle delusion is expressed in these words! The ancient texts (Upanishads) do not use these words. As we think and think, we have gone very far. We know - She is our mother. Her name is Mahamaya. The world is sustained by Her influence. The world is played out by our ego (attachment) in the form of mother. Even in the form of a fallen being in attachment, She is the same. Those who see Her in this form are bound by nothing; when there is no bond, then what is liberation? We know that we are born from the womb of the Mother, sitting in Her lap, we are an innocent naked child in the lap of Mahamaya. This is what happens when we think about 'Knowledge and Power' for so long.

O Jagatvimohini Ma,

## Page 270

Whether it is Mouni Baba or a sanyasi who resides in a cave or a Nirjana Maharanee, no one can escape the influence of Mahamaya. Oh brother, as long as the body exists, Mahamaya exists; Videha Kaivalya will come one day. Where there is no return after going, that is our Mother's ultimate abode. There, on the day she wishes, she will take you. Before that, no one has the right to go there. And what is the need to go? See the joy in Mother's game by staying in Mother's abode! So far, you have played your own world. You have played the game of the world as a creator. How many troubles you have faced, how much dust has stuck to your body. Now you have known Mother, you have found Mother, stay in Mother's abode and play in Mother's game. At that time, you were the creator, now Mother is the creator. Now you will not face any trouble, no dust will stick to you. Then what is the fault in playing? Why are you busy in giving up?

Those who have reached a high level in Sadhana, that is, those whom Mother has specially understood and shown her form, do not fear this 'Baladakarshana', they do not become sad or angry; but the first - the state of the aspirant who enters this is extremely difficult. Suppose someone starts with some confusion, worship, or some such activity, in which they can remain somewhat detached for some time, but cannot remain fully detached, then 'Baladakrsya' attracts the mind with force and makes it subject-oriented. Or if you firmly resolve that you will not do any other work except good work throughout your life; but even there, you will see that someone forcefully makes you deviate from your resolve. Mother is like this.

---

## Page 273

Creation, Renunciation, and Manifestation
255
Only that attraction, which is powerful, is the attraction of Maya. Just see this, this is what is special to be known in this Mantra.
The world, mobile and immobile, is created by it. It becomes happy and generous to women, who are liberated. ||41||
Translation - This world, which is both static and dynamic, ever-changing, is created by it. Only when it becomes extremely close in the form of happiness and generosity do humans become eligible for liberation.
Explanation - Many commentators have given various meanings to this Mantra by seeing the repetition of the words mobile, immobile, world, and universe. We will not go into the circle of such interpretations. Mobile is transient, immobile is stationary; that is, static and dynamic. World = ever-changing, universe - which always stays in the Mother's lap. This static and dynamic, which always moves in the universe, enters into the unmanifested state, it is its creation. In this Mantra, the word 'srishti' (creation) has three activities - creation, manifestation, and dissolution. To understand these three activities, these three words have been used. Mobile and immobile - creation, world - manifestation, and universe - dissolution is the illuminator. There is also a mystery within creation. The word 'srishti' means manifestation and renunciation, therefore, understand the world as the renunciation of the world. The previous world was in an invisible state, as a cause - it was in the form of a seed in the Mother's womb. The world became visible from that invisible state, became a cause, became a tree from the seed form. This is also called renunciation or creation. In the Gita too, "Create from the essence of the past, manifestation is a little condensed, exactly this is described." Here we will briefly discuss the relationship with the nature of creation - from nature to mahat, from mahat to ego, etc., in the sequence of the results of nature.

---

## Page 274

256
Brief Creation Mystery
Creation as described by Samkhya, or creation from the Atman to Akasha, Akasha to Vayu, etc., is not considered a special benefit for the first group of aspirants. It is helpful for those who wander in the kingdom of knowledge. However, let us attempt to understand the nature of creation from another perspective.
Earlier, we spoke about unbroken knowledge and great power, which is nothing but a mere wish. The desires of each being, such as the desire for wealth, children, objects, and happiness, are minor and specific aspects of this wish. When these specific aspects are removed from the wish, a great, universal wish appears. Unbroken knowledge or Chit-shakti is embodied in this great wish. From this non-dual great wish, the manifestation of various emotions is conceived. This conception is the world. Conception is the dharma of the mind. When the Niranjana, Nirvikalpa, Chaitanya Maya appears in the form of Manomaya or Ichchhamaya, then she becomes the manifest creation. If we can show our own emotions to someone outside, then we can create such a creation, but we cannot do so because our mind is entangled within the limits of life by making independent conceptions from the mind of Maya or the Virat Man. However, when the Manomaya, Ichchhamaya, Mahamaya Maya conceives something, that same kind of emotion becomes dense there; therefore, it appears in a gross form. Many practices such as shaping a clay figure, moving hands and feet, and collecting clay are required to create a figure from matter. However, when we create a figure using the mind, no effort is required, nor is any material needed.

---

## Page 275

Why is this creation
心
awake in the midst of the high praise of thousands of spectators, wandering on the streets of the magnificent city? In this state, as if the elephant, the spectators, the magnificent city, the streets, etc., are nothing but your imagination; yet, at the time of seeing a dream, it appears so directly, so grossly, that what cannot be experienced by mere imagination is manifested in it. This is the essence of creation. It is said that She is herself Sat-chit-ananda; therefore, in this creation or manifestation of Her, Her existence, consciousness, and bliss are clearly manifested. Then, a special quality is that She is Herself non-dual; therefore, every element of this creation is non-dual. Two elephants, two leaves, even two particles of a boulder are not alike. O seeker! Once walk and see, everywhere our Mother is manifest in Her non-dual Sat-chit-ananda form. Whatever be the greatness or meanness, why should She not shine? The non-dual Sat-chit-ananda form of this Mother has never been shaken. This world is our Mother's gross form. What will those see who cannot call it Mother, who cannot see it even after calling it Mother? What kind of vision will they have of Mother's world, which is the most subtle, the past, the future, just knowledge, just will, just power? How can one grasp non-manifest Mother, how can one obtain pure consciousness Mother? Let me tell you - this is another matter. Why did She create this? Her will; why is it varied? Her play. One is an outsider, another is external, one is a master, one is a servant, one is a sinner, one is righteous. All these are Her play. She does not give any punishment or reward to anyone. Less fruit, reward, encouragement, protection of sages, destruction of evil, etc.

---

## Page 276

258
Prasana Varada Ma
is the first stage of knowledge. Until one sees her, the life is bound to wander in all these kinds of knowledge; but after the vision of the universe, the seeker sees that "You are dear to me," "I am the essence of all beings," "I am the essence of all beings," "Whatever is, I leave it somewhere else, nothing is." Therefore, how will rewards, punishments, or accolades be given? Suppose righteousness is revealed in your mind, then you do not reward a separate person; or if unrighteousness is revealed, you do not drive him away, because you yourself have become the embodiment of all. This is also the same. Our mother "Sat, Asat, Tat, Param." Creation, as per the results of actions, is depicted through the realistic thought of knowledge. Within the independent play of our mother, countless schemes become manifest by establishing the cause and effect in the tradition. The description of the process of the visionary or the puranic view is given for the discovery of these rules and schemes. Indeed, our mother is the creative Brahma-murti from the movable and immovable, the preservative Vishnu-murti from the form of the world, and the destructive Shiva-murti from the universal form. These three actions of creation, which are combined in a place, are known as Ishvara, Akshar Purusha, etc. These three sentiments were hidden in the form of Mahamaya. To make them luminous, she first abandoned the form of the unmanifest. This abandonment means rejection. This is why in the Tantric Ocean of Causes, the birth of Brahma, Vishnu, and Maheshwar from Mahakali occurs. This is when she, after abandoning the mental form to be revealed only in the pure form of enlightenment, after abandoning the many forms of liberation for men, rises to manifest herself in her form, then only our mother is the most dear to her beloved children in the form of Prasana and Varada.

---

## Page 277

Vidya and Avidya
256
are revealed and remain. And see, this Nityatriti, Nityaprasanna, Nityavaradani Ma is situated within the hearts of living beings for their liberation. This is why the word "Eshaa" in the Mantra indicates extreme proximity. She is the ultimate Vidya, the eternal truth. She is the cause of both bondage and liberation in the world. She is the eternal Ma, the ruler of all and the ruler of Ishvara. ||42||
Translation - This Vidya and Avidya, Parama and Aparama; therefore, both bondage and liberation are her causes. She is the eternal Ma, the ruler of all and the ruler of Ishvara.
Explanation - Mahamaya Ma is our Vidya. Vidya is "Yaya tadaksharamadhigamya" - that which leads to the attainment of the Akshara, which is the knowledge of Brahman. Vidya is of two kinds - Parama and Aparama. The word Avidya does not mean something opposed to Vidya; because Vidya is self-luminous. Nothing can oppose or cover it. Here, the word Eshat is used in the sense of "this". When Vidya appears as a distinct form of Jeeva, then only does Avidya appear as something opposed to Vidya. Here, the word "Sa Vidya" means both Vidya and Avidya. Similarly, the word Parama indicates both Parama and Aparama. This meaning arises from the absence of the a-kara. "Paraman brahmaadi mati iti parama" - Brahma, Vishnu, Maheshwar are also subject to her. Therefore, our Ma is Parama. Apar, that is, the ruler of the living world and the ruler of Ishwari, that is, Maya-infused consciousness (Brahma, Vishnu, Maheshwar), she is the ruler of all three. In one word, Mahamaya is the CCO. Vasishtha Tripathi Collection. Digitized By Siddhanta eGangotri Gyaan Kosha

## Page 278

260
Bondage and Liberation
All is this. Beyond both is Ishvara and the world. Mahamaya alone is the destroyer of Kshara and Akshara, the Purushottama, the Jiva is Ishvara, the Brahma, the Man is Prana, the Atma, the Jnana is Jneya, and the Jnana, in these three distinct forms, shines as the light of the Atma. This Mantra speaks of both liberation and the bond of the world; therefore, some discussion is necessary regarding its relationship. Until the Jiva does not experience the reflection of pure consciousness, it cannot think of the bond of the world. When the Mantra is revealed within the mind, intellect, senses, etc., accepting the condition of a Sudra, that smallness - the limitation - which is some kind of suffering until the Jiva does not experience it, even if one says a thousand times that there are bonds, the knowledge of actual bonds cannot be obtained. The real thing is that knowledge of bonds comes before seeing Ma. Without wandering in the open air after the cage is opened, one does not understand the pain of being in a cage. What is the form of the bond? As long as the mind is, so long is the bond, so long is the world, it is an Atma-samvedana - "The seed of the world is the mind, and knowledge, not the son, wealth, or direction. The destruction of the world is by the mind's absorption, not by abandoning the household." The mind is the cause of the world; the son, wealth, and subject are not the world. The world and the bond are destroyed when the mind merges. Abandoning the household does not mean abandoning the world. Liberation! This is a great matter; knowledge of the bond! This is a great matter; Ma knows that at the moment when true knowledge of the bond arises, we are eternally liberated; because we are the sons of the wish-fulfilling tree. Oh Ma! Even now, this state of bond is more dear to know! Why should we not do anything? We love to keep the bond. Ma! In this world, whoever has become famous as a powerful man, he too has been given the four Vedas.

## Page 279

Freedom and Pressure
261
The crown of Siddhis placed on the head of Maya has established her in the temple of importance. Mother, how many humans were there who desired true freedom? After that, those who approached your lotus feet, seeking pure devotion or pure knowledge, were called the greatest sages in the world. They too were specially suited to protect Maya and to be established in the bound state. Their special quality is that they want to remove Maya's dirty clothes and adorn themselves with bright, valuable clothes! Mother, are they also seekers of freedom? Then, those who become detached from the world's suffering to become free, their need for freedom ends when they are freed from the world's absence. But we know that, Mother, your all-encompassing, infinite love continues to enjoy us until the end of life. This small mouth cannot hold your infinite love. These two small eyes cannot grasp your always alluring cosmic wealth. This ear cannot retain the divine sound of your beloved throat's sweet invitation (Om). Your body's divine fragrance cannot be carried by this low nose. This skin cannot bear your self-loving touch. These small baby hands cannot offer the mixed flower of devotion in your three-worldly lotus feet. Our head is not capable of bowing to your thousands of feet. How will I experience your deep love! Now, can a sky-wide, sweet moon be obtained by staying in a well? Therefore, Mother, to enjoy your love - to become free in true self-love - is what will happen. Mother, we do not need freedom for freedom.

## Page 280

262
O Eternal Liberator, I desire nothing from liberation. If we can enjoy your love while still bound. Or we know that the day we understand you as life, as soul, the day we can become self-sacrificing, on that day, the concepts of bond and liberation cease to exist. Before self-sacrifice, even in the heart of the being, you are revealed as bond and liberation. O Mother! You are eternally liberated; yet, since time immemorial, you are bound in this world's bond. This bond of creation and dissolution is your eternal ornament. Even bound in this, you remain eternally liberated! And I, sitting in the lap of my eternally liberated Mother, am still bound! Take note of my muddled knowledge! Take note of my sonhood! Even if a son, nurtured on the open bosom of an eternally open mother, still considers himself bound, his sonhood is merely a spectacle. But there is another matter: - Bonded knowledge does not sprout in the breath, until the act of giving and receiving becomes very good, then what is the bond? This feeling, until it is not distant, one must understand that our Mother has not yet been revealed in the heart as knowledge of bond. By praying, she returns to that form. Then, the knowledge of unbearable bondage remains; from that suffering, the wish for liberation sprouts. Then Mother takes the being step by step to higher and higher states. If even after attaining that higher state, one can strongly desire powerful liberation, then only does Mother Liberator, with her love, embrace her child from her heart. He who reaches the abode of liberation within both states, is only acknowledged by the Mantra with the word 'Sarveshwar' (all-powerful). CCO. Vasishtha Tripathi Collection. ## Page 281

He is the Lord of all. Both the universe and the Lord are His. First, the ignorant living beings are satisfied by clinging to the dust of the world on the body of our Mother in Her various forms. This is the essence of life or the essence of all. Remembering this, the Lord is the one from whom all are born, exist, and are united. The first living being tries to reach this state of lordship from the state of all. Ultimately, the past of both is the supreme essence. In that essence, both these forms are established in perfect consciousness, and from which, the state of these two is not experienced in the realm of experience, that is the form of our Mother, the Lord of all lords. Suppose you see a piece of cloth; as long as you are ignorant about the cloth, the name, form, and behavior are established in the various forms of our Mother. Then, your vision falls on the thread that is the cause of the cloth. This is an example of our Mother's form of lordship. Ultimately, by investigating the cause of the thread, it was known that there is nothing other than the thread. Then, the name, form, qualities, and cause of the cloth, that is, the seven elements, become invisible to you. Then, you become ignorant from the great cause. This is an example of our Mother's form of the Lord of all lords. Among these three forms, the one who can investigate with desire is the great sage. He is the one who is free from desire. He is the one who is beyond bond and liberation. This distinction is also seen in the species of life. Some are bound, some are free, and some are beyond these two. (The liberated souls are within the bound souls). These three states are known as ignorance, knowledge, and the highest name. And within these three states, the one who understands the unchanging, eternal, and immutable essence of truth, that is why the use of the eternal word in this mantra has been made.

## Page 282

264
Sura's Request
Raja said,
"O Lord! O Brahmin! Who are You, whom they call Devi Mahamaya? How do You manifest? What is Your nature? I, O knower of Brahman, wish to hear about Your very nature, Your form, and Your origin."
Translation - The king said, "O Lord! O Brahmin! Who are You, whom they call Devi Mahamaya? Why do You manifest? And what is Your nature? I, O knower of Brahman, wish to hear about all these aspects of You."
Explanation - By this time, the king had become so engrossed in the Guru's teachings that he had become one with his own mind. Having caught a glimpse of Mahamaya, he was so eager to know her true form that he expressed his strong desire to hear everything about her. The Guru is pleased to see such a strong request to know. Here, Sura's excitement in accepting the divine wisdom of the Guru is evident. This is why the first word is 'Bhagwan.' Bhagwan, who is not, will not tell about Bhagwan. Only Bhagwan can tell about Bhagwan. Sura understood this very well, which is why he addressed him as 'Bhagwan.' In this mantra, there is another word - 'Brahma Vid.' The Shrutis say - 'Brahma Ved Brahma Bhavati' - he who knows Brahma is Brahma. Without knowing Brahma, who can understand the nature of Brahma? By the grace of fate, if a Jiva gets a Guru who is a knower of Brahma, all doubts and fears are dispelled. From a spiritual perspective, it is seen that the Jivatma, being in Samadhi, sees the same.

## Page 283

Six Questions
265
Only after attaining pure knowledge does one gain the capacity to experience all these elements. Pure knowledge is the ultimate means of self-realization. The Self is reflected in our pure knowledge alone. Therefore, it is called Brahman. So far, the individual soul has experienced only a little of the great power of Mahamaya through the help of Samadhi. The individual can enter this power with great fortune. Without becoming a Brahmin or attaining a glimpse of Brahman knowledge, this power does not manifest.
Once one is near this power or Mahamaya, all the doubts of the individual and the attachment to the three worlds, which are like a blazing fire, disappear. Only then does the individual understand that in this world, one is deluded by attachment, which is merely the will or play of Mahamaya. For this reason, along with these six questions, (1) Who is it? (2) Why does it arise? What is its purpose? (4) What is its nature? (5) What is its form? (6) And where does its origin lie?
Vasishtha said:
Eternal, she is the source of the universe, pervading everything. Yet, her origin is often heard from me.
When the purpose of the gods is fulfilled, she arises. Then, in the world, she is eternally named.

---

## Page 284

266
Rishi's Response
Tell us about the origin of the form. (It has no work of its own.) The boon of the gods comes from her, even when she is eternal, she is called the origin.  
Explanation - Surath asked, "Who is Mahamaya?" Rishi replied that she is eternal; she has no destruction or origin; therefore, she is not perceivable by the senses. Because only perceptible things are subject to destruction and origin. What we perceive with our eyes and other senses has destruction and origin as its nature. In Mahamaya, that nature does not exist. Therefore, she is eternal and beyond the senses.  
O seeker! The consciousness within you - that which you always experience - is not perceivable by the senses. Yet, it is eternal truth - it has no destruction, no origin, no increase, no decrease; it is good, unburnable, imperishable, and unbreakable. Even if you cannot grasp, understand, or enjoy it, you still always engage with it. You come into being within birth, death, youth, and old age, but you are never separated from it. Whatever transformations you undergo, they are all temporary; but there is no transformation in it. The essence is that which you call life, which is consciousness, which is joy, which is this, which is you, which is an atom or great, which is not named, which has no form, and which has no qualities. In this way, know it in ordinary understanding. But in reality, it cannot be known; because it is the form of knowledge, and when you seek to know it, its eternal form becomes evident.  
When a student wants to know the form of the Lord, he thinks, "What benefit is there in this?" and "What is the practice?" Even if it becomes easy, it remains difficult.

## Page 285

Jaganmurti Ma
267
Sadhana is beyond all Dharmas; because Sadhana is a special Dharma, and it is beyond all Dharmas. Therefore, Sadhana is not attainable or achievable, but the Sadhaka's thirst is not quenched by this. He wants to experience it directly. In the state of immediate experience, the practiced Jiva, until he cannot bring Ma into the experience, until he cannot offer the joy and sorrow of the heart directly to her feet, how can his thirst for life be quenched? From this, the agitated disciple says, "I am eternal, even if I am ignorant, O Guru! Please make me worthy of your embrace. Please make me worthy of your direct experience." In this way, when the disciple's suffering is completely transformed into extreme agitation, only then does the Advaitic treasure of the Guru, opening the eyes of the disciple's ignorant and blind eyes, with deep and profound emotion, says, "Son! Disciple! Seeker! Do you really want to see Ma - the Great Illusion?" Do you really want to sacrifice your life to obtain her? Then see - the one you have always seen, the one you have experienced in many births, the one you have abandoned many times in vain despite calling her Nishwara and false, the one you have called a dream and a delusion and blackened your divine eyes on yourself, see, she is "Jaganmurti," this world is her manifest form.

In the path of Sadhana, compared to this, all general advice, the best education, and no understanding at all comes. Call this world Ma. If you cannot believe, then say it by lying, say it by calling it false, because it is not achievable. Air is invisible; but because it manifests in the form of a breeze, it becomes enjoyable for everyone. Similarly, our Ma is not perceptible to the senses in her eternal form; she is not enjoyable; but for us, she is eternally radiant in this gross Jaganmurti, in her manifest form if you cannot believe, then it is an inconceivable element.

New Life

Those who, after practicing the Sadhana of the Mantra from the Gita, "I see Mother everywhere, I see the whole world in her," will enter the state of Chandi, with the help of Samadhi, will listen to the pure Bodhroop Guru near, who will tell them: CCO. ## Page 287

This world is a great lie.
266
"Only the eternal embodiment of the world," all their hopes will disappear, and a complete perversion will come into their life. And those who say that this is a known fact! What new thing is known in this? The Lord is manifested in the beings; what new thing is there in this? Those who will remain engaged in searching for the new mystery will certainly reach the eternal search, reaching this ancient, universally known truth. This is the echo of this one thing in all the scriptures. The Sadhana system of the Vedic era also began from this same cosmic form, which was described in detail in the Upanishads. Sadhana business remains difficult until it is not known easily, until then the spiritual progress of the Sadhak remains in a soft state. Today, such an era has come that Sadhana is considered a difficult business, how much sacrifice, how much restraint, how much harshness will have to be done! But this is not the talk of the era of Rishis. They used to worship this cosmic form with simple faith and they used to attain Rishitva from its fruits. Whatever they saw, they used to recognize it as God. They used to say, seeing water - "You are the purest element, the mind." "You are the source of light, the source of energy, the source of vision." Seeing fire, they used to say - "You are the priest." Touching the wind, they used to say - "You are the sweet breeze, the destroyer of life." Seeing the sun, they used to say - "You are the most auspicious form, I see you." Seeing the flower, they used to say - "You are the embodiment of the divine nectar." Seeing the earth, they used to say - "You are the sweet essence of the earth." Saying this and this, they used to play with simple life, like a naked child with mud. The simple Sadhana of those pure Rishis will be established in every home of India in how many days! After being initiated with the truth Mantra, becoming valorous with the truth establishment, becoming accomplished with the truth attainment, when will India say that this world is a great truth! When will it say that the earth is truth, water is truth.

## Page 288

270
Satya Pratistha
is, Vayu is Satya, Akasa is Satya, Man is Satya, Prana is Satya! The luminous light of Satya will dispel the stain of falsehood forever!
But there is another matter.
This Jagmurti Mahamaya's darshan or Satya Pratistha begins from the first inert objects. Because consciousness, the emotional aspect of the living being, creates a disturbance in the sadhak's mind when he first enters the realm of sadhana. From this, the first fruit, flower, soil, stone, moon, sun, Akasa, etc., objects provide the experience of maternal awareness or Satya Jnana. In this way, as sadhana, i.e., Satya Pratistha progresses, the mind, driven by distraction, may forget Ma's words, but there will be no loss in becoming subject-oriented. Like Ma being forgotten due to the fickleness of the mind, through various actions, sometimes Ma's remembrance may also be forgotten. Sadhak! Try to behave well during that moment of remembrance. Know how many days you have forgotten, do not worry about it; because even in illusion, Ma shines. In that moment when her words come to mind, that very moment when you meet, that will be your direct Ma. Standing in this simple faith, a fully committed, fearless person will stand. By practicing in this way for a few days, Ma will manifest through this Jagmurti, he will become capable of experiencing. Surath asked what his form was? The sage answered that this world is his form. Now, what is his nature? Regarding this, the answer is given - "Tayya sarvamidam tatam. This world is pervaded by him. Through this, the student's rootless doubt has also been dispelled. That doubt is that he is said to manifest from the transient world even if he becomes eternal." Through the practice of this transient form, what do we gain?

## Page 289

The Search for the Eternal Substance
271
Will we gain anything? We, who are unenlightened and impermanent, are seeking the eternal substance (realization)! How can we obtain eternal benefits by engaging in the practices of the impermanent? Why would impermanence not become even more dense? The reason is that whoever engages in the practice of something becomes that thing itself ("As you serve, so you gain. Serve the king, receive the crown"). Therefore, by engaging in the practices of the impermanent world, we will also remain impermanent! "And all is pervaded by That" - this thought has also been dispelled. Even when the impermanent manifestation appears, its eternal essence is unbroken everywhere - it is pervaded everywhere by the feeling of oneness. It is not revealed by being separated from its eternal essence and appearing in the form of this name-and-form-based impermanent world. Just as every atom of basalt is nothing but basalt, or every atom of ice is nothing but water, in the same way, every atom of this impermanent world is nothing but eternal; therefore, there is no reason for doubt. By calling the world "Ma," you will not be enslaved by the eternal substance. If the one searching for rubies finds a piece of cloth, or if the one desiring water finds a drop of dew or a snowflake, will they be enslaved by the thing they have sought? Similarly, you too will see that by calling the world "Ma," Ma's manifestation will disappear, and the eternal essence will begin to shine. Our Ma is all-pervading and omnipresent. She is pervading this entire world from her own essence. By calling the separate physical substances "Ma," you will gain many proofs; you will see that these inert substances are engaged in conscious behavior with you. By calling the inert substances "Ma" and "truth," in that moment when your faith becomes firm, the knowledge of inertness will disappear.

## Page 290

272
The Mystery of Manifestation
A conscious being will interact with you in the form of your own light. A dry tree will offer you the fruits of your desires, and dry earth will fulfill your wishes. The trees in the ashram of Sage Kanwa had given clothes and ornaments to Shakuntala; this is not poetic fancy, it is a truth. This is the fruit of establishing truth. Flowers start blooming on dry trees when truth is established. Even in the current age, many sadhakas have become conscious by interacting with inert objects by saying truth to Mother and are becoming more and more so. By establishing the truth in inert objects with the name of the name that is most dear to God and the relationship with God that is most desired, you will see that the inert object speaks nothing; it is only the manifestation of consciousness. When truth is established in abundance, when truth-knowledge is established in belief, you will see that - where has the world-mother become playful, where is the great consciousness devoid of all senses, yet Mother's eternal form, which is united with all senses, is pervading everywhere. This is "I am everything." This is the form of Mother that pervades the world; or it has no form, or no formlessness, it is unmanifest; like the sky - it is only a form of knowledge. It is also like the ocean of milk or the source of creation. The gods, who were tormented by the demons, are present in this ocean of milk in the unmanifest field, and due to the manifestation of consciousness in a specific form, they continue to pray with a life-breath. And in a short time, they are manifested in their desired form and grant them the boon of fearlessness. In this way, manifestation is called 'creation'. In reality, the creation of Mahamaya is not, nor is there any karma. It is only the manifestation for the gods. And the divine work is its karma. Your consciousness, established by the senses, is the assembly of gods. When they are repeatedly agitated by the demons in the form of the ever-changing world, they continue to pray with a life-breath. And in a short time, they are manifested in their desired form and grant them the boon of fearlessness. In this way, manifestation is called 'creation'. In reality, the creation of Mahamaya is not, nor is there any karma. It is only the manifestation for the gods. And the divine work is its karma.

From here, the description of Devi Mahatmya begins.
Kalpaant refers to the time of dissolution. When the seeds of creation are absorbed into Brahma and remain there, the world does not remain; it becomes one with the cause. This absorption into the cause is called Ekarnav. According to the Rg Veda, the ocean and the sea are mentioned as two creations. Although it appears as a single meaning in gross form, it is a signifier of two causes. Just as the seed of a banyan tree, which becomes a vast banyan tree, is its seed; similarly, when this world form, which is like the seed of the Ashvattha tree or the aggregate of karma impressions, is established in the supreme cause, Brahma, then it becomes known as Kalpaant. At this time, nothing other than pure consciousness exists; hence, it is called Ekarnav or the ocean of causes.
In this Kalpaant time, Vishnu performs the worship of Yog nidra. The word Vishnu means the all-encompassing consciousness power in which the world is established; it is consciousness that is distinct from the world, and its name is Vishnu. In the field of Sadhana, it is called Prana. The Sruti also states that he holds the entire world in his breath, and he is Bhagwan. "He knows the origin, dissolution, and migration of beings, their birth and death, knowledge, and ignorance. He is Bhagwan." The birth, death, migration, knowledge, and ignorance of living beings are all things that are well known; he is Bhagwan. Another special quality of Vishnu is that he is Prabhu. The word Prabhu means one who can independently move the willpower. She has given him such a high authority that he can fulfill all intentions merely by the power of will; hence, he is Prabhu. He is someone who is, when the world does not remain, the all-encompassing consciousness or Prana in what form?

Translation - Prajapati Tejas, seated in Vishnu's navel lotus, and Brahma, the husband, saw those two extremely old asuras, Janardana Vishnu, in deep sleep. Seeing Hari in that Yog nidra, they became eager to break Hari's sleep - Hari's eye-created abode, Visvesvari, Jagaddhatri, the cause of creation and destruction, Bhagavati, incomparable, Vishnu's incarnation of sleep, they began to praise that Yog nidra from their single-pointed heart.
Explanation - When Madhukaitabh, who had risen to kill Brahma seated in Vishnu's navel lotus, that is, when the two primary impressions, when the soul, seated in the god of life, is again directed towards the world's activities, when the mind, desiring to cut off those two impressions, seeks refuge in the god of life and sees that the life is enveloped in Yog nidra; the world's activities are outside. In that state, to bring the life back, he seeks refuge in Yog nidra. The Yog nidra, which is the manifestation of that great power that has separated the life from the world's activities, the cause of the creation, sustenance, and destruction of the world, they pray to that great illusion with a desire to become its special manifestation. This is the spiritual mystery here.
In this mantra, a special quality of Yog nidra is - Hari-netra-krita-alaya. The meaning of Hari is Vishnu or life. He who takes away all desires is called Hari. In Chandogya Upanishad, where the worship of life is proposed, there life itself is the creator of the world or the dissolution of all desires.

Hari-netra-krita-ayaya

Therefore, Prana is Hari himself. As long as the Jiva does not find the foundation of this Prana, as long as it cannot understand Hari as Prana or Prana as Hari, as long as it cannot establish itself in its own Prana, as long as it cannot merge its Prana with the Guru's Prana, even when the name Hari is uttered by the voice that transcends the sky, the Jiva does not find the foundation of immortality or fearlessness. The name whose utterance makes the aspirant forget in the experience of Prana is now uttered by millions of voices; but how many have achieved immortality and success by truly finding the foundation of Prana? The shedding of tears while uttering the name, or seeing a deity without Prana, these are all high states for a common man, but they do not provide the introduction to the reality that is truly accomplished. So be it. The word Hari-netra-krita-ayaya is understood to mean the external manifestation of the light of Prana. The eyes are the external place of Prana's light. The special nature of the living and the dead is revealed through the eyes. The eternal Prana-filled abode in which we are situated is primarily manifested in the eyes. This is specially mentioned in the Upanishad as the subject of the Sadhana of the eye-gazed person. The act of worshiping an idol by saying 'Chakshudan' is a special practice of the first manifestation of the external light of Prana. Those who know the Sadhana of establishing themselves in Prana are aware of this.

## Page 302

284
Stotra of Brahma
Translation - Brahma praises You, Mother! You are Swaha, You are Swadha, You are the Vashatkara and Udatta mantras, You are the essence, O Akshara! O Nitya! You are the Trimatra form. You are used as speech for the person facing directly. You are not mentioned for the person who is not directly seen. For the one who is seen directly, You are used as speech. Here, Brahma or the mind, the Yoginidra form of Mahamaya, is being directly addressed, which is why the word 'sva' is used. Only the Sadhaka does this. Sva stuti, aveda, nivedana, karuna, prayas, and karuna are all forms of worship. Never keep Me out of sight. Where am I? I am placed in an unperceivable location. You sit in this location with the help of imagination and purpose, and perform stuti or sadhana. As long as this feeling remains, progress in the path of sadhana will not be quick. In the kingdom of sadhana, imagination and assumption do not have a place. There is no sadhana without the purpose of the unseen. There will be direct experience of directness at every step of sadhana; directness is the life of sadhana. Therefore, before explaining this Brahma-stotra, we will briefly discuss the subject of mantra-chaitanya because only when mantra-chaitanya is present does the deity become direct. Speaking mantras without chaitanya, that is, without the deity being direct, usually leads to long-term fruitless sadhana. This is the advice of the sages.
Mantra - A special word. The word whose contemplation brings salvation is the mantra. The ultimate meaning expressed in the mantra is the guru. And such meaningless experience or feeling is called chaitanya, that is, the desired deity. In this way, mantra, guru, and deity become one only when mantra-chaitanya is present. By this example, it is explained.

## Page 305

In the Devi Mahatmya, Chapter 287, it is known that in the initial state of Samadhi, the mind, intellect, consciousness, and subtle elements like ego become directly visible, and their specific functional systems are observed.

Now, we will attempt to explain the meaning of the stotra. Brahma said, "You are Svaha." Svaha is the Deva Nirvana Mantra; however, here it serves as an example for all Deva rituals. You are Svadha - this is the Pitru Dana Mantra; but in this place, it serves as an example for all Pitru rituals. When we contemplate on You, the first karma-kanda is reflected on the eye; because this is the first indication of the manifestation of the mother. The karma-kanda is divided into two parts: one for Deva and one for Pitru. Both are special Mantra Sadhana practices.

Oh Mother! You are Svaha, Svadha, and Vashatkara. Puja, Homa, Vrata, Japa, Purashcharana, etc., are Deva rituals, and Shraddha, Tarpana, etc., are Pitru rituals. The Mantras that are performed in these two types of rituals are all You. The three types of swara - Udatta, Anudatta, and Svarita - that are uttered while reciting these Mantras, according to the pitch of their pronunciation, the result of the desired action also varies; these swaras are also You. Therefore, You are also a Swaratmika. Due to the desire of Indra to kill Vritrasura, when the Rishis offered oblations from a high place while reciting Mantras, then You, Oh Mother, shone in the throats of those truthful Rishis! During the time of pronouncing the words of Indra's enemy, instead of Anudatta swara, You came out in Udatta swara form! Due to this, Indra killed Vritrasura; therefore, You are also a Swaratmika. Apart from this, the swara that comes out in the form of Nad in the throats of living beings, which is famous as Para, Pashyanti, Madhyama, and Vaikhari, is also You!

## Page 306

29
Trimātṝa Svārupa
The fruit or unique result of the rituals performed for the gods and ancestors, as mentioned earlier,
is also you, Ma. The fruit of action is also nectar, and because of this, you are the embodiment of nectar. The Upaniṣad states, "From food comes life, from life comes mind, from mind comes truth, from truth come the worlds, from action comes immortality." Shankara Acharya defined the word 'amrita' as 'the fruit'. As long as the rituals for gods and ancestors are seen merely as external actions, the words spoken in the Vedas are seen merely as mantras, and the results produced through rituals are seen merely as the fruit of actions, they are perishable. But when you see that the letters are eternal, and the manifestation of gods and ancestral duties is you, and when you see that the high-pitched sounds and mantras are you, then how can the results of actions be called anything other than nectar or immortality? The result of action alone is ordinary, and its name is knowledge. The main purpose of Ma, who is the embodiment of action, is to manifest knowledge. Knowledge is eternal, and because of this, it is called nectar, and because of this, the fruit of action is also called nectar or immortality.
After this, the essence of all mantras is the three-lettered Om. Through which the world is created, through which Brahma, Vishnu, and Maheshwara are, which manifest as the creation of the world through the sounds of A, U, and M, you are also the Trimātṝa. Here, some discussion is made about the relationship between the Trimātṝa and its form. The word 'mātṝa' means vibration. Vibration is a flow of energy. The Chit-maya Mahāśakti, when manifested in the gross creation, is known as the material energy. That energy manifests the three types of actions. First, it is the creation or the specific manifestation of a dense center of energy. This is creation or the A-kāra. Second, it is the state. When that specific center of energy, with the power of dissolution, competes and maintains its own identity, it is the CCO.
