## Page 307

Birth is the beginning of death. 
286
Stability or the first form is known by the name of Uka. The third, Laya, is when the described Shaktikendra becomes invisible in the presence of Mahashakti, and then it is known as Laya or Makar. Consider taking a fruit in your hand; suppose it is a manifestation of Shakti, binding invisible paramanus into the form of a fruit. This is the first Spanda or Akar. In the language of Sadhana, it is called Brahma. Yogis visualize it as Manarupa. Then look, from that fruit form, when the manifestation of Shaktipravaha in the gross becomes visible in its true self in the presence of Laya or opposing Shakti, until then it is the second Spanda or Uka. In the language of Sadhana, it is called Vishnu. Yogis visualize it as Pranarupa. After some days, you will see that the fruit begins to decay. That which is destruction or pralaya is the work of the third Spanda or Makar. In the language of Sadhana, it is called Shiva. Yogis visualize it as Jnanarupa. This pralaya is not an abrupt change. It is the result of a momentary change in each paramanu. Within these three types of Spanda, the six changes of existence, asti, varddha, etc., occur in each paramanu in each moment. The day a being becomes ashes, from that day onwards, it moves towards death. Death does not happen in one day. The meaning of the word birth is the beginning of death. So, until the second Spanda or Uka remains in contrast to the third Spanda or Makar, death does not occur as a specific event. In every element of the world, this threefold Spanda or Trishaktipravaha is in motion. When Spanda acts with strong power, then it alone becomes visible. A Yogachakshushman person sees this world only in three types of Padan and nothing else.

## Page 309

Nādabindu
261
The third matra or the letter Ma is half a matra, it is half manifested as sound and light on the head of Ukāra. Its state is fixed, but it does not expand; it is the point. It follows the rules of geometry, it is the destroyer of the formless Brahman and the illuminator of the formful Brahma or Shakti. This is the Nād. Those who do not accept the qualities or power of the formless, they see Maya as Prithvi from Maha. Its state is fixed, but it must necessarily expand, because the aggregation of points is the substance. If the point is only perceived as mere consciousness and the Nād as mere inert power, the point loses its potency. According to this view, the formless cannot be devoid of potency, which is contrary to the Vedas and reason.
But in our understanding, O Mother! You are the formless point, the formful sound, and the form of the three matras. Even then, your half-matra form is beyond change and beyond the reach of speech. Therefore, O Devi! You are the illuminating mother! You are the creator of the world, Savitri, the source of creation. Then you are the mother. From the form of the three matras, you are Jagajjanani, and from the half-matra form, you are the Parājanani.
The meaning of the words trimātra and ardhamātra, in order, as sounds and letters, does not cause any confusion in us; because it is with the help of sounds that letters are uttered and manifested. It is only with the support of Shakti or power that formless Brahman is manifested from the formful.

## Page 310

O Mother! This world has always been held by you; you alone create and sustain this world. O Devi! Then, at the end, you alone consume or digest it.

Then you alone sustain it and, at the end, consume or dissolve it. This is your motherhood. Holding it in the womb, giving birth, holding it in the heart, and nourishing it with the milk of knowledge - and finally meeting you in the state of complete knowledge - this is your Mahamayatva or motherhood. Until I did not acquire the capability to enjoy the world, you alone held me in your womb and completed my capability to enjoy through your blood, that is, through the power of Uparjan. After that, when I saw that I have acquired the capability to enjoy the world, then you gave birth to me by creating the world and took me to the lap. You nourished me with your milk - the nectar of knowledge. Through countless births and deaths, I acquired the capability to attain immortality by accumulating knowledge point by point. When I realize with motherly love that I am foolish, and you teach me to become one with myself, and I attain the foundation of unbroken knowledge, and I appear in the state of complete knowledge, then I will become immortal and your food. Then you will complete my name as Annapurna by eating and digesting me. 
We who are your food. "Mother Sarvaprasini, one day you will eat us." Until you understand this, you alone will be our food. We drink your milk and are nourished by you. O Mother! How many forms you have, even thinking about enjoying them makes one wise. When one desires something, they should seek it from that source.

assumes a form. You alone come as the object of desire to fulfill our senses, satisfying our cravings according to our wishes. In this Mantra, creation, sustenance, protection, and destruction are contained. Through these four actions, the full manifestation of Saguna Brahman's Mahamayatva or motherly nature is revealed.
In creation, you are the form of creation; in sustenance, you are the form of stability; and at the end, in dissolution, you are the form of this world.
Translation - O Jagadmayi! In the act of creation, you are the form of creation; in sustenance, you are the form of stability; and in dissolution, you are the form of this world.
Explanation - Mother! It was previously said that you are the creator, sustainer, and destroyer of this world, but that was not entirely correct; because saying so would make the world separate from you. In reality, you are Jagadmayi, and apart from you, there is nothing else in this world. Although generally we see that each thing needs three things - a cause, a material cause, and a specific result; but Mother! In your business of this world, you are the cause, you are the material cause, and you are the result. Water turns into ice, but even there, an external cause exists; but in your business of this world, there is nothing like that. You are the cause, you are the material cause, and then you are the doer.

## Page 313

Trishakti Ma

I desired a fruit. This desire or vasana is the seed of the fruit. (Avyakta Ma saw the vasana in the form of a seed of the fruit). Then, that thickened vasana manifested as a fruit, because the thickened state of vasana is the fruit. That fruit merges back into the avyakta at the end of our enjoyment. Such an event always happens. From one avyakta field, one by one, a vasana sprouts. Again and again, that vasana goes and appears as a planned subject - in the form of an enjoyable sense. At the end of enjoyment, it merges back into the avyakta. This is the kind of light that is of three types; it is known as creation, existence, and culture. Always, these three powers are manifested equally in every being. Oh Jagadgauri Ma! You have no rest even for a moment. You become knowledge in the form of the unknown for our sake, beyond power, you become a form of power, beyond action, you become an active being. From this, it is seen that in the creation period, you are the form of creation, in the maintenance period, you are the form of existence, and then in the dissolution period, you are the form of integration. In these three types, you alone manifest your actional light.

## Page 314

266
Mutual Contradictory Nature
You are the embodiment of splendor in the form of Prakriti; therefore, you are also Mahasuri even when you are Mahadevi; because, even when you are Mahavidya, you are also Mahamaya. Established as the eternal embodiment of great Brahma Vidya, you are the embodiment of the living world in the form of Mahamaya-Murti; then, even when you are Mahameda, you are the embodiment of great immortality. You are the embodiment of great wisdom, the holder of self-knowledge, the embodiment of great patience, Mahameda; then, even if you are forgotten, your existence is forgotten, this too is your own influence. You are the embodiment of splendor in the form of great forgetfulness, you are the embodiment of life in the heart; for this reason, you are also Mahamoharupini. All your actions, the cause of the world's relationship, are from the past of the knowledge of the chain. Human intelligence cannot understand you. Humans think that light and darkness cannot stay in the same place. Knowledge, ignorance, truth, and forgetfulness cannot stay in the same place; but, oh! All is possible in you. Even when the nature of Devi and Asuri is extremely contradictory, you are always present in me. Oh Mother! You have taught us that the meagerness of light is darkness, and the meagerness of knowledge is ignorance. From this, Mother, in the living world created by you, you also see that both these contradictory forms of embodiment are present together at the same time. (1)
(1) We have been told since childhood that knowledge and ignorance, wisdom and ignorance, truth and falsehood are extremely contradictory substances, but is it true that they are extremely contradictory? Even in the past of human intelligence, accepting the statement, "In Parameshwara, all is possible," logic accepts contradictory statements. Experience also shows that in the rise of knowledge, ignorance does not remain, and until ignorance remains, knowledge is not directly present. However, when ignorance is in the state of knowledge, it is not called contradictory to knowledge, but rather it is accepted as part of knowledge; because, unbroken perfect knowledge and ignorance are not contradictory.

Mother! You are the illuminator of the three Gunas; Sattva, Rajas, and Tamas. Your individual and collective nature is revealed through these three Gunas. O Nirguna Mother! When you became enlightened with the first realization of unity, then that became the Sattva Guna. Then, when the power of action was manifested for becoming many, it became doubled - that is the Rajas Guna. And when, becoming many, your conscious form became hardened, it became tripled - that is the Tamas Guna. In the Sattva Guna, your Sattva is revealed, in the Rajas Guna, your Chit, and in the Tamas Guna, the light of Bliss is revealed. O Sanchidananda Maya! You, by dividing yourself with a special feeling, became multiplied by the three Gunas, and revealed as the great, divine, and demonic nature in the collective, and as the individual life nature.
Even as the indescribable and unmanifest, by the inspiration of maternal love, you are the embodiment of the three Gunas - nature. The seers say that the equilibrium of the three Gunas is nature. It is the Adhyaksha; that is why it is insurmountable. We need to enjoy you through our gross senses. We have become gross; that is why we seek satisfaction in serving your gross feelings, that is why the Trigunatmika Vyastimurti is our worshipable form. Those who have known the collective are Hiranyakashipu; they are high officials - they should worship your collective nature, the Mahadevi form. These are ignorant, unenlightened children, who have love for toy forms; that is why, your all-feeling, all-sensed individual nature form is dear to us. That is why, you should be the embodiment of the three Gunas near us. We know.

## Page 320

302
Terrible Prakriti
By worshipping this form of yours, you will meet the great form; because, to completely absorb these three gunas, you alone manifest as Kalaratri, Maharatri, and Moharatri.
Kala, which does not shine anywhere, is Kalaratri. The absorption point of the Sattva guna is called Kalaratri. Similarly, the absorption point of the Rajas guna is Maharatri, and the absorption point of the Tamas guna is Moharatri. Moh is the external manifestation of the Tamas guna; its night, or unmanifest state, is the absorption point.
O Devi, who illuminates the three gunas! You are our mother and no one else's; you are only our mother. By worshipping your own mother (you), you will sequentially see your three forms, and here you will manifest as their previous incarnation. You will manifest as Kalaratri and dispel our knowledge of time. The past, future, and present forms of time will dissolve in the deep darkness of this unmanifest place. Everything will appear as the present. Then, having transcended the past of time, I will conquer death, placing your blood-stained feet on my heart and becoming Shiva. I will be released from the cycle of life. This is the dissolution of the Sattva guna. Then, manifesting as Maharatri, you will completely dissolve me in greatness. Then, my activity and Rajas guna will be lost, and I will gain non-action; I will remain awake in the consciousness of self-awareness. Then, manifesting as Moharatri, I will dissolve my world, the illusion of the material world, and the delusion of the world. Then, I will conquer Ajeya Moh and remain eternally in the state of Chit, always deluded. O Mother! Your three forms are terrible - extremely terrifying. Where Kalaratri, the light of the world, is unmanifest, where the power of Moh is uncontrolled, you are present.

Somyatara
forms do not manifest without manifesting as the Somya form. This is stated in the next Mantra.
Somya, the essence of Somyatara, is beautiful. You are the supreme among the supreme, the Parameshwari. || 60 ||
Anuvada-ma! You are Somya, Somyatara, and Somyatama. You are excessively beautiful. You are the refuge and worshipable for both the higher and lower realms; therefore, you are the Parameshwari.
Vyaakhyana-ma! Those who have not practiced seeing you with all their hearts have their ignorant children's eyes opened by manifesting as many weapon-bearing forms. And those who recognize their own nature as Ma, seeing Ma's creation everywhere, consider themselves as instruments, as Arjuna says in the Bhagavad Gita, "I stand at the end of all beings, I am the instrument that bewilders all beings." This Mantra from the Gita, through Sadhana, has led to realization. Near them, you manifest as the Somya form.
Those who understand only through knowledge that you are the only one revered by all hearts, that is, those who have become the first in the rights of Jnana Yoga, near them, you manifest as Somya. Those who give you life and see only the special manifestation of their life in all hearts, near them, you manifest as Somyatara. And those who are capable of offering their hearts to you from all hearts, that is, those who are capable of merging all hearts in you, near them, you manifest as "the most beautiful of all Somyas," that is, as the Somyatama form. In this way, you manifest in three forms at these three places. Near the Jnana Yogis, you manifest as Somya, near the Prana Darshis, you manifest as Somyatara, and near the Manavilayakaris, you manifest as Somyatama.

## Page 335

The Manifestation of Mother
317
Explanation: From Prajapati's prayer, Mahamaya took the form of a Tamasic manifestation. It is only in the Tamogun that all desires are dissolved. It was previously stated that Mahamaya manifests in two types of Prakriti: Mahat Prakriti and Jeevabhavik Prakriti. Prakriti is characterized by the three Gunas. Just as Jeevabhavik Prakriti is Sattvic, Rajasic, and Tamasic, Mahat Prakriti is also Trigunatmika. The Bhagavad Gita explains these two types of Prakriti as Para and Apara. In the place where the Sattvagun shines in Para Prakriti, the first manifestation or result of Apara Prakriti occurs. Para Prakriti begins with the Tamogun and then sequentially manifests as Tam, Raj, and Sattvagun. Apara Prakriti, on the other hand, manifests sequentially as Sattva, Raj, and Tamogun. At the end of Apara Prakriti and at the beginning of Para Prakriti, the Tamogun is present. The Sattvagun is the middle point (Sandhishtal) of both Prakritis! Nidra, Tandra, Moha, Aalasya, and other Tamogun characteristics of Apara Prakriti are responsible for the dissolution of all desires and the essence of existence in Para Prakriti. The manifestation of Mahamaya's world is called Apara Prakriti, and the manifestation of Mahamaya towards Paramatma is called Para Prakriti. When the Jeev Prakriti is established in pure Sattvagun by overcoming the predominance of Tam and Rajogun, then the Rajogun's activity in Para Prakriti leads to the dissolution of Sattvagun, i.e., it merges into the Tamogun of Para Prakriti; this is why the manifestation of Mahamaya's Tamasic form is extremely necessary in this place. Madhu and Kaitabh, born from Sattvagun, are dissolved in the Tamasic form through the activity of Rajogun, which is the mediator. This is the reason for the manifestation of Mahamaya's Tamasic form.

## Page 336

318
Murtidashana Rahasya
In the praise of the manifestation of the Tamasic form and to dispel the poison of Madhukaitabh,
Vishnu's awakening occurs.
This Tamasic Prakriti manifests from its gross form, from the previously mentioned Kali Maha Devi forms like Khadga and Shula. This form is radiant like the Nila Kantamani; its hands, feet, and face are each a different continent. It is the illuminator of the flow of consciousness in the form of ten types of knowledge and karmendriya. A single chitshakti, traversing the ten senses, experiences many states; this manifestation of the Tamasic Kali form is to dissolve this multiplicity into unity. In this form, the unique synthesis of unity and multiplicity is revealed. The seeker of liberation is blessed by the sight of this form. When the manifestation of a specific form occurs in the Chiyom field, that form is constructed according to the seeker's samskaras. Ma does not have any specific form; she is the embodiment of all forms. Yet, she is self-manifested. However, "for the benefit of the seekers, the form of Brahma is imagined." For the benefit of the seeker, she imagines herself in a specific form. These are the gods and goddesses described in our ancient scriptures. The seeker, by which specific samskaras, characteristics, or virtues they attribute to the soul, the beloved form of the Paramatma, by manifesting those virtues, fulfills the seeker's aspirations. This is the mystery of the manifestation of specific forms in the world. Such a manifestation of a specific form occurs in two places: in a manomaya field and in the vijnanamaya kosha or wisdom. By repeatedly imagining a specific form in the mind (as a result of daily practice), one usually sees such a form.

## Page 337

Yog nidra
316
Even without engaging in such practices, one may occasionally see a manifestation of a deity. It should be understood that this is the result of accumulated heavy thoughts from previous births. Indeed, the deities seen in the mind field are momentary, blissful, and increase one's faith in the divine; there is no doubt about this. However, these deities cannot fulfill the aspirant's desires because they lack the development of the life force; they do not manifest omniscience, omnipresence, or other great qualities; they are merely a special shadow of the mind. Therefore, the aspirant cannot be endowed with immortality or any other boon. However, when a specific deity is seen in the field of knowledge, all doubts are dispelled, the eye of wisdom is opened, and the aspirant, having obtained the desired boon, becomes blessed.

Indeed, it is said in the Mantra that the eyes, mouth, nose, hands, heart, and genitalia were once seen by the tamasic goddess Brahma. These are centers of specific experiences of vital energy. Without losing the power to reanimate the seeds of the world, the state where one attempts to meet the Supreme Self is called Yog nidra. In this state, the activity of vital energy remains stagnant. Therefore, the centers of development such as the eyes, mouth, nose, hands, heart, and genitalia, when united, attempt to enter the lap of the Supreme Self. When this state is exited, i.e., when Yog nidra breaks, the activity of vital energy in these centers reverts to its previous state. Only the aspirant can understand that when, after abandoning the identification with the body-mind and the renunciation of various states, one gradually approaches the Supreme Self, the natural pulsation or deviation of the elements such as the eyes, mouth, and heart continues. Then, when united with Yog nidra, the natural pulsation or deviation of these elements ceases.

## Page 341

The Battle of the Lord
223
The meaning of the word "Shabd" is vast and infinite in power. The meaning of the word "Hari" is the destroyer of all. This experience of gaining the capability to destroy the demons does not come to the soul if it does not transcend these three kinds of experiences!
Removing weakness is the first Sadhana. "Can I remove this accumulated ignorance from the beginningless time?" This feeling indicates the weakness of the soul; therefore, in one aspect, this is a local sleep. By firmly holding onto the feet of Ma with determination, and by sincerely praying to Ma for her grace, Ma infuses such strength into the soul that the Sadhak can truly experience that I am the Lord, I am the Infinite, I am the destroyer Hari. Therefore, I am determined to completely eradicate the demon race, this is the ultimate goal.
The term "Bahu-praharana" refers to the power of grasping or taking. The hand or the eye, which is the instrument of grasping, which means a special weapon, is known as Bahu-praharana. To remove the fruit of the impressions of samskaras such as Prana-shakti - to merge with oneself, the power of taking is used. This is the battle of Vishnu with Madhu-Kaitabha. This battle lasted for five thousand years. The literal meaning of "Panchavarsa-sahasran" is five thousand years long; but from an spiritual perspective, it has a different meaning. The word "Panch" means the five senses, and the word "Varsa" means place and "Sahasra" means the indicator of the infinite. Therefore, the meaning of the phrase "Panchavarsa-sahasran" is the experience of an infinite number of specific five-sense objects.
The place where the five kinds of experiences of the mind emerge is called Panchavarsa. It is only then that these five kinds of experiences become infinite in terms of name, form, and other differences. That is why the word "sahasran" is used so much. It is from this place that one becomes helpless.

---

## Page 342

324
The experience of five types of poisons, each with a thousand variations over five years, unfolds, and they aim at the center. The essence of this tradition is that there are two emotions at its root: joy and the desire for greatness. This is the MadhuKaitabh. To destroy him, they repeatedly immerse themselves in the power of absorption by targeting the centers of many emotions. Until the collection of experiences becomes self-satisfied and self-aware, this method of using the power of absorption is absolutely necessary. Experiences run towards objects, then, like a life force, they grab both hands from behind and merge into themselves. This is the mystery of the power of absorption and the use of the arms. The center of experiences is a practitioner who has been trained by waves of five types from many births. These waves then become countless, each with different names, forms, and behaviors; therefore, to destroy MadhuKaitabh, they aim at the center of experiences or the thousand-year tradition and use the power of absorption, meaning that without using the arms, no other method can achieve his destruction. This is why here the story of the duel between Vishnu and the two asuras is told. Some interpret the thousand-year tradition as a long-term form. The meaning of this view is that these two powerful traditions are special states of divine consciousness. Therefore, to destroy them, long-term practice and the use of the power of both hands with dispassion are required through the use of the power of life. In reality, without long-term faith, continuous practice, and dispassion towards the subject, the essence of life is not completely destroyed.

## Page 343

The Asura Varadaan was arrogant.
Even so, Mahamaya, deluded by her form, asked Keshava, "Grant us a boon."
Translation - They were both powerful and arrogant; however, deluded by Mahamaya's form, they asked Keshava, "Grant us a boon today."
Explanation - Madhukaitabh is an extremely powerful and arrogant Asura because of the desire "We shall beget many," which is a desire-sankalpa born from the greatest field or Brahman. This is why he is extremely powerful. On the other hand, the desire "To be one in the manifestation of many" is born from the sankalpas of life. This is why he is weak. It is impossible for the weak to defeat the powerful. Therefore, Mahamaya herself revealed her form to delude these two Asuras. The truth is that the soul is deluded by maternal love, and the mind is deluded by the greatness of Mother's power. It is only when both do not desire the many that their purpose becomes one, an intense desire to merge in Mahamaya's infinite, boundless, eternal, tranquil, desireless, and pure existence. Then, overwhelmed by their heartfelt prayer, Mahamaya deluded Madhukaitabh with her form. He was deluded by the beautiful form of the Tamasic Nila Mani, which was as radiant as a blue sapphire. In that form, the desire to merge was clearly expressed.
Exactly the same happens with us. Our Mother's form is like that! Once seen, one cannot forget it. She is the smooth Shyama, the sweetness of the ocean of nectar, the freshness of the new moon, the beauty of the young girl. Once seen, the world does not seem good, nor does anything else seem good. There is always a desire to merge in her, who is my true form.

## Page 344

326
Keshava's True Form
He who is in me, here who says "I am I," is not truly so. He who is in time, bound by hand and foot, is the life in me. He is the independent, simple, luminous, blissful, and so on. Once I see that, then who would want to remain in this "I"? From this, the two asuras, freed from maternal nature, have risen to destroy themselves through Sadhana; they have gone to become immortal after dying. From this, Keshava says, "Accept our boon." Here, the Prana in the Keshava form is manifested, that is, by consolidating the seeds of all emotions and dissolving them into their specific emotion, they are dedicated to being included in the Mahakaran. This is the true form of the Keshava form; the meaning of the 'k' word is water! Because the one who sleeps like a corpse (dead) in water is Keshava! Indeed, having caught a glimpse of the power of Prana's dissolution, the two asuras, enchanted by Mahamaya, that is, in their maternal form, from Prana said to each other, "Whatever you say, we will do, then we will not stand against you." Until now, they did not know that you are the only reason for our great fortune, from this, they developed many emotions and from the joy born from them, they were enchanted, but now you can understand your opinion that you will make us an ornament of your mother's body, in that effort, you will give us whatever we want.
The conclusion is that the essence of the tradition is also knowledge. The substance that is born from knowledge is not; therefore, the destruction of knowledge is impossible. So, that essence of the tradition also remains stable in Brahman, like a burned seed. It is also the garland in the mother's neck. If any emotion cannot be born, then it is dead. All these mysteries will be explained in the second and third story with special emotion.

## Page 345

Concentration of Multiple States

After some days, by focusing on the center of experience, by practicing the power of giving or by seeing in the form of mother, that is, by experiencing each state, that is Ma; this realization comes when doubt and opposition are absent; then you alone become the destroyer (worthy of being destroyed). O seeker! Look, your five years (center of experience) are always present with thousands of differences. Your arms are your weapons. You also take that state with both hands and say - "Come, our Ma, come, our soul, come into us, come into our possession! Come as form, come Ma! Come as taste, come Ma! Come as word, come Ma!" In this way, by seeing all states in the soul and the soul in all states, you will see that the Ajey Asura will ask for his own death from his own desire. How does this happen? When you see that the firm resolve has made the impressions motherly, and the true fire is burning in your center of experience, and the impressions like butterflies fall into that fire and become true, and all have become Ma, then you can say to the motherly daughter with respect - "Why are you developing from so many states now? Now, gather all your states." Then the motherly daughter will gather all her forms. If Ma, out of love for her child, becomes foolish, she will gather her Asura form.

This is the true reality.
However, we see that Lord Vishnu, bound by the demons, prays, "By what other means?" And what need is there for another boon! And nothing else is needed. I do not want to establish myself as a powerful being in the world through the power of Siddhi, nor do I want to become a great man adorned with the best ornaments of devotion, knowledge, good deeds, etc. I do not want to adorn myself with the dirty clothes of the body and decorate myself with precious jewels. This is not my desire. I want to be - to surrender myself to you with all my heart. By offering my life force at your feet, I want to try to atone for a single point of infinite life-impervious ignorance. In this way, only by developing the desire for renunciation or true mumukshu bhava in the breaths, the prayer "May we be bound by you" is made to the demons! The demonic mother form is always available to us only through this prayer.

## Page 347

Rasa Leela
326
Translation - The sage says that both Asuras, considering themselves to be in control, and seeing the world filled with joy, asked Lord Vishnu with lotus eyes to bind us.
Explanation - Madhu Kaitabh is now bewildered by the form of Mahamaya; from this, he has understood that we have been deceived for so many days. After playing many games, the earth has remained under control. Similarly, as long as the living beings remain bewildered in the full form of Mahamaya, they remain bewildered in the world of many emotions, considering the world to be the place of joy. As long as the living beings consider themselves satisfied with the temporary pleasure of sensory objects, they cannot understand that they are in control. But on the day Mahamaya reveals her true form, facing her eyes, they understand - "Oh! For so many days, we have remained under control in the world of true joy." At the time of explaining the Devi Sukta "Apo Mayam Jagat," the teacher Sayanadev interpreted the word "ap" as "expansion of consciousness." In this place, that is, in this state of understanding, the special experience of the Supreme Self is possible. The seeker, after removing self-awareness from the body, rises in the field of consciousness, then, with a loving heart towards the living beings, goes back to the field of wisdom. It is here that the meeting of the living being and the Supreme Self is organized. This is the joy of liberation. This is the Vrindavan in the language of the Vaishnavas - it is here that the Rasa Leela takes place. The soul, in the form of rasa, surrounded by the Gopis in the form of sensory power, with the living being as the worshiper, revels in this place. This cannot be described in the language of joy. "Atmaaramoapi rere mat" - even when one becomes happy with the self, in what way does she not revel with us.

## Page 348

330
They are,
unlimited bliss,
how could they understand if He does not come to this Vrindavan?
When the Gopis or the senses, abandoning the entire world, run in search of Krishna with intense speed, driven by the sound of the flute, Radha, when she is completely immersed in Krishna's love, becomes bewildered by the Supreme Soul's enchantment and reaches this field of wisdom, Vrindavan. Only then is the moment of the great union of the soul. In the language of the Shaivas, this field is Kailash. Here, the scientific Shiva enjoys bliss with Parvati, the embodiment of Prakriti. From here, the entire world appears as a manifestation of the Self, pervading the entire world with a slow, enlightened vision. Everything is here; but it is only constructed through wisdom, that is, it is Chit. The inert nature is completely hidden here. In contrast, the meaning of the word is taste. The Supreme Self is nothing but a form of taste. The world appears as a manifestation of the Self and as a taste when the Self is seen with bliss. Madhukaitabh, after so many days, became bewildered by the form of the blissful Mahamaya; that is why the entire world appears as a manifestation of the Self. The one who is determined to destroy them is also seen as "Lord Kamalekshan" by them at this time. Because at this time, they can understand that the soul is their only friend. The soul, which is determined to destroy them, wants to dive into the ocean of taste, and through this experience, it can understand. The soul is just a wave of the ocean; it will not develop from being a wave; it will become the entire ocean. The soul is the only means of this great union; that is why the soul is the most beloved; therefore, he is seen as Kamalekshan, the vision of love, here. Madhukaitabh, who prays to Vishnu, becomes even more devoted. "May we be blessed where the earth is not flooded by water." What a beautiful prayer! This prayer makes the world an enlightened manifestation.

## Page 349

The Destruction of Asuras
331
They see the vision of nectar. The nectar is like some waves of the ocean of bliss, which shines from the form of the earth or the form of the universe. Where the earth is also frightened by this water, where the unconditioned water, that is, unconditioned nectar, pure bliss is, there is our destruction. Then there is no need for special bliss or the development of many emotions like a worm. Where they come from, they should take it with them.
Listen - upon entering the field of wisdom, the world's existence is almost lost. Here, the world is revealed in a wisdom form. Where there is no emotional world, where there is pure wisdom or bliss, there the end of sense-pleasing bliss and importance occurs. From the rise of wisdom or greatness, the experience of the seer, that is, the witnessing consciousness, occurs. Let the world be like a shadow in the form of wisdom consciousness. Happiness, sorrow, crying, laughing, etc., contradictory emotions, the seeker cannot be shaken. "I am only the witness of all emotions" - this wisdom arises. In this state, in the field of self-wisdom, the world's existence becomes weak; this is "The world is like water." Where the world's weak existence is not at all, in this pure wisdom form, all emotions end. Madhukaitabh wants to know this. May his wish be fulfilled.
The sage said.
Thus speaking, the Lord, the holder of the conch, discus, and mace. With the discus, he severed the heads of the two asuras.

## Page 350

332
Mastakachhedan
Explanation - The conch is the embodiment of Nadashakti (Jamin), which is pervading the infinite universe through the Pranava sound. The Sadhaka hears this sound from the Anahata Chakra, whose different waves are known in the world as words. The conch is the embodiment of this sound. See in the Gita - Lord Shri Krishna, who is the charioteer, has a conch in his hand; here too, Lord Madhukaitabh has a conch, the embodiment of the sound of Nadashakti. The nature of Nad will be described later.
The word Chakra means the world. From Ann to Pran, from Megh to Ann, from Yajna to Panchajanya (Megh), from Karma to Yajna, from Ved to less and Akshar Purush to Ved, from Anulobh and Vilobhabhav, this Chakra path is called Samsara. This is the Chakra that is placed in the hands of Lord Vishnu. This is also known as the Sudarshana Chakra. This world Chakra, which is propagated from Brahma and is always established in Brahma, appears extremely beautiful to those who see it in Brahma's presence.
The embodiment of Gadala or Samhara Shakti is this. The conch or Pranava sound is the cause of the creation of the world; it is the unmanifest sound. And the Gadala or manifest sound is the cause of the destruction of the world in the form of the sky (Vi+Oom); therefore, the conch, Chakra, and Gadadhari are understood to be the creator, sustainer, and destroyer of the world.
Madhukaitabh wishes to be destroyed by his own wish. He is powerful with the strength of Mahamaya's Pranashakti. He is beautiful with the ability to create, sustain, and destroy. In this state, Vishnu cut off Madhukaitabh's head by placing it on his own thighs. "Mahitale Tajjagane" - Vishnu's Jagad is Mahitale. Mahi or Kshiti is the ultimate manifestation of the inert. To separate Chaitanya from inert, Kshiti takes refuge in the form of a solar eclipse.
The most important point is that

---

## Page 351

The Secret of the Head
333
Without the human body, the experience of the difference between inert and consciousness is not possible; therefore, the human body is the field of Sadhana. From here, both liberation and rebirth are obtained. This is why Vishnu is described as the land of Jagannath.
There is a mystery in the cutting of the head. Our sensory organs are located in the upper part of the throat. Although they are spread throughout the body, the main sense of touch is revealed in the lower part of the throat, at the base of the tongue. The upper part of the throat is the field of knowledge, and the lower part is the field of the inert. This blending of consciousness and the inert is called the living being. Separating this is to break the bond of form. From this mixture of the inert, consciousness withdraws from its pure nature and is revealed in the pure nature of the living being. From this living being, consciousness is freed; this is the ultimate goal of all forms of Sadhana. Pursuing this goal, the throat of the bull, which is cut in the worship of the gods in our country, is severed.
Thus, Yog nidra-liberated Vishnu cut off the head of Madhu-Kaitabh. The meaning of this is that the original impression-based living being, which flows through the channels of birth and death for an infinite time, is freed from the bond of the original impression - which is the source of great importance and many desires - only through the active vital energy of consciousness in these days, by residing in the gross or physical body. This is called the separation of the living being from Brahman. The bond that the mind, which is always filled with the desire for importance and finds happiness in it, breaks is called the separation of the Brahman bond. The bond from which Brahma or the mind is bound is the separation of the original impression-based bond, which is described in the story of Madhu-Kaitabh. From the separation of the Brahman bond, the aspirant becomes free from this world, this woman, this society, this body.

---

## Page 352

334
Karmaphal-Vilaya
The fruits of actions are mere imagination. The imagination of Ma's vast mind is what gives rise to the experience of the world. And the hope and desire for the future also disappear. The distinction between Vishnu and Rudra will be explained in the second and third chapters, respectively. The difference between these three knots is seen only through the vision of the Paramatma. The point is that these three are known as the destruction of future, past, and present fruits. In the matter of the destruction of the fruits of actions, Bhagwan has said - Jnanagni s karmani bhasmasat kurute. Just as a blazing fire turns a heap of fuel into ashes, in the same way, Jnanagni turns all actions into ashes. When Acharya Shankara explained this, he condensed the meaning of all the words in this place. He says that by attaining knowledge, one gains the destruction of future and past fruits; but the destruction of the present fruit of actions does not occur. An example of disease is cited. A disease aimed at a deer with an arrow on its bow, another arrow in its hand, and a quiver on its back filled with arrows. The deer, running away a little, left the arrow behind. At that moment, the disease received knowledge. Suddenly, the disease abandoned the arrows in its hand and on its back. And then it said that from now on, I will never kill a living being; but the arrow that escaped from its hand will still hit the target deer. In the same way, by attaining knowledge, the fruits of actions that are like seeds of future fruits or whose fruits have not yet begun to be enjoyed remain destroyed, but the fruits of actions whose fruits have already begun to be enjoyed in the present body continue to be a source of destruction.

---

## Page 353

The Mystery of Prārabdha
Much reasoning is necessary in this matter; it is also written in the scriptures - "Mother consumes countless karmas." Unconsumed karmas are not destroyed even in countless kalpas. But when Lord has said, "The fire of knowledge destroys all karmas; only then, upon truly attaining knowledge, does the certainty of the destruction of all karmas come. When knowledge becomes so radiant - when the state is reached where one attains this elevated stage of knowledge - the sadhaka's prārabdha, the fruit of karma accumulated in this gross body, is also completely dissolved. Only when one reaches this elevated stage of knowledge is the complete destruction of all karmas truly attained. When knowledge becomes so radiant, only future and present karmas are destroyed; with the strength of firm resolve, the sadhakas can achieve this much benefit. But to attain the destruction of prārabdha, one must attain such radiant knowledge, which is an extremely difficult endeavor. Those who frequently enter samādhi and then emerge into dehātma bodh should understand that they cannot ascend to that most radiant field of knowledge. For this reason, their prārabdha remains in the field of experience in the form of the body. But there comes a day for the sadhaka - the day when entering samādhi, they do not return to dehātma bodh. 'Yadgatvā na nivartante taddhāma paramaṁ mam'; this is the most radiant form of knowledge, and it is only by attaining this state of knowledge that true, perfect knowledge is attained.

## Page 354

336
End of Chapter
Thus, Mahamaya, being praised by Brahma, manifested Herself. Now, listen attentively as I describe Her glory. Thus, in the Markandeya Purana, in the Savarnika Manvantara, in the Devimahatmya, the story of Madhu and Kaitabha ends.
Translation - Praised by Brahma, Mahamaya manifested Herself in this way! Listen, Vasishtha! Now, I will describe the glory of this Devi. Listen with one-pointed concentration.
In the Markandeya Purana, within the Savarnika Manvantara, in the Devimahatmya, the story of Madhu and Kaitabha ends.
Explanation - It is only after being praised by Brahma or the mind that the Devi manifests Herself in a special form. Until Bhagavadbhava (divine consciousness) blossoms in the mind, only the experience of existence is felt. When Bhagavadbhava blossoms in the prana (life force), then the all-pervading consciousness becomes manifest everywhere. Only when the mind merges entirely in Bhagavadbhava does the Devi manifest Herself in Her special form as the mind-made, sense-made, and body-made form. Therefore, if Brahma or the mind worships the Devi, if they are truly devoted to Her manifestation, then the Devi definitely manifests Herself in this form. Thus, those who, with a balanced mind, worship the Devi as their mother and are blessed by Her motherly grace, are blessed by Her vision, which is free of all doubts and fills their hearts with joy.
Those who, without seeking the balance of mind and prana, rush towards the path of devotion by elevating the mind to Bhagavadbhava, also see the manifestation of the special form. However, that form is like a carved statue, rigid and lifeless.

## Page 355

Guru and Mother are one.
337
The glory of Mother's greatness not being present, the form of the deity cannot fulfill the aspirant's desires.
Indeed, in this first story, we see that Samadhi, the embodiment of wisdom, in the form of a cloud, takes refuge at the feet of the Guru, who is like a celestial being, and gradually, by seeing the greatness of Mother - the power of Mahamaya, he becomes blessed. Madhu and Kaitabh are the seeds of future destruction. These seeds are destroyed, meaning they lose their reproductive power, only then does the difference between Brahma and Pranathi occur. "Then I do not want anything, neither in this world nor in the other world, my desire is for the fruit." This kind of desireless attitude is contrary to the primitive concept of "more in one." In the language of Acharya Shankara, this is called "Ihamutra fruit enjoyment renunciation." They say that only then is the realization of the Paramatman obtained, and in the Devi Mahatmya, it is said that in the tamasic impurity of Mahamaya, the awakening of Avirbhava and the awakening of Vishnu occur, only then does the true fruit enjoyment renunciation appear. We know that before seeing Mother, no one can give up their full-hearted desire. What is the way to see Mother? The way is desire. Seeing is done as soon as the desire to see arises. Then it is not hidden that whoever finds it with the help of some method will have to bring it out. It is perfectly illuminated everywhere. If the desire of the living being does not arise, he does not see it. Only when the desire to see Mother arises does she first show herself as a Sadguru. Only after obtaining the Guru does the aspirant become motivated to offer his body, mind, life, and all possessions at the Guru's feet. Gradually, the Guru becomes his 'I', the sense of living being's agency becomes weak, even if there is any good or bad, he cannot then think, "I am doing." "As directed by Devendra, I am present in the heart and do as I am told." - Vasishtha Tripathi Collection. Digitized By Siddhanta eGangotri Gyaan Kosha

## Page 356

Satya Chaitanya Ananda
In this way, actions performed in the world are carried out through the power of knowledge. From the fruits of these actions, current activities become imbued with love and free from hatred; therefore, they do not transform into seeds of future suffering or into bonds. In worldly activities, as attachment decreases, the devotee's attachment to the heart-centered guru increases. As attachment increases, the devotee remains more and more bewildered by it. Gradually, through complete dedication from all aspects, the devotee becomes absorbed. At that time, the devotee understands that the guru and the mother are not different; they are one. He remains within them and fulfills all their rituals. At that time, the mother appears to the devotee in a specific manner to destroy the fruits of their three types of actions. Then, there is no longer any sense of duty. There is no need to use special actions from the intellect. All actions become selfless, behind one another, due to some inherent principle. Just as the necessary guidance is required to distinguish between father and mother, our mother herself illuminates herself with that kind of feeling, which is the true path of the world's practice. Whether a devotee belongs to any tradition or not, they must fall into this simple process. And another thing is that from the very beginning, the subsequent steps come on their own, this is the simple way of practice. This principle is clearly explained in the story of Suratha Samadhi. The first is Madhu-Kaitabhanidhan or Satya Pratishtha, the second is Mahisasura Badh or Chaitanya Pratishtha, and finally, Shumbhavadh or Ananda Pratishtha, our mother is "Sanchedanand Swarupa," her world-facing light or creation, which is of the same nature as Sanchedanand (previously, this was her specific manifestation from time 8).

---

## Page 357

The Tamasic manifestation is of the form of Sanchedananda; therefore, the establishment of Sat or Truth is the first stage of Sadhana. (1) The establishment of Chit or Prana is the second stage, and the rest is the establishment of Ananda, which means the state of eternal liberation. Alternatively, when the establishment of Truth and Prana occurs, the establishment of Ananda happens by itself. Only the experience of existence is the true establishment of Truth. This is why the belief that 'She is here' becomes heavy, and the vital agency becomes weak. The root of future actions is destroyed. This is also called the separation of Brahma or the destruction of Madhu-Kaitabh. Love and hatred are called Madhu and Kaitabh. We have no conflict with them; because love and hatred are both causes of true bondage. Only when love and hatred are freed does the power of action become weak. In all actions, only the eternal Mahamaya remains; when the true aspect of life becomes the goal, less love and hatred remain. Besides this, there is no other way to achieve it. Therefore, even here, the establishment of Truth is the internal fire that destroys Madhu-Kaitabh, and there is no doubt about it. The scientific Guru Medhas has previously described for the sake of dispelling the doubt of the living soul, "When Mahamaya manifests from a specific state, then she is called 'manifested'." The merciful Guru has shown Surath the manifestation of Mahamaya directly and said, "Thus, she is manifested." How Mahamaya manifested in the form of a Tamasic manifestation to protect the destroyed Brahma from Asurakraman is shown, and then in sequence, other specific manifestations will be shown, up to Vasihtkar. This is discussed in the Siddhanta eGangotri Gyaan Kosha.

## Page 358

340
Conclusion
It is said that we describe the greatness of Mahamaya further, listen to it and see it with one-pointed mind. O most beloved son of Mother! O seeker of human beings! Do you consider yourself to be troubled by the poison of Madhukaitabh - the fickleness caused by momentary joys? If you understand this great joy by agitating and self-reflection, then surely you will become a fool in the grace of a true guru and maternal love, and Mother will take you to her lap in a short time. This plan has already been set in motion. You have crossed the steps of Moksha-shastra Upanishads, the riddles of the Gita, and reached the shore of "Sadharmān paritijya" - you have fallen into the tranquil, elevated lap of Mother - in the ocean of joyous liberation! You will surely drown. You will only see three waves. In the first wave, the doubt and disbelief that you had will be swept away, calming all kinds of desires. Even if you try to reach the lowest point within, you will not find the resolution of your single-pointed desire! Everywhere, the belief in the joyous motherly nature will be as firm and immovable as Himachal. The mind that considers itself unworthy of motherly attainment at this time of attachment to greatness and subject matter; that same mind, advancing, will awaken maternal power, and will perform the Sadhana to cut off greatness and fundamental joy or attachment. Madhukaitabh will be defeated. The seed of your future work will be destroyed. The distinction between Brahma and Pranathibheda will occur - you will become established in truth. That wave will pass. Two more waves will follow in sequence. One of them will make your all-encompassing self-motherly belief vital and conscious. Seeing the play of your own life, you will start to make mistakes. The prayer of Vishnu will be as great as the ocean.

---

## Page 359

From the hands of Sadhana Samara, you will receive salvation. You will become established in Prana. At the end, another wave will come – it will immerse you in the ocean of bliss within the vast expanse of your own world. The pristine knowledge-infused Rudramanthi will be severed. Forgetfulness will reach the physical body, which is the result of past karma. You will be established in bliss. From this, the science-infused Guru Brahma Rishi Medhas, the sister of Satyavijayanti, calls out with a heart full of love and compassion – "Come, Suratha! Come, Samadhi! Come, Sadhaka! Come, son of Amrita!" "O Devi, listen again, I will tell you about your glory." Then, the Devi will speak about her greatness – reveal yourself. Where are you? Come together, from a million throats, with a high voice, and say 'Maa, Maa' and advance! Through the medium of maternal power and the wondrous story of her supreme character, hear the ancient mystery of Sadhana – see it with your own eyes! With blind eyes, open them with the eye of knowledge! Without faith and devotion, let your dry heart be drowned in the pure stream of devotion. Let your restless, karmaless, lazy life become devoted to karma. You will be initiated into the unparalleled conjunction of knowledge, devotion, and karma in a perfect state. Come to our Maa! Bewitched by the love of our children, come once again, freed from the world of truth! We are sitting here, big and dirty, big and stained. In some way, we cannot rid ourselves of this dirtiness. Darkness from all sides is thickening, like a thick fog of false illusion. Once see Maa! Your dearest children, suffering from famine, great epidemics, and burning forests, are trembling from the storm of doubt, disbelief, and lack of faith. The heart is filled with a cool and peaceful feeling, and let death be the only fear in this era. This is a sign of the end of this era. Therefore, in this moment of the end of this era, once again, be born anew, Maa! Stand up from the ashes of death.

## Page 376

Brahmananda, the source of supreme joy, is the embodiment of knowledge alone,
Beyond the dual, resembling the sky, that is the ultimate truth, as seen in the scriptures.
Eternal, pure, and changeless, the witness of all wisdom,
Beyond emotion, free from the three gunas, I bow to that Guru.
Guru! You, in your many forms as Narayana, have devised this plan for my service,
You are surrounded by your own service! Once, abandon the swing of materialism,
And reveal your form as a conscious, vital spirit. Be free from the tamas of the world.
The servant's hope is fulfilled.
By mere remembrance of him, knowledge is attained,
He is the very embodiment of all wealth. Salutations to that revered Guru.
In every pot, Hari resides, none knows him.
Without knowing the navel, a deer searches frantically.
Neither liberation nor the sky, nor the netherworld, nor the earth,
All desires are doubts in the mind, destruction is liberation, thus say the wise.

## Page 377

Mother's Love Awakens.
May all beings know the essence of immortality.
1
The Call of Love! Have you heard the sweet lullaby of truth? Have you seen the light beam that came from afar, drawn by the desire to see it, even with half-closed eyes? Has the love for mother, cooled by many births, distanced itself from touch? The fire has gone out, but you cannot rise? Has the hard shell of sleep not yet broken? Good - just! Sit at the junction of sleep and wakefulness, listen to mother's teachings with your ears. (This is called unbroken hearing.) That alluring call will surely awaken you, leading you to the goal of the call. Your eternal hard shell will break. Just keep your ears open with a little excitement. Those who woke you will give you the strength to rise. They will create excitement in your life. Driven by the powerful attraction of that excitement, you will leave the material world and go towards your mother. Oh! Son, who has been deceived by the imagined love of the madira! You are being deceived by the taste of immortality, which you are experiencing only in a dream. Sit in mother's lap and experience the joy of motherly stories, which will be thousands of times more than what you are experiencing. You will be filled with the nectar of motherly love. You will be overwhelmed by the vastness of mother's grace.

## Page 378

4)
I call out to you again and again,
- Come, O Sons of Immortality! If you have awakened, if the world has understood the form of Truth, if the world has learned to adore in the form of Chit, if you have practiced the vision of Bhagwan Sat in all beings, then come, stand up! Come closer to me and see - countless celestial bodies (sun, moon, stars, etc.) are always my worship. The countless worlds are found in my limbs from age to age. The countless living beings have been offering worship to me with their offerings since time immemorial. See - in the Brahma Yajna hall, each atom offers Prana as a sacrifice to my purpose. The purpose is Atmanivedana. The purpose is simply to become identical to me by seeing me once. Oh, particle of my life! How long will you remain entangled in complex emotions and suffer the blows of birth, death, happiness, and sorrow? Come, run towards the ocean of great life. Don't be afraid, you won't lose yourself, but you will find yourself. Whatever you gain in this moment, if you become delirious, it becomes extremely worthless. Desires are destroyed in it. Unfulfilled aspirations become mere pretense. Birth and death are a joke, and even if nothing happens here, everything is there. Complete joy, only Immortality, only love - an unbroken stream of maternal compassion, and eternal, unchanging life, great victory. Son! You have established yourself on Truth; now establish yourself on Chit (Prana). May the blessings of Shri Guru pour down on your head.

## Page 379

This narrative expands on the concept of Pranapratistha. Pranapratistha means to attain the state of Prana, to remain in the experience of Prana while witnessing the Pranamaya leela everywhere. Prana is Parambrahma. It is such a power of the Paramatma through which the creation, sustenance, and dissolution of the world occur. The living world is a dense manifestation of this Mahapraana. Our vital force is a part of this collective Mahapraana and a wave in the ocean of Prana. It resides in a drop of our blood, making our body strong and capable of performing all actions, and then sits inside and performs all these actions. The circumstances and mind of our body are merely a game of this Prana. By witnessing the leela of this Prana's power in all the activities of the world, we will be freed from ego and gain the ability to see the leela of the Paramatma everywhere. Then, words like "I am doing," "I am eating," etc., will not come out; we will be told that our Paramatma is sitting inside and performing all these actions, and this will become firm.

## Page 381

It is a seed. The word Durga, which is the response to the word Durg, is formed from the Dhātu, and those who destroy all adversities are considered Durga. Only after Mahishasura's defeat does the end of human adversity come. The destruction of adversity is the seed and the fundamental cause of this middle path story. The element of air. When the Prāṇa Shakti manifests in the form of gross elements, then it shines in the form of air. Inhalation and exhalation are the external signs of Prāṇa. Therefore, air is its element. The Yajurveda, in the form of a collection of words, is born from the experience or perception of air. From this, the Yajurveda begins with the Mantra of the air deity. The application of this is to gain the love of Mahalakshmi, i.e., the love for the great Prāṇamayī Ma. First, decide what you want. Then, with the guidance, grace, and support of a Siddha Guru, find the path. Then perform Sadhana on that path. Through this, your goal will be achieved or the vision of the deity. Rishi is the Tattvadarshi Guru, bound is the Sadhana process, deity is the unparalleled, sleepless worshipable Shakti. Application is to enter the path of Sadhana of that Shakti with the courage of her glory and, by dedicating yourself to a specific task, to achieve complete success - the success of the goal. ## Page 386

(10)
They were blessed by the Guru Meghas, who was a repository of knowledge. Their hope has not yet been fulfilled because as wisdom opens, ignorance gradually fades away. The more the aspirant becomes aware of their hidden faults, the more they become capable of focusing on them specifically. Just as a mark left by extremely dirty clothes does not show up until the clothes are cleaned, the more the person cleans themselves, the more the previously unseen marks become completely visible. Similarly, as long as the life remains in ignorance, it cannot see its faults. But when the eye of wisdom opens with the grace of the Guru, it becomes capable of seeing its hidden faults clearly. The soul-oriented, motherly-anchored aspirant initially thought that "the bond of wife, son, etc., in the world is the only way to meet the Paramatma. Without giving up the household life and in some way, this bond cannot be broken." But the Guru, by showing with a finger, indicates that the bond of wife and son is not the only bond; the real bonds are the impressions of the other world. The world is situated within the other world. Why go to a cave in a mountain in some remote place? The world cannot be left behind in any way. The aspirant, when he tries to remove the root of the world by understanding and experiencing the essence, due to the result of the establishment of the truth of the world, the life force, which has fallen asleep due to the grace of the Guru, kills the seed of future actions. The root of the world is removed. But now, two more big and deep roots remain, which do not easily come out. Aspirant! You are saying like your grandfather that as much as you are proud.

## Page 387

(1)
The desire to advance further to sit in the lap of Ma, who is the embodiment of Prana, is there. The cunning and deceitful Ma gradually moves away from there. In some way, she does not leave completely; in some way, all the Pranas are not able to get relief from her (unsteadiness) and are not able to offer themselves at the feet of the Mahapranamayi Ma. Ma also does not always keep the child in her lap with her affectionate embrace. Sometimes she takes the child out and then leaves. Ma does not always meet the life of the Jeeva with her full embodiment of knowledge. Why does this happen? The demon Madhu was killed, the new new hopes were cut off, yet why are we not able to take rest on the Mother Yaksha? When a Sadhak gets stuck in such a feeling and the reason for this does not come to his understanding, then he takes shelter at the feet of Shri Guru, "I am your disciple, please enlighten me!" The picture that the enlightened Guru creates in front of the Sadhak will be explained as the killing of Mahishasura or the separation of Vishnu and Pradhana. At the end of the killing of Madhu, Sage Medhas had said, "Listen, I will tell you again." They knew that whatever was said here would not completely fulfill the hope of Suratha, even if the point of life remained, the hope would not completely disappear. Until the Jeeva is not able to reach the ultimate with full feeling, until the Jeeva and Brahma are not able to experience their absolute non-difference in full, this hope will not be fulfilled. Only by thinking about this, the student is not given the opportunity to ask questions and the intermediary, enlightened Guru goes forward. This is why the first chapter of this chapter is called "Ripiruvaach."

## Page 388

He said, "O Suratha! Even if your future actions are destroyed, the accumulated merit still exists. Those who produce significant results have not yet been realized. Whether you want new things or not, do not continuously have new desires; even if the enchanting form of a new hope does not overwhelm you; but those who had a strong desire long ago, who have nourished countless hopes and aspirations over many births, these, which have taken the form of impressions of significant importance, have become an unshakeable foundation in your mind. Reflect - your accumulated impressions still increase your rights. Without being destroyed, they do not give up your hope of unconditioned happiness. But do not be afraid, dear! Your mother has, in the form of a guru, shed her light on you. I will enter the battlefield with my own hand and dissolve all your impressions. Just sit in my lap or experience my unique play with a single heart. Foolish child! Terrified son! Victory to you for calling me mother, when you only understand me as an eternal refuge, and you are desperately seeking to merge your life with mine in my great energy. Then there is no fear. I will cut all your bonds and merge with your body forever. You will be blessed, O life! Understand this only as a high language of emotion. Truly, you should simply call me with a simple breath, truly experience my eternal refuge from the core of your heart, then you will have nothing to do. I will perfect all your practices and purifications."

## Page 389

You will become a witness or a mere observer. You will sit in my affectionate lap, like a naked child in the joy of happiness and sorrow, and remain in the form of a witness or a mere observer. I will purify you with the imagined purity of your foundation. Your life will be filled with merit.

In this narrative, the accumulated karma and samskaras from previous births, which are evil in nature, will be depicted. The soul gathers a collection of samskaras related to the ultimate goal of the Paramatma through various rituals, practices of yoga and tapasya, or through the cultivation of knowledge and devotion. These are the deities, meaning the mind, intellect, and senses that strive for union with the Paramatma. And the mind, intellect, and senses that are directed towards destruction, or in other words, are characterized by desire, are called the Asuras.

In the seventeenth chapter of the Bhagavad Gita, where Lord Krishna divides the wealth of the gods and the demons, a brief summary of their attributes is necessary. The attributes of the gods, or the divine power, include absolute dedication, charity, control of senses, sacrifice, study of the Vedas, penance, simplicity, non-violence, truth, non-anger, renunciation, peace, modesty, gentleness, shame, patience, strength, forgiveness, virtue, non-enmity, and non-arrogance. And their opposites, such as fear, impurity, and arrogance, are described as the wealth and power of the demons.

In the Chandogya Upanishad, it is also said in the second section of the first chapter, "Devas and Asuras are bound by a machine." Lord Shiva's teachings also emphasize this.

## Page 390

(x)
"Rajo-guna samudbhavaḥ" - Kama and Krodha are born from Rajo-guna. Furthermore, in another Manasa Puja Vidhi, it is said - "Krodha mahip yāt" - meaning, Krodha should be imagined as Mahip and offered as a sacrifice to the Devi. Although here only Krodha is mentioned as Mahip, we should not understand Mahip as only Krodha, but rather as that Rajo-guna from which Krodha arises. The three mysteries of the real Chandi are merely the explanation of three gunas. In the first story, two samskaras, external developments of Sattva-guna, are named Madhu-kaitabh. In this second story, the samskaras that are external developments of Rajo-guna, which are highly conscious, will be described as Asuras. "One shines with many emotions" - this emotion has been removed; but the one we have accepted, that is, the highly conscious samskara that has been pouring into the mind-field since time immemorial, has not been removed. These are the Asura group described in this middle story. Their development comes from Rajo-guna; all desires, passions, and many dama, darpa, abhiman, etc., from the Bhagavad Gita, are all Asuric qualities, mere gross developments of this Rajo-guna. Therefore, Rajo-guna form Mahipasur is their overlord.

Then, the end of this Rajo-guna is the group of female developments, which is the Devi. Purandar is their overlord. He who destroys or disperses Pur is called Purandar. Through this new (6) door, Baladeva, in the form of Pur, destroys it, that is, by dissolving the body-mind, reaching the state beyond the body, beyond the state, beyond the gunas, in the Paramatma state (Mata Ang), that is, the attempt to completely merge. His name is Purandar. He is the overlord of the devas. All deva-shakti,

---

## Page 391

Translation:

In his commentary, he states, "Devas are the embodiment of divinity, their actions are the manifestation of scriptures, and their nature is virtuous. Asuras, on the other hand, are the embodiment of tamoguna, their actions are the manifestation of scriptures, and their nature is demonic. Thus, the constant battle between them is like a game of chess, where both sides are engaged in a perpetual struggle for dominance. This eternal struggle between deities and asuras is ongoing since time immemorial."

The essence of this is: "The battle between deities and asuras has been ongoing in the body of living beings for a long time. The virtuous actions of the senses are the deities, and their opposite, i.e., the actions of desire and anger, are the asuras. Both sides are always engaged in a struggle for dominance. In the body of living beings, both types of actions exist. The virtuous actions of the senses, born from knowledge of the self, and the actions of desire and attachment are the demonic actions of the senses. The intense hatred between these two actions is eternal."

In this way, by knowing the mystery of deities and asuras and their mutual battle from the Bhagavad Gita, Upanishads, and Shankara's commentary, one will gain insight into Devi Mahatmya. It should also be considered that although the mystery of deities and asuras is explained in all branches of the Vedas, there is no reason why two types of beings cannot coexist. The reason for this is that the gross, subtle, and causal reasons are all the same truth. These stories have been elaborately told in the first chapter. When Mahishasura was the king of asuras and Purandara, i.e., Indra, was the king of deities, this battle between deities and asuras began. Mahishasura represents the rajoguna, as mentioned in the Gita.

## Page 392

That is, they follow the same path of fearlessness, goodness, purity, charity, self-control, endurance, etc. All divine qualities are under the command of this Lord. Here, some discussion about the relationship between the three gunas is possible. Let's assume that in the pure consciousness, i.e., the state of absolute, formless Supreme Self, there is some eternal, established ignorance. The form of this ignorance is "I do not know myself." This ignorance is also above knowledge; because "not knowing" is this ignorance, which is essentially some form of knowledge. This eternal, established, unique meeting of knowledge and ignorance is called Maya or Lila or the Prakriti of the universe. Knowledge itself is its form; if one says in the mind, "I do not know," then that thought in the mind should be called Lila. Like a young father, even if he does not forget his knowledge, he enjoys pure joy by playing with his child, just like a child. This is also the same; in fact, the Chitmati Devi, by saying "I do not know myself," becomes enlightened once, that is, to know her own self, she creates a desire in herself, some fleeting emotion, which is called Rajguna. Along with this first vibration, two more vibrations also manifest on both sides. One is light, and the other is state. The special emotion of light that the Supreme Self receives from the first vibration is called the light of knowledge or Satguna, and the Rajguna that can be stabilized in that light is called the state or Tamguna. These are interdependent - one cannot exist without the other, and they also try to complete each other fully. These three gunas are like the outward manifestation of the Dharmic deities, just like that.

## Page 393

13
(17)
They are the end. As previously stated, these three types of Spanda occur above the combined state of knowledge and ignorance. Therefore, just as their development is towards ignorance, it is also towards knowledge. The Spanda that is ignorance, meaning "not knowing oneself with a special feeling," is called Asura; and the Spanda that is knowledge, meaning aiding in the realization of one's true self, is called Devi.
This subject is not entirely simple. For those who are not accustomed to philosophical contemplation, it may be difficult to understand. Therefore, I will briefly and simply re-examine the previously discussed topic. The effort or attempt to know oneself, understanding "I do not know myself," is called Rajguna. The result of that effort, which was to know something, is called Sattvaguna, and the effort to hold onto that knowledge is called Tamguna. This is the Prakarana, Prayuti, and Stiti, or the calm, terrible, and mouthful state, in the Yoga branch. The Gita calls this Prakasha, Pravritti, and Moha.
There is no other way to understand the battle between Devas and Asuras without deeply feeling the nature of these three Gunas. Then it is said that Sattvaguna is the illuminating nature, Rajguna is the active nature, and Tamguna is the nature of attachment. These two are the nature of holding or retaining. They are always resultant and changeable. The solid world is the result of these three Gunas. From Brahma to the solid atom, everything is nothing but the mutual conjunction, separation, and mixture of these three Gunas, and nothing else.
Happiness, sorrow, and other states are also Trigunatmik. Where there is an excess of effort - active nature - and a lack of self-knowledge, there is a turning.

## Page 394

(18)
They are called unhappy in the world because there is more activity of the Rajas guna and less of the Sattva guna. Where the activity of the Sattva guna, that is, the light quality, is more, and the activity of the Rajas guna (restlessness) is less, that is happiness. And when the activity of the Tamas guna becomes strong, the light quality disappears completely, and neither happiness nor sorrow is heard; its name is delusion. The highest manifestation of the Sattva guna is unbroken light, that is, the realization of pure self-knowledge. Only when one reaches this state does the highest manifestation of the Rajas guna occur. This is called dispassion, that is, the absence of the effort to know who I am. Similarly, the highest manifestation of the Tamas guna is the control of delusion, that is, controlling the Rajas guna from arising again. In this way, the two directions of the three gunas have been reached. One direction is creation, maintenance, and destruction - the world of living beings, birth and death, happiness and sorrow, etc. And on the other hand, dispassion and control on unbroken light. They also say in simple terms - one side enjoyment, the other side liberation or freedom. The name of the path of enjoyment of the three gunas is Asura bhava, and the name of the path of liberation is Deva bhava. This battle between gods and demons has been occurring in living beings since time immemorial. The day this battle ends, that day the soul will cross beyond the three gunas; (become gunatita) enjoyment or liberation, bondage or freedom, both these delusions will be far away for all time.

---

## Page 395

The names of these demons will be Andhaka, Uma, Umavi, Mahainu, Vidyala, Durdhara, Durmukha, and Asiloma. They will be explained as Rajoguna, Vikshepa, Avaran, Darpa, Bhaya, Damah, Bhogabhilap, Lobha, Moha, Krodha, Sharirik Bala, Abhimana, Dopadrishti, Akshama Nishthurata, and Dvepa. A detailed discussion of these vices will be held in their respective places.

Now we move towards the actual proposal. The explanation of Mahip and Purandar has already been done. When on one side Mahip and on the other side Purandar, prepared to determine the power of the demons and the gods as their respective lords; then this battle between the gods and the demons is organized. Although every living being always acts out this battle in their body. One desire for enjoyment and the other for liberation, this conflict between the two occurs at the atomic level; however, until the living being does not reach humanity, until they cannot gather self-awareness in the scientific anger, they cannot experience it directly (anubhava). Due to the accumulated good deeds of many births, the infinite mercy of Ma, the divine inspiration of Shri Guru, when the sadhak begins to experience this battle in their heart, then only should it be understood that their birth was blessed, now this battle will soon end. Sadhak! See, on one side your accumulated impressions are running as demonic power to defeat you, they are quenching your life-thirst for meeting your mother. Can you understand - the divine powers of Abhay, Satyashuddhi, Jnanayog, etc., which are full helpers in meeting your mother, which are unique companions in leading you to the temple of Amrit of Shanti, these divine powers are damah, darpa, at this time.
