## Page 396

(20)
The asuras are constantly tormented by pride. When the gods are agitated, come forward, seek refuge, and while lying on the ground, remember 'Ma' with a humble heart. With tearful eyes, ask for the mixture of power from the great power. Experience the truth of your suffering with a simple life. You will see that 'Ma' herself, having incarnated in the form of a warrior, will destroy the demon lineage, bestow joy on the divine lineage, and place you in the immortal, nectar-like form of her motherly symbol. Come, let us, like the lotus child, offer our devotion to 'Ma' and become fearless and actionless. Indeed, the battle between the gods and the asuras lasted for a hundred years - "Purnamada Shatam." The human lifespan is a hundred years - "Shatam ve purushanamayu." The saying that in the Satya Yuga, humans lived for a hundred thousand years is true in another sense. The average human lifespan in all ages has been a hundred years. However, one can increase its measure with the influence of yoga and other powers. In our astrology, the method of counting the duration of a prahana according to the Ashtottari and Vishottari systems also results in a lifespan of a hundred and eight or a hundred and twenty years. Indeed, even after a hundred years, many people continue to live. This does not nullify the ultimate limit. There is no need to argue with the calculation of months, years, etc. The phrase 'Purna Shatam' refers to the complete human life, that is, the complete life of a being. This battle between the gods and the asuras is experienced up to the end of a complete human life. 'After many births, the wise seek refuge in Ma.' (Gita 7.16) After many births, a human becomes wise. After that, I am.

---

## Page 397

One can know 'Ma' (the Divine Mother) if one starts to know 'Ma' little by little. Then the path of this battle is revealed. To experience a full human life encompassing the battle between gods and demons, many births and deaths must be transcended, because our average lifespan is only sixty years. In this, roughly thirty years are spent on daily activities like eating and sleeping. After thirty years of childhood, youth, and the time spent in disease and sorrow, what remains is merely ordinary. Even this time is spent on activities like earning a living and worldly thoughts, so how much time do we have in one life to experience the battle between gods and demons? Our current life is not worthy of being called a real life. Because life is understood as a life that has the power of movement. Let's say that coal, water, and fire are combined daily in the engine of a train to produce steam; but the train cannot move forward from there. The engine, which was built there, stood there for sixty years, consuming only water and steam. Is it not that our one life is also a waste of time? Every day we eat and drink, take care of our body, the purpose is to move forward, to experience the battle between gods and demons, the purpose is to see the Chandiavatar of Ma, the destroyer of demons, but does it happen? "Wherever one is born, there one dies. In this way, why not experience the same life? Even after sixty lives, it is unlikely that a full human life encompassing the battle between gods and demons will be seen. If it is said that we need to see this battle in our heart field, is there any need to dig a well with our own wish and then call someone from the well to our home?"

## Page 398

(22)
Why should one who practices Sadhana experience unrest? This Sadhana is not for those who have not understood, but for those who have realized that they have been disconnected from the kingdom of Ang. Only they experience supreme bliss by seeing this spectacle.
Indeed, the battle between the gods and demons has been going on for many years, and in this Mantra, the word "shat shat varsh" (hundred and hundred years) has been used to mean "for many births." This is also meant to explain that the beginning of this war started from previous births. The practitioner should remember that the ability to understand and discuss the spiritual explanation of Chandi today is not the result of the fruits of one birth's good deeds. It is the accumulation of good deeds from many births that allows one to experience the object of "Ma's grace." And as a result of this experience, the ability to internalize these principles comes gradually. But there is one thing: if anyone continues to experience this battle between the gods and demons within themselves, they are surely blessed. Their life is filled with virtue, and by their presence, virtue is gained. Their blessings are pure, they are the salt of the earth, and by touching their body, the atmosphere becomes pure, and by touching their feet, the earth becomes sacred. O soul, have you ever seen such a battle in your heart's Chaitra? If you cannot see it, then step on the feet of the guru-like 'Ma' and catch her, for she will open the door of your knowledge; there you will see that your heart's battlefield has also become a terrible battlefield. || 1 ||
Moreover, the great army of the gods, led by Mahatripathi, has been defeated. Having defeated all the gods, Indra became the king of the demons. || 2 ||

## Page 399

But
(23)
Translation - In that battle, the army of the devas was defeated by the Mahavidevas, and Mahipā Sur Indra became the conqueror of the gods.
Explanation - The asura army is immensely powerful. Due to the practice of performing countless actions over many lifetimes, a situation arises where our mind remains engaged in the flow of form-sense-desire experiences. We do not want to be at the end or the beginning. We do not want to stay even for a moment in that stable, elevated field. This is the sign of the asura's boundless strength. On the other hand, the divine qualities of the army of the devas are extremely fragile. Because their involution has happened for a short time. The group of good nature is still not satisfied. In this situation, the first thing will be the defeat of divine power by the asuras.
Imagine if an isopod were to coil up in a spring-like manner and be in a state of despair, even then, due to the effect of the power of establishing natural state, it would move towards expansion every moment. But if that isopod is kept under a stone, then that isopod's natural expansion power will remain in a state of cessation even if it moves. The defeat of the gods by the asuras is also something like this.
O Sadhak! You have looked at your chandana and said 'give form to the body,' which is the manifestation of the form of Ma, but you have tried to stop your power of seeing with the breath, but you have come together with the form of the four worlds. Similarly, the ears have heard the eternal sound hidden in the words, Pranava or the special mantra you have uttered.

---

## Page 400

(24)
If you listen to this, then in that moment, the vain words of the world come to you. Your mind, drawn towards the form of Satchidananda, the Supreme Self, becomes engrossed, but in that moment, it starts to revert to its previous nature and begins to entertain various kinds of delusions. Thus, a battle for the kingdom begins. Only those who are truly great and have come close to the nectar can experience this battle. Indeed, in this battle, the gods were defeated first (the internal attraction power was defeated). Although there is always an internal battle, the maternal attraction power always remains. However, as long as the effect of attraction is greater, this battle cannot be experienced. Then, when the attraction power gradually becomes strong due to the grace of Ma, then the battle with the opposing party begins. The first result of this battle is defeat. The reason for defeat has already been mentioned. When the gods approached, then Mahi (the external manifestation of Rajoguna) obtained Indrapada. Mahi had so far only been running the Asuric power, but now the divine power also came under its control. Try to understand this with deep feeling, the ultimate result of Rajoguna is dispassion. After dispassion named Vasikara, the qualities become detached, and then (the best) dispassion arises (Yo. 8. 16 to 2. 56). Collecting the flow of all powers in its entirety is the ultimate manifestation of Rajoguna. This is the end of the translation.

## Page 401

7
(25)
Puranandar, who is the destroyer of the universe and the ruler of Jayadevashakti, completely stops the flow of materialistic activities and as a result, disinterest arises. However, Mahish has obtained the sovereignty of the devas. His nature is characterized by external failures and arrogance, so he will also turn the devas outward. Compassion, forgiveness, generosity, and dispassion, which are the qualities of a deva, will be suppressed by the asuras, but there is no hope for disinterest. Without the destruction of the seeds of actions and without any form of disinterest, it is impossible to exist.

The essence is that on one side is disinterest and on the other side is indulgence. This is the sequence of Purandar and Mahipasur. The form of disinterest is the renunciation of everything, body, mind, and senses, while the form of indulgence is the enjoyment of everything. Purandar desires liberation, while Mahip desires enjoyment. Until the desire for liberation is not fulfilled, Mahip's defeat of Purandar will continue, and Mahip will obtain Indratva. Then the defeated gods, Padmavatiman, were honored by Garuda's voice.

Translation - After being defeated, the gods honored Padmavatiman, who was taken to where Shiva and Vishnu were.
Explanation - Padmavatiman - These are the lords of all emotions, hence they are called Prajapati. Emotion and Prajapati are the same thing. This has been said in the first part and in the Upanishad - "Both are Prajapati," meaning that both the devas and asuras are born from Prajapati. Deva-shakti and asura-shakti are both emotions of the mind.

---

## Page 402

(26)
That part of the mind where the Asuras' influence increases does not become Padmavati even when it becomes Prajapati. The region below the Nabhi Kamal is the domain of the Asuras, and above it is the domain of the Devas. The origin of Brahma is from the Nabhi Kamal, and the part of the mind that has become Paramatma-oriented, where the desire for true maternal gain has awakened, is Padmavati. By advancing this, the Deity, meaning the consciousness of the senses established in the Chaitanya-Vrnd, approaches Vishnu and Shiva. Vishnu's Prana-Shakti and Shiva's Jnana-Shakti. Vishnu's place is in the Hriday Kamal or Anahat, and Shiva's place is in the Lalat or Ajna Chakra. Therefore, the defeated Deity, Padmavati, approaches Shiva and Vishnu. The meaning of this is that the Paramatma-oriented sensory power, wounded by Asuric emotions, approaches Prana and Jnana. The Sadhak can experience that with full mind, neither can one desire Ma nor can one enjoy the world. On one side of the mind, as if a desire for a show of violence arises, or a curiosity to hear about maternal greatness, on the other side, the play of desires for son, etc., continues. One sees the king of the Devas, Purandar, performing his duties on one side of the mind, and on the other side, the king of the Asuras, Mahip, performing his role of causing pain. As a result of causing this pain, the first Deval powers are defeated. The mind cannot gain full strength without being maternal-oriented with full emotion, therefore, the mind becomes helpless and seeks their shelter. Before this, the Prana that went to Yama and tied the knot of Madhukaitabh, who, as a scientific guru, gradually removes ignorance by shining in the form of knowledge.
